skeleton now shimmers via a new `--animate-shimmer` keyframe; data-table's header is now sticky+opaque with refined row hover) — plus three genuinely new shared primitives: `tooltip.tsx` (new dep `@radix-ui/react-tooltip`, one `TooltipProvider` mounted app-wide in `app-providers.tsx`), `tabs.tsx` (new dep `@radix-ui/react-tabs`, for in-page client-state tab switching), `pagination.tsx` (windowed page-number + prev/next, replacing every module's hand-rolled "Page X of Y" footer), and `route-tab-nav.tsx` (extracts the Link-based URL-driven tab-nav pattern shared by IAM/Gym Settings/Billing's secondary nav bars into one component). **Layout chrome**: sidebar gained purely-presentational section grouping (`NavItem.section`, a new optional field on `nav-config.ts` — Overview/People/Programs/Finance/Insights/Communication/Administration — filtering/permission/featureFlag logic untouched) with a left accent-bar active-state indicator; header bumped to h-16 with a shadow-xs + stronger backdrop-blur; notification bell gained an animated ping ring. **Auth** (`app/(auth)/layout.tsx`, now reading `useTenant()` client-side instead of a second server-side `resolveTenant()` fetch, to avoid duplicating the root layout's network call): reworked into a responsive split-screen — a `.gradient-brand` hero panel (tenant logo in a glass badge, a `.glass-panel` feature-highlights card) on `lg+`, collapsing to the original single centered column below `lg`; every one of the 16 `(auth)` routes plus the separate `/register` 7-step onboarding wizard (its own layout, restyled at the Card/step level, not restructured — the sessionStorage state machine and the `success-step.tsx` fire-on-mount-via-`useRef`-guard pattern are untouched) inherited the refresh through shared components (`AuthHeader`/`FormAlert`/`PasswordInput`/`StatusScreen`/`PasswordStrengthMeter`/`OtpInput`) already restyled once. **Dashboard**: `charts-grid.tsx` rebuilt as the canonical Recharts pattern every other chart in the app now follows — CSS-var-themed axis ticks/tooltip (fixes a real pre-existing dark-mode bug: default Recharts axis text is hardcoded gray, invisible against a dark surface) and gradient `<defs>` area fills on `var(--chart-1)`. **Every remaining module** (IAM/Staff/Branches, Members/Memberships/Attendance, Workout/Diet plans, Finance/Billing, Reports/Analytics/Notifications/Announcements, Gym Settings/Profile/Settings/Support) got the same sweep applied via six parallel-scoped passes: hand-rolled pagination → `Pagination`, ad-hoc `rounded-md`/`shadow-sm` inputs/selects → the refreshed `rounded-lg`/`shadow-xs` scale, hardcoded-hex status badges → the semantic `success`/`warning`/`destructive` Badge variants (dark-mode-correct by construction), required-field labels → `Label required`, and every multi-series chart (Finance's 2-line Revenue Trends, Analytics' 7 charts including the Branch Comparison pie cycling `--chart-1..8`) re-themed onto the categorical token order with a `Legend` added wherever 2+ series are shown. Two new Next 15 route-boundary files added (previously missing entirely): `(portal)/loading.tsx`/`(auth)/loading.tsx` (the already-built-but-unused `PageLoader`) and `(portal)/not-found.tsx` (`EmptyState`). **Verified**: `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint --max-warnings=0` clean across the whole app (checked both per-module during the parallel sweep and once more fully merged); a 35-route compile-sweep via curl against the live dev server (zero 500s/error markers); a Playwright pass (login flow, dashboard/members/branches/analytics, light+dark, desktop 1440px+mobile 390px, mobile sidebar drawer) with zero console errors beyond a pre-existing (not introduced by this prompt) missing `favicon.ico`. `next build` was deliberately **not** re-run this pass — a second, unrelated live session was concurrently using the same `next dev` server for its own work (Prompt 26.5) at the time, and `next build` while `next dev` holds the port is a documented corrupt-`.next`/kill-the-dev-server hazard (see Prompt 13/14's own gotcha note) — relying on the clean typecheck+lint+live-route-sweep instead, same call the concurrent Prompt 26.5 session made for its own smaller change. **Recommended before shipping**: run `pnpm run build` once the dev server is free.

28 — **Super Admin: Tenant 360 View (platform billing)**: user-requested extension of the Tenant Detail page (`apps/super-admin/.../tenants/[tenantId]/page.tsx`), previously a bare Subscription snapshot + always-empty Usage card + Users + audit log, with no payment/invoice history and no way to collect an overdue payment. This is entirely **platform billing** (FitCloud charging the TENANT for its own subscription — `Subscription`/`Payment`/`Invoice`, Prompt 8) — deliberately separate from the Finance module's Member Payments (Prompt 25, the gym charging its own members); no shared code, reuses only the generic, already-real `razorpay-gateway.service.ts` wrapper (its file comment updated to note it's now dual-purposed). New `modules/admin-tenants/services/admin-tenant-billing.service.ts` owns: per-tenant payments/invoices list (small `tenantId` filter added to `AdminPaymentRepository`, previously status-only), a real Razorpay Payment Link flow (`createPaymentLink`/`verifyPaymentStatus`/`resendNotification`) that finds-or-auto-generates the tenant's outstanding `OPEN` invoice (renewal-lifecycle jobs can leave a subscription `PAST_DUE`/`GRACE`/`SUSPENDED` with no saved payment method and thus no invoice), and is unique-constraint-safe against `Payment.invoiceId`'s real `@unique` DB constraint (reuses/refreshes an existing row instead of a second `create()`, idempotent on repeat clicks — verified live). On a confirmed `paid` link, `reactivateAfterPayment()` mirrors `SubscriptionService.checkout()`'s exact success tail (roll `currentPeriodEnd`, `Subscription.status='ACTIVE'`, clear grace/suspended/cancelled fields, `Tenant.status`/`subscriptionExpiresAt`/`suspendedAt` mirror since that's what `tenantMiddleware` fast-path-gates on, `SubscriptionHistory` action `RENEWED`) and emits the same `billing.subscription_activated` event `checkout()` does, so the tenant's existing "activated"+"invoice" emails fire with zero new email code. Also added `InvoiceService#renderPdf` (platform `Invoice` was HTML-only before this — `pdfkit` already a dependency, method mirrors `MemberInvoiceService.renderPdf`'s layout) and an admin-triggered "email invoice/receipt" action (reuses the existing `invoiceEmail` template). New admin permission `payments:manage` (granted to `SUPER_ADMIN`/`FINANCE_ADMIN`) gates all 5 write actions; the pre-existing `tenants:read` gates the 3 read-only ones. New routes all under `/admin/tenants/:tenantId/...`: `GET payments`, `GET invoices`, `POST payment-link`, `POST payments/:id/verify`, `POST payments/:id/resend-notification`, `GET invoices/:id/download`, `POST invoices/:id/email`. Frontend: new `features/billing/` (mutations) + `features/tenants` extended (richer `subscriptions[]` type incl. trial/grace/cancel/coupon fields — repository's `findById` include gained `coupon: true`; new `payments`/`invoices` service methods+hooks) — Tenant Detail page redesigned with a richer Subscription card, an "Outstanding balance" panel (Send Payment Link → Copy/Check-status/Resend, gated on `useHasPermission('payments:manage')`) inline in that card, and new Payments/Invoices `DataTable`+`Pagination` sections reusing the exact column/status-color convention from the standalone `/payments` admin page. Deliberately did **not** introduce a `LoadingButton` component — this app's existing convention (confirmed by reading the page before editing) is a plain `Button` + `disabled={mutation.isPending}`, matched instead of adding a new component. Verified: typecheck/eslint (0 warnings, api + super-admin), full API vitest suite 237/237 green, and live end-to-end against the running dev stack using real Razorpay test-mode credentials (already in `.env`) against a real tenant (`prompt10test`): created a genuine Payment Link (`https://rzp.io/...`), confirmed the second immediate call returned the *identical* link (idempotent, no unique-constraint 500), confirmed `verify` correctly returns `PENDING` for an unpaid link, confirmed `resend-notification` and `email invoice` both actually enqueue (invoice email confirmed arriving in Mailpit), confirmed the downloaded PDF is a real binary (`file` reported "PDF document, version 1.3"), and a full Playwright pass on the redesigned page (zero console errors) including clicking "Send payment link" → the Copy/Check-status panel appearing with the real short URL → "Check status" correctly returning PENDING. The test Payment/Invoice created during verification were marked FAILED/VOID afterward via direct SQL, same cleanup precedent as Prompt 25. **Not done this pass**: an actual Razorpay-hosted-checkout click-through to reach a real `SUCCEEDED` state (would require automating a third-party payment page) — same limitation Prompt 25 itself already accepted; the `reactivateAfterPayment` success path is exercised for real the first time an admin collects an actual overdue payment this way.

28.5 — **Tenant 360 View: Gym Profile + Branches, plus two live-found bugs (same-session follow-up)**: user feedback after P28 landed — the page still didn't show the gym's own profile (`TenantProfile`: legal name/GST/contact/address/description) or its branches at all, which the admin explicitly wanted as part of "360 view." `admin-tenant.repository.ts#findById`'s include gained `profile: true` and `branches: { where: { deletedAt: null }, orderBy: [{isDefault:'desc'},{name:'asc'}] }` (same ordering convention as `BranchRepository.list()`) — no new endpoint needed, both were already 1:1/1:many off `Tenant` and simply never selected. Frontend: `TenantDetail` type widened to match; new Gym Profile card (identity/contact/address/GST via lucide icons) and a Branches `DataTable` (code/location/contact/capacity/default+active badges) added to the page; the old plain-text Usage list replaced with `StatisticCard` tiles (red tone at 100% utilization) plus a horizontal recharts `BarChart` ("Resource usage") — reusing the exact chart-styling conventions already established on the admin Dashboard/Revenue pages (`ResponsiveContainer`+`var(--chart-*)` fill), and `Badge` (this app's real component, not an inline-span hack) for every status pill. **Two real bugs found and fixed during the live Playwright pass this same follow-up**: (1) a hydration error — `<StatusBadge>` (a `Badge`, which renders a `<div>`) was nested inside a `<p>` in the header, invalid HTML; same bug class this codebase has hit and fixed at least three times before (Prompt 16/21's own notes) — fixed by changing that wrapper to a `<div>`. (2) The "Send payment link" button was only rendered when an `OPEN` invoice happened to already exist, which hid it entirely for the common case (a tenant with no outstanding invoice yet) even though the backend already auto-generates one on demand — reported directly by the user testing it live ("nothing is showing", "from where can send the payment link"). Fixed by gating the button on `subscription && canManageBilling` alone; the "Outstanding: $X" line is now just informational when an invoice happens to exist, with a neutral explanatory line when it doesn't. Verified: typecheck/eslint (0 warnings) both apps, and a live Playwright pass confirming zero console errors (the hydration warning specifically) and the button now visible/clickable with no pre-existing invoice.

29 — **Plans page: clickable cards + Plan Detail (subscribers, Edit, Upgrade/Downgrade/Assign)**: user-requested redesign — `/plans` was a bare `DataTable`, with no way to see who's on a plan or move a tenant between plans from the admin side. `/plans` rebuilt as a clickable pricing-card grid (price/trial/subscriber-count/description, Edit+Delete still inline via `stopPropagation`); new `/plans/[planId]` detail page (full package info incl. every limit + the feature checklist with check/x icons, an "At a glance" card, and a `DataTable` of every tenant currently on that plan). The Create/Edit form (~24 fields) was extracted from the old inline dialog into a shared `features/plans/components/plan-form-dialog.tsx` so it isn't duplicated between the list page and the detail page's "Edit plan" button.

New backend: `GET /admin/plans/:planId/subscribers` (`AdminPlanRepository.subscribers` — one `Subscription` row per tenant on that plan; upgrade/downgrade mutates the existing row's `planId` rather than inserting a new one, per `SubscriptionService.checkout()`'s own pattern, so this is genuinely "who's on this plan right now," not history). The real feature — **Upgrade/Downgrade/Assign a fresh plan, per subscriber row, both manually and via a real Razorpay payment link** — lives in `AdminTenantBillingService` (same file P28 built): new `POST /admin/tenants/:tenantId/subscription/change-plan` (`{planId, mode: 'manual'|'payment_link'}`, perm `payments:manage`) dispatches to:
- **`changePlanManually`** — an admin-asserted switch with no online payment (e.g. paid by bank transfer, or a support comp) — generates an invoice for the target plan's price and immediately marks it PAID (no sandboxed-gateway charge involved, there's nothing real to call), then applies the switch.
- **`createPaymentLink` extended with an optional `planId`** — when present, always generates a FRESH invoice for the target plan's price (deliberately never reuses an existing open invoice, which would be for the old plan's amount) and tags the `Payment.metadata` with `{targetPlanId}`; `verifyPaymentStatus`'s paid-branch now reads that metadata and switches the plan on confirmation instead of just renewing the current one.

Both paths funnel through one new private `applyPlanChange(planId, invoice, note)` — mirrors `checkout()`'s exact success tail (period roll, `Subscription.status`, `Tenant` mirror, `SubscriptionHistory`, `billing.subscription_activated` event) and is generalized to also handle a tenant with **no subscription yet** ("assign a fresh plan," the same `current ? update : create` branch `checkout()` uses), computing `UPGRADED`/`DOWNGRADED`/`RENEWED` via plan `sortOrder` comparison. A plain renewal (no plan change requested) is now just `applyPlanChange` targeting the tenant's own current plan — collapsed the old P28 `reactivateAfterPayment` renewal-only logic into this same path rather than keeping two near-duplicate state-transition blocks.

Frontend: `features/plans` gained `usePlanById`/`usePlanSubscribers` hooks + `PlanSubscriber` type; `features/billing` gained `useChangePlan(tenantId)`. The subscriber-row "Change plan" dialog (`ChangePlanDialog`, inline in the detail page) excludes the tenant's current plan from the target dropdown and offers "Assign manually" / "Send payment link" side by side — the latter reuses the same Copy-link panel pattern from the Tenant Detail page (P28) rather than inventing a second one.

Verified: typecheck/eslint (0 warnings) + build + vitest (237/237) on the API, typecheck/eslint (0 warnings) on super-admin, and live end-to-end against the running dev stack with real Razorpay test-mode credentials: `changePlanManually` confirmed live (subscription's `planId` updated, a new PAID invoice generated at the new plan's price, `admin.tenant_plan_changed_manual` recorded on the **admin** audit log — not the tenant's own, a real distinction worth remembering when checking either), the real "subscription changed" + invoice emails arrived in Mailpit; `payment_link` mode confirmed to leave the subscription untouched until paid (`Payment.metadata.targetPlanId` correctly set, verified as still `PENDING`); a full Playwright pass on both new pages (card grid → click → detail page → open Change Plan dialog → pick a plan → generate a real `https://rzp.io/...` link) with zero console errors. One real display bug found and fixed during this same pass: the "At a glance" card's subscriber count read `plan._count.subscriptions`, which only the LIST endpoint populates — the detail endpoint (`GET /admin/plans/:planId`) never has, so it silently showed 0; switched to the already-fetched `subscribers.data.total` instead. Test plan changes made during verification were reverted (tenant restored to its original plan) and the generated test invoices/payments voided/failed via direct SQL, same cleanup precedent as P28.

31 — **Staff: manager-side manual activation**: found via the `kaushalgym` seed data — a `PENDING_VERIFICATION` staff member's row-action menu had genuinely no way to activate them at all (only Suspend/Deactivate, which don't apply to an account that's never logged in) unless the staff member personally clicked their activation email. New `POST /staff/{staffId}/manual-activate` (`StaffService#manualActivate`, perm `staff:activate`) is a real activation — not the pre-existing status-only `activate()` (that's for un-suspending an *already-activated* account and would otherwise leave a `PENDING_VERIFICATION` user `ACTIVE` with no usable password). Reuses the exact same two-call sequence the self-service `acceptActivation` flow already uses (`authUserRepository.updatePasswordHash` + `status: 'ACTIVE'`/`emailVerifiedAt`), generating a real password and returning it once, directly, in the response (`{email, temporaryPassword}`) — this module has no secure side-channel to deliver it otherwise, same precedent as the Super Admin portal's `AdminRoleService#createAdmin`. Frontend: `/staff`'s row menu for `PENDING_VERIFICATION` now shows "Manually activate" (opens a reveal dialog — email + password + Copy button, closes on "Done", password never shown again) and "Resend activation email" (wires up the pre-existing `resendActivation` endpoint, which had a working backend but no frontend button at all until now) instead of Suspend/Deactivate. Verified live end-to-end against `kaushalgym`'s real seed data: activated a `PENDING_VERIFICATION` staff member, confirmed the row flips to `ACTIVE` after reload, and confirmed the revealed temporary password genuinely logs in via `POST /auth/login` — zero console errors.

32 — **Support ticket module (tenant-facing create/view + admin reply)**: `support_tickets` (the plan feature flag) and `SupportTicket`/`SupportTicketNote` (Prisma models, Prompt 9's Super Admin portal) already existed, but there was no way for a tenant to actually raise a ticket — `/support` was a hardcoded `EmptyState` placeholder, no tenant-facing endpoint existed anywhere. New `modules/support/` (mirrors `modules/staff/` layout): `TicketRepository`/`TicketService`/`TicketController`/validators, mounted at `/support/tickets` (`GET /`, `GET /:ticketId`, `POST /`). `SupportTicket` has **no RLS policy** (confirmed via migration grep, same as the admin-support module already knew) — every query explicitly filters `tenantId` in application code as the real isolation boundary. New permissions `support:view`/`support:create`, granted to every tenant role (OWNER via `*`, MANAGER/TRAINER/RECEPTIONIST/MEMBER explicitly) since any staff member or member should be able to raise an issue. `TicketRepository#findById` deliberately filters `notes: {where: {isInternal: false}}` — a tenant only ever sees notes an admin explicitly marked customer-visible, never internal triage chatter. Frontend: new `features/support/` (types/service/hooks, mirrors `features/staff/`), `/support` page rewritten — `NewTicketDialog` (subject/priority/description form), a real `DataTable`+`Pagination` ticket list gated on `support:view`/`support:create` and the existing `support_tickets` plan-feature flag (unchanged gate, `hasTickets`), `TicketDetailDialog` showing description + any customer-visible replies.

Live verification surfaced a real product gap the user caught by testing the loop end-to-end: the **pre-existing** Super Admin ticket detail page (`apps/super-admin/.../support/[ticketId]/page.tsx`) hardcoded every admin note as `isInternal: true` with no way to actually reply to the tenant — the backend (`addTicketNoteSchema`) always supported a per-note `isInternal` flag, the admin UI just never exposed it. Fixed in the same pass: the page now has two separate sections — "Reply to tenant" (`isInternal: false`, visible to the tenant) and "Internal notes" (`isInternal: true`, staff-only), no backend/service/hook changes needed since `isInternal` was already threaded end-to-end, only the page UI was missing the second input. Verified live: admin sends a public reply + an internal note on the same ticket → tenant's ticket detail dialog shows the reply under "Replies" and correctly omits the internal note — zero console errors on both sides.

Also surfaced (not a bug in this feature, pre-existing behavior): the permission cache (`perm:{userId}:{permVer}`, 30 min TTL) is keyed by `permVer`, which only bumps on a user-specific role/grant change — re-running `prisma db seed` to add a permission to an existing *role's* catalog (as this prompt did for `support:view`/`support:create`) does **not** bump already-logged-in users' `permVer`, so they keep serving a stale cached permission set for up to 30 minutes after a seed re-run. Worked around during verification by deleting the specific stale Redis key; no code change made (out of scope for this prompt, but worth knowing for any future "add a permission to seed.ts and re-seed" prompt — a currently-logged-in test session may need either a wait or a manual cache-key delete to see it immediately).

Verified: typecheck/eslint (0 warnings)/build/vitest (237/237) on the API, typecheck/eslint (0 warnings) on both tenant-web and super-admin, `prisma db seed` re-run to apply the new permissions, and a full live Playwright pass covering: tenant creates a ticket via the UI → appears in tenant's own list → appears on Super Admin's Support Tickets list correctly attributed to the tenant/requester → admin sends a public reply + an internal note → tenant sees only the public reply. Test tickets created during verification were deleted via direct SQL afterward, except one (`"Cannot check in members at Downtown branch"`, HIGH priority, OPEN) deliberately left on the `kaushalgym` seed tenant as a realistic example ticket.

33 — **Global loading indicator + DB hot-path indexing (perf/UX pass)**: user reported clicking any nav item or button gave no visible feedback for a couple of seconds while the backend request was still in flight.

**Loading indicator**: tenant-web already had a full "Prompt 23" loading infrastructure (`ui-slice.ts`'s `pendingRequests`/`navigationPending`, `NavigationProgressProvider` patching `history.pushState`/`replaceState`, `TopProgressBar`) — it just wasn't visible enough (a 2px top bar). **Ported the entire loading-indicator stack to `apps/super-admin` for the first time** too — it had none of it.

Three real bugs found via live Playwright testing before shipping, none caught by typecheck/eslint (exactly why CLAUDE.md requires live verification):
1. **An early version of the centered overlay blocked all clicks** (`FullScreenLoader`'s opaque backdrop, `z-200`) — confirmed live under throttled network: a background dashboard fetch the user never triggered was enough to trap every subsequent click behind the overlay, including on a completely different nav item. Fixed by making it `pointer-events-none` (visual-only, can never trap the UI) and swapping the full-viewport backdrop for a small non-blocking pill.
2. **`navigationPending` could get stuck `true` forever** on a fast/prefetched navigation. Root cause: `notifyStart()`'s `dispatch(navigationStarted())` is deliberately deferred via `queueMicrotask` (dispatching synchronously reproduces a real "useInsertionEffect must not schedule updates" React error — confirmed by temporarily removing the defer and observing it). For an already-prefetched route, Next.js can resolve the whole navigation before that deferred microtask even runs, so the "finished" effect fires first and the flag flips back to `true` right after with nothing left to clear it (confirmed via a temporary Redux-state-polling script: stuck `true` for 5.8+ seconds after a nav click that itself completed in 64ms). Fixed with two synchronous refs: `pendingGenRef` (generation counter — only the latest `notifyStart` call may dispatch "started") and `pathKeyRef` (kept fresh every render — the deferred microtask checks whether its route already resolved by the time it runs).
3. **No loader at all for the exact scenario the user reported**, caught via a user-provided screenshot (DevTools Network tab): clicking "Staff" showed nothing — no route change, no loader — while the route's `_rsc` fetch sat at "Pending". Root cause: `pushState` isn't called until the App Router's RSC fetch resolves, and in `next dev` the *first* visit to a route triggers on-demand server compilation that can take several real seconds — during that whole window nothing client-side had fired yet, so a `pushState`-only trigger has no chance to show anything. Fixed by adding a capture-phase `document` `click` listener as the *primary* trigger (fires the instant a same-tab internal link is clicked, filtering out modifier-key clicks/new-tab/download/external/hash-only), with `pushState`-patching kept as a secondary trigger for programmatic `router.push()` calls. The listener lives in its own effect, deliberately *not* gated by the `historyPatched` module guard (unlike the history-patching itself, which must only happen once) — Strict Mode's dev-only mount→cleanup→remount dance would otherwise remove the listener in the cleanup pass and skip re-adding it. Verified by stalling the target route's `_rsc` fetch 2.5–3s in Playwright: pill now appears at ~t+300-400ms regardless.

**Consolidated to a single loader on user request** (`TopProgressBar` was still showing simultaneously with the centered pill, plus a third, unrelated spinner from the App Router's `loading.tsx` Suspense boundary — three different loaders that could overlap). `TopProgressBar` deleted from both apps; `(portal)/loading.tsx` and `(auth)/loading.tsx` now render `null` (the file must still exist so the App Router treats navigation as non-blocking, it just no longer shows a competing spinner — `GlobalLoader` is the only visible indicator). The remaining component was renamed/relocated for clarity as the intended single edit point: `components/loading/global-loader.tsx` exporting `GlobalLoader` (the container — owns WHEN it shows) + `components/loading/gym-loader.tsx` exporting `GymLoader` (pure presentational — a spring-animated progress ring + a barbell that racks up plates as progress climbs, cycling tip text; takes `progress`/`label` props, no store reads — this is the file to edit to change what the loader *looks like*). Mounted once in `AppProviders` — nothing to import per-page, every route and every button that goes through the shared `apiClient` gets it automatically. No real request reports byte-level progress, so `GlobalLoader` drives `GymLoader`'s `progress` prop with a cosmetic asymptotic climb (fast start, crawls toward a 92% cap while genuinely still pending, snaps to 100% the instant loading ends — same trick NProgress-style bars use).

**A fourth bug, found after the above shipped** (user: "loader hits 100% and closes, but the page hasn't changed yet"): a real gap between two chained loading signals — `navigationPending` clearing (the route/pathname has committed) and the new page's own first data fetch actually registering in `pendingRequests` (the route's JS chunk still has to load + the component still has to mount + its effect still has to fire the query) — confirmed live via network-log timestamps to run 500-1100ms in dev mode, i.e. NOT a fixed/small number. During that gap `isLoading` reads `false`, so the shared `useDelayedLoading` hook (`src/hooks/use-delayed-loading.ts`, ported identically to both apps) started its hide countdown prematurely. Fixed with a new **off-debounce**: once `isLoading` flips false, the hook now waits `offDelayMs` (hook default 200ms) before committing to hide — if `isLoading` flips back to `true` within that window (the dependency change re-runs the effect, clearing the pending hide timer via its cleanup), the hide is cancelled outright and the loader just stays visible with no flicker. `GlobalLoader` overrides this to `900ms` specifically (wider than the hook's own default) since the measured real-world gap exceeded 200ms. Verified live: screenshots at 300ms/700ms/1300ms/1900ms/2500ms through a click with the data request itself delayed 1.5s — loader stays up (with the page's own skeleton correctly visible underneath) through the ENTIRE wait, reaches "100% ready" only once the real rows have already rendered behind it, then fades — zero gap between loader-gone and content-present.

**A fifth bug, same symptom but a different and more serious root cause** (user report + DevTools screenshot: clicked "Billing," loader hit 100% and closed, but the page didn't actually change until the route's `_rsc` fetch — visibly still "Pending" in the Network tab — finished 10.17 REAL seconds later). Root cause: `NavigationProgressProvider`'s `SAFETY_TIMEOUT_MS` fallback (meant only as a last-resort backstop for a navigation that never resolves at all — a swallowed error, a same-route soft nav) was set to 4000ms, and was firing on a **legitimately slow but genuinely working** navigation — a heavier route (Billing, with its tabs/plan data) compiling for the first time in `next dev`. Confirmed the 10s wasn't a real backend/DB problem: direct timing on `/billing` showed 1.64s → 0.38s → 0.33s across three hits — the classic signature of Next.js dev-mode's one-time, per-route, on-demand compile (nothing to do with the database — the real API calls visible in the same screenshot, `plans`/`subscription`, both returned in 50-66ms; this compile step doesn't exist at all in a production build, and even in dev, only fires once per route per server lifetime). Since the underlying `_rsc` fetch isn't tracked by `pendingRequests` at all (it's a Next.js-internal fetch, not an `apiClient` call), once the 4s safety timeout force-cleared `navigationPending`, NOTHING was left to keep `isLoading` true for the remaining ~6 seconds — the loader fell silent while the OLD page just sat there, exactly the failure mode this component exists to prevent. Fixed by widening `SAFETY_TIMEOUT_MS` to `20_000` in both apps — a too-long safety timeout costs nothing in the common case (it essentially never fires), while a too-short one actively lies about completion. Verified live: `page.route()`-stalled a route's `_rsc` fetch for 7 real seconds (past the old 4s cutoff, comfortably under the new 20s one) and confirmed the loader stays visible the entire time, only clearing once the route genuinely resolves.

**A sixth issue, different class entirely** (user-reported + screenshot: uploading a branding image covered the whole screen with the global loader — "look odd," and the user couldn't see/click the OTHER "Change image" buttons while one upload was in flight). This wasn't a bug in the loader's timing — every upload correctly goes through the shared `apiClient`, so `pendingRequests` correctly ticks up, and the global loader correctly shows... it's just the wrong loader for this case. An in-page action with its own local feedback (a file already selected, a thumbnail already visible) doesn't need — and is actively worse off with — a full-screen takeover. Fixed by adding a `silent?: boolean` axios config flag (module-augmented onto `AxiosRequestConfig`, both apps' `api-client.ts`) that opts a specific request OUT of `pendingRequests` entirely; the interceptor checks `config.silent`/`response.config.silent` before dispatching `requestStarted`/`requestFinished`. Applied to every immediate-upload-on-select call site found by grepping for `fileToDataUrl` usage app-wide: the 3 branding upload endpoints (logo/favicon/generic asset) and the member-document upload endpoint — NOT to avatar upload, expense receipts, or exercise images, which only stage a data-URL into local FORM state and upload later as part of a normal Save (already correctly covered by the global loader, no double-loader problem there). `ImageUploadField` (`features/gym-settings/components/image-upload-field.tsx`, shared by all 5 branding image slots) was rebuilt to own its own upload lifecycle end-to-end: `onUpload` is now `(dataUrl, onProgress) => Promise<void>` instead of fire-and-forget, so the field's local `uploading` state spans the full round trip (not just the client-side canvas-downscale step it covered before), and a REAL progress bar (axios `onUploadProgress`, not simulated) renders directly on the thumbnail plus "Uploading… N%" on the button. Verified live: uploading a real image with the network throttled — confirmed zero global-loader elements appear at any point, the local progress bar/percentage genuinely animates via the real upload's byte progress, and every other "Change image" button on the page stays fully visible (not dimmed/covered) the entire time.

**A seventh issue, unrelated to loaders** (user question: "of the 5 branding images, only the logo shows anywhere — where do the other 4 actually render?"). Investigated via a research agent rather than assumption, and confirmed genuine gaps, not user error:
- `faviconUrl` — WAS in the tenant-resolve payload but never actually set as the browser tab icon anywhere. Fixed: `app/layout.tsx`'s `generateMetadata()` now returns `icons: { icon: tenant.branding.faviconUrl }` when set (falls back to the static `favicon.ico` otherwise).
- `loginBackgroundUrl` / `dashboardBannerUrl` — NOT EVEN IN the tenant-resolve payload at all: `ResolvedTenantRecord`/`ResolvedTenant`'s `branding` type (`modules/tenants/interfaces/tenant.interface.ts`) only declared 6 of the `TenantBranding` table's 9 columns, and `toResolvedTenant()` (`tenant.service.ts`) only mapped those 6 — even though Prisma's `RESOLVE_INCLUDE.branding: true` was already fetching the full row. Pure type/mapping gap, no query change needed. Fixed by widening both interfaces (repository's local `TenantWithRelations.branding` type too) and adding the 2 missing field mappings.
  - `loginBackgroundUrl` renders on the ACTUAL `/login` page — which turned out to be a separate top-level route (`app/login/page.tsx`) using its own fully custom `LoginForm`/`LoginHero` (a deliberate, polished, theme-independent neon "energetic gym" design, NOT the shared `(auth)/layout.tsx` split-screen the docs previously implied covers login — confirmed via that file's own comment: `LoginForm` is deliberately NOT nested in `(auth)/layout.tsx`, "same pattern `/register` already uses"). `LoginHero` gained an optional `backgroundImageUrl` prop — when set, it replaces the fixed `#07070b` base layer (all the decorative glow/streaks/diamonds/chips/dots/vignette still render on top unchanged) with the tenant's photo plus an added `bg-black/45` scrim for legibility against an unpredictable image. `(auth)/layout.tsx`'s own hero panel ALSO got the same treatment (for the OTHER auth-shell pages — forgot-password, reset-password, etc. — that do use the shared layout), even though it isn't what `/login` itself uses.
  - `dashboardBannerUrl` renders as the dashboard header's background image (`app/(portal)/dashboard/page.tsx`), replacing the hardcoded CSS gradient when set, with a matching dark scrim + white text for legibility.
- `emailLogoUrl` — worse than a rendering gap: **orphaned even on the backend**, contrary to its own UI description ("Used in transactional emails..."). All 3 places that build `EmailBranding` for outgoing mail (`auth-email.listeners.ts`, `staff-email.listeners.ts`, `invitation-email.listeners.ts`) hardcoded `logoUrl: tenant.branding.logoUrl` — the *main* logo, not the dedicated email one — and `ResolvedTenant` didn't even have an `emailLogoUrl` field for them to reach for. Fixed by adding the field (same interface/mapping fix as above) and changing all 3 call sites to `tenant.branding.emailLogoUrl ?? tenant.branding.logoUrl`. Verified live end-to-end: triggered a real invitation email, fetched it from Mailpit, confirmed the `<img>` src length in the sent HTML (6583 chars) matches `email_logo_url` in the DB exactly, not `logo_url` (1035 chars) — the fallback genuinely fires, not just compiles.

Verified live (all 3): screenshots of the real `/login` page (tenant's uploaded photo visible through the neon overlay) and `/dashboard` (uploaded banner image with "KAUSHAL FITNESS STUDIO" text visible, heading legible on top), favicon `<link rel="icon">` href confirmed pointing at the tenant's data-URL, and the Mailpit email-logo check above. Full typecheck/eslint/build/vitest (237/237) on the API, typecheck/eslint on tenant-web, zero console errors, full nav-loader regression suite still green.

**An eighth issue, found immediately after** (user-screenshotted: right after "Image uploaded" toasted — meaning the upload's OWN silent+local-progress fix from issue #6 had already done its job — the global loader popped back up anyway). Root cause: the branding page's `handleUpload` calls `router.refresh()` after every successful upload (to refresh the portal chrome — sidebar logo, etc. — reading updated branding). `router.refresh()` is a same-URL "soft" navigation (re-fetches the current route's RSC payload, doesn't change the pathname), but `NavigationProgressProvider`'s patched `pushState`/`replaceState` was treating EVERY call as "a navigation started," unconditionally — and since the pathname never actually changes for a same-URL call, the `[pathname, searchParams]`-keyed clearing effect never fires for it, leaving the loader up until the `SAFETY_TIMEOUT_MS` backstop (now 20s, per the fifth issue above — worse than before that widening, not better). Confirmed live with a polling script watching specifically the 4 seconds after the success toast: the global loader was visible continuously from ~500ms through the full observation window. Fixed at the root: `pushState`/`replaceState`'s 3rd argument is the target URL (`null`/undefined means "keep current"); the patched wrappers now pass it through to `notifyStart()`, which resolves it against `window.location.href` and no-ops entirely for a same-URL call — no gen bump, no dispatch, no timer, nothing to ever get stuck on. Ported identically to both apps. This is a general, `router.refresh()`-agnostic fix — it fires for ANY same-URL `pushState`/`replaceState` call, not just this one call site, so it also protects every OTHER place in the app that calls `router.refresh()` after a mutation (there are several, per the `UnsavedChangesBar`/settings-save convention) from the same class of bug, not just the branding page. Verified live: the exact toast-then-4-second-watch scenario above now shows zero loader appearances; a genuine cross-page navigation (different URL) still shows the loader correctly, confirming the fix is scoped to same-URL calls only, not navigation in general.

Verified live: exactly one loader element ever renders at a time in either app (Playwright element-count assertion), confirmed with the same simulated RSC-stall scenario, plus the network-log-timed gap check above.

Verified live throughout: Playwright scripts under throttled/heavily-throttled CDP network conditions confirming (a) the pill appears for genuinely slow requests including a stalled route-fetch, (b) it never blocks a second click even mid-display, (c) `navigationPending` always clears (direct Redux-state polling, no stuck state), (d) zero "useInsertionEffect" or other console warnings across rapid nav clicks + back/forward, (e) exactly one loader element visible at any time — all in both tenant-web and super-admin.

**DB indexing**: static analysis (repository `where`/`orderBy` grep cross-referenced against `schema.prisma`'s existing `@@index` inventory — no `pg_stat_statements`/query-log evidence available in this dev setup, noted as a gap) found 15 missing composite indexes on hot-path tenant-scoped queries — mostly `branchId`-filtered finance/attendance dashboard aggregates with no index containing `branchId` at all, and several "browse all X" default-sort columns (`createdAt`/`checkInTime`) with no covering index. Added via `prisma migrate dev` (`20260801192949_add_hot_path_indexes`, plain `CREATE INDEX`, matching this repo's existing migration convention — no schema change beyond `@@index` additions, so no RLS migration needed): `users(tenantId,createdAt)`, `users(tenantId,lastLoginAt)`, `staffProfiles(tenantId,workStatus)`, `members(tenantId,createdAt)`, `members(tenantId,trainerId)`, `attendance(tenantId,checkInTime)`, `attendance(tenantId,branchId,attendanceDate)`, `memberPayments(tenantId,branchId,paymentDate)`, `memberInvoices(tenantId,branchId,status)`, `memberInvoices(tenantId,createdAt)`, `auditLogs(tenantId,actorUserId)`, `membershipPlans(tenantId,isActive)` (every sibling catalog — Workout/Diet/Exercise/Food — already had this; only Membership Plans was missing it), `income(tenantId,branchId,incomeDate)`, `expenses(tenantId,branchId,expenseDate)`, and `supportTickets(tenantId,status,createdAt)` (replacing the old lone `[tenantId]` index, which couldn't serve the status filter or the createdAt sort — its leftmost prefix still covers everything the old index did; the separate `[status,priority]` index for the cross-tenant admin queue view is untouched). Verified live: `EXPLAIN` with `enable_seqscan=off` confirms each new index is valid and gets picked by the planner (an unforced `EXPLAIN` on the current near-empty dev tables correctly still prefers a sequential scan — expected Postgres behavior at this row count, not a sign the index is unused).

Verified: typecheck/eslint (0 warnings, both apps) + build + vitest (237/237) on the API, typecheck/eslint (0 warnings) on both tenant-web and super-admin.

34 — **Scheduler & Background Jobs module**: user-requested centralized job scheduling/queue management system — the explicit mandate was "do not use cron logic inside individual modules... all automation must be managed through this centralized scheduler," which is exactly the pre-existing architecture: 4 modules (`subscription`, `reports`, `members`, `tenant-announcements`) each ran their own module-local `Queue`/`Worker`/`{repeat:{every: N_MS}}` with `removeOnComplete/Fail: true` (zero persisted execution history ever existed for any of them).

**New Prisma models** (admin-plane, no RLS — same convention as `FeatureFlag`/`AdminAuditLog`): `ScheduledJob` (one row per job TYPE — mutable scheduling/runtime state: `status`/`isPaused`/`cronPattern`/`timezone`/`priority`/`maxRetries`/`retryDelayMs`/`timeoutMs`/`lastRunAt`/`lastStatus`/`nextRunAt`, seeded/upserted from an in-code registry at boot) and `JobExecution` (durable per-attempt history — `status`/`trigger`/`attempt`/`startedAt`/`finishedAt`/`durationMs`/`result` (Json)/`error`, one row per actual run — this is what makes "Get Job History"/"Get Failed Jobs" real queries instead of reading ephemeral, self-pruning BullMQ state). Shared `JobRunStatus` enum (`PENDING/SCHEDULED/RUNNING/COMPLETED/FAILED/CANCELLED/PAUSED`) covers both the job definition's own display status and a single execution's outcome; `JobTriggerType` is `SCHEDULED/MANUAL/RETRY`.

**Centralized engine** (`modules/scheduler/services/scheduler-engine.service.ts`): one BullMQ `Queue`+`Worker` per `JobCategory` (6 total: `scheduler-membership/attendance/payment/notification/report/maintenance`, concurrency 2 each) — matches the spec's "Multiple Queues." A static `JOB_REGISTRY` (`constants/job-registry.ts`, 27 entries) is the single source of truth: `{name, category, cronPattern, timezone, priority, maxRetries, retryDelayMs, timeoutMs, handler}`. `initScheduler()` upserts every entry into `ScheduledJob` and registers a BullMQ repeatable job (`repeat:{pattern, tz}`, real cron patterns — not the old `every: ms`) for every job not `PAUSED`/`CANCELLED`; safe to call once per process start (BullMQ dedupes repeatable jobs by name+pattern, so re-registering across restarts is a no-op, not a duplicate). Every execution (scheduled, manual, or retry) funnels through one `executeJob()`: a Redis `SET NX` lock (`scheduler-lock:{jobName}`, TTL = timeout+30s — belt-and-suspenders duplicate-execution prevention on top of BullMQ's own per-queue processing) guards against overlap, a `JobExecution` row is created before the handler runs and updated after (COMPLETED/FAILED, with `durationMs`/`result`/`error`), the handler races against `timeoutMs` via `Promise.race`, and failure rethrows so BullMQ's own `attempts`/`backoff:{type:'exponential'}` drives the retry (each retry attempt gets its own `JobExecution` row via `bullJob.attemptsMade`). `ScheduledJob.nextRunAt` is refreshed from BullMQ's own repeatable-job `next` timestamp both at registration and after every execution. Job-level actions: `triggerJob`/`pauseJob`/`resumeJob`/`cancelJob` (cancel = remove the repeatable schedule AND purge any already-queued waiting/delayed instances of that job name, stronger than pause; `resumeJob` reactivates from either PAUSED or CANCELLED)/`retryFailedJob` (re-enqueues based on the latest FAILED `JobExecution`, 400s via `ValidationError` if none exists). Queue-level actions: `getQueueStatuses` (`getJobCounts`+`isPaused`), `retryQueue` (retries every BullMQ-failed job in that queue), `clearQueue` (`queue.drain(true)` — waiting/delayed backlog only, deliberately leaves failed/completed history intact since that's what the DB-backed history/failed-jobs views read from, not `obliterate()`), `pauseQueue`/`resumeQueue` (native BullMQ queue-level pause).

**Backend admin module** (`modules/scheduler/`, 5-layer, mirrors `admin-payments`): routes mounted at `/admin/scheduler/...` — `GET /dashboard`, `GET /jobs` (paginated, `?category=`), `GET /jobs/history` (paginated, `?jobName=&status=`), `GET /jobs/failed` (paginated), `GET /jobs/:name`, `POST /jobs/:name/{trigger,retry,pause,resume,cancel}`, `GET /queues`, `POST /queues/:queueName/{retry,clear,pause,resume}` — each gated per-route by new colon-separated permissions `scheduler:view`/`scheduler:manage`/`scheduler:trigger`/`scheduler:retry`/`scheduler:pause` (granted to `SUPER_ADMIN` via `*` and explicitly to `TECHNICAL_ADMIN`). Dashboard aggregates `runningJobs`/`scheduledJobs`/`failedJobs`/`pausedJobs`/`queueSize`/`queueHealth` (healthy/degraded/unhealthy heuristic)/`workerStatus`/`avgProcessingTimeMs`/`successRate` from the last 200 executions.

**Migrated the 4 existing scattered jobs** — their own `Queue`/`Worker`/`repeat` registration files were deleted entirely (`subscription-billing.jobs.ts`, `scheduled-reports.jobs.ts`, `membership-expiry.jobs.ts`, `tenant-announcement-scheduler.jobs.ts`), and `server.ts`'s bootstrap/shutdown now calls only `initScheduler()`/`stopScheduler()` instead of 4 separate `startXJobs()`/`stopXJobs()` pairs. Their tested business logic moved UNCHANGED into `modules/scheduler/handlers/*.handlers.ts` (`subscription-billing.handlers.ts`'s `platformSubscriptionBillingSweep` kept as one atomic 7-phase handler rather than split apart — it's FitCloud's own tenant-billing lifecycle, a different concern from the spec's per-member "Payment Jobs", and its phases were already tested together).

**~22 net-new job types** across the 6 spec'd categories, real (not stubbed) wherever a real implementation was feasible: **Membership** — `membership-renewal-reminder` (existing logic, moved), `membership-expiry-check` (new: read-only audit, doesn't mutate), `auto-membership-status-update` (existing status-flip logic), `membership-expired-notification` (existing notify logic, now separately deduped over a bounded 14-day window — the original combined query naturally self-limited by re-querying only ACTIVE rows; splitting it required an explicit bounded window + longer dedupe TTL to avoid the split re-scanning the entire historical EXPIRED population forever). **Attendance** — daily/weekly/monthly check-in aggregation (new, real cross-tenant `groupBy`). **Payment** — `payment-reminder`/`outstanding-payment-reminder` (new, real `MemberInvoice` queries, two different windows: due-soon vs. already-overdue), `failed-payment-retry` (new, real `MemberPayment` FAILED-status nudge), `invoice-generation` (new, real — reuses `MemberInvoiceRepository`'s exact create/numbering logic via a tenant-scoped client for memberships entering their renewal window). **Notification** — `send-scheduled-notifications` (closes a previously-documented real gap: `Notification.scheduledAt` was persisted but never acted on; `admin-notification.service.ts`'s dispatch logic was extracted into an exported `dispatchNotification()` so the manual "Send" button and this new sweep share one path), `send-scheduled-announcements` (existing logic, moved), `birthday-wishes` (new — the `BIRTHDAY_WISHES` template existed since the Notifications module was built with zero firing point until now; new `notifyBirthdayWishes()` trigger), `welcome-messages` (welcome mail fires synchronously+event-driven at registration, NOT normally schedulable — this job is a safety-net catch-up only, using a new `welcome-sent:{memberId}` Redis marker set by the synchronous path itself so the sweep only catches genuine misses, never double-sends). **Report** — `tenant-scheduled-reports-dispatch` (existing logic, moved), `daily-revenue-summary`/`weekly-branch-performance-report`/`monthly-reports-rollup` (new, real cross-tenant `Payment`/`MemberPayment`/`Attendance` aggregations, stored as `JobExecution.result` JSON — no recipient config exists for these platform-level rollups, so they aggregate-and-record rather than email). **Maintenance** (7, matching the spec exactly) — `clear-expired-sessions` (real `RefreshToken` deletion), `cleanup-old-notifications`/`cleanup-audit-logs` (real retention deletes, 90d/180d), `cache-refresh` (real — clears known Redis prefixes via `redis.keys()`+`del()`, NOT a full flush since Redis also holds BullMQ/rate-limit state), `database-optimization` (real — `ANALYZE`, deliberately not `VACUUM FULL`/`REINDEX` which lock tables), `cleanup-temporary-files`/`cleanup-failed-uploads` (honest no-ops — no local temp-file storage or upload-attempt tracking table exists in this codebase to act on; each returns `{skipped:true, reason:...}` rather than fabricating a fake "cleaned" result).

**Frontend** (`apps/super-admin`, new `features/scheduler/` mirroring `features/tenants/`): Dashboard (`/scheduler`, `StatisticCard` grid + queue-health badge + worker-status grid, 5s polling via `refetchInterval`), Job List (`/scheduler/jobs`, category filter, `DataTable`+`Pagination`, per-row Trigger/Pause/Resume/Cancel gated on the matching `scheduler:*` permission), Job Details (`/scheduler/jobs/[name]`, full config + action buttons + recent-executions table, Retry button only shown when a FAILED execution exists), Queue Monitor (`/scheduler/queues`, per-queue counts/health + Retry Failed/Clear Backlog/Pause/Resume), Failed Jobs (`/scheduler/failed`, paginated, per-row Retry), Job History (`/scheduler/history`, job-name text filter + status `<select>`, paginated) — all sharing one `SchedulerSubNav` tab strip, reusing `DataTable`/`Pagination`/`StatisticCard`/`Badge` verbatim (no new UI primitives), registered in `constants/routes.ts` + `(portal)/layout.tsx`'s `NAV_ITEMS`.

Verified: typecheck/eslint (0 warnings, both apps)/build/vitest (237/237, API) all green; `prisma migrate dev` applied cleanly (`20260803104246_scheduler_background_jobs_module`) and `prisma db seed` re-run (TECHNICAL_ADMIN went 5→10 permissions, SUPER_ADMIN 17→22). Live verification against the running dev stack (not just static checks): dashboard shows all 27 registered jobs with real BullMQ-backed counts; manually triggered a job end-to-end (execution row created→COMPLETED, real `result` JSON persisted, `triggeredBy` captured); paused a job then confirmed `resume` correctly restored its `nextRunAt`; **paused an entire queue and confirmed a manually-triggered job genuinely sat queued (not executed) until the queue was resumed** — execution history showed it started the instant the queue resumed, not before; cancelled a job (state→CANCELLED) then resumed it back; confirmed 404s for unknown job/queue names and a 422 `ValidationError` when retrying a job with no failed execution; confirmed `send-scheduled-notifications`/`tenant-scheduled-reports-dispatch` genuinely self-fired on their own cron schedule during the session (not just via manual trigger) — visible as `completed` counts climbing between dashboard polls with zero manual intervention. Full Playwright pass across all 6 new pages (dashboard/jobs list+filter/job detail/queue monitor/failed jobs/history) confirmed zero console errors, real toast notifications on every mutation (`[data-sonner-toast]` DOM-verified directly after a script's `text=` locator gave a false negative on the first pass — a script-timing artifact, not a real bug, cross-checked and confirmed), and end-to-end trigger→pause→resume state transitions visible in the UI.

35 — **AI Business Assistant module**: user-requested tenant-facing AI chat assistant with a swappable provider abstraction, streaming, conversation history, tenant/role-aware context, and confirm-before-execute "AI Actions." Explicit constraints: never hardcode a provider/key, default to a free OpenRouter model, and never execute a write without explicit user confirmation.

**Provider abstraction** (`modules/ai-assistant/providers/`): one `AiProvider` interface (`complete()`/`stream()`, `AsyncGenerator<string>` deltas) — `factory.ts`'s `getAiProvider()` is the only place `AI_PROVIDER` is switched on, returning a cached singleton keyed by `(provider, baseUrl, apiKey)`. `openai-compatible.provider.ts` implements the OpenAI Chat Completions wire format and is shared VERBATIM by `openrouter` (default), `openai`, `azure-openai` (via `buildUrl`/`authHeader` overrides — deployment-in-URL + `api-key` header instead of `Authorization: Bearer`), and `ollama` (local, no key required); `anthropic.provider.ts` and `gemini.provider.ts` are separate real adapters for their genuinely different wire formats (Anthropic: `x-api-key`/`anthropic-version` headers + a top-level `system` field instead of a system-role message + named SSE event types; Gemini: API key as a query param + `user`/`model` roles + `systemInstruction`). Adding a provider means one new adapter file + one `case` in the factory switch — nothing in the chat service/routes/controllers changes. `providers/sse-parser.ts` is a small shared SSE-line reader (buffers partial chunks, splits on blank-line records) every streaming adapter reuses.

**Configuration** — `config/env.ts`'s `env.ai` (new): `AI_PROVIDER` (enum, default `openrouter`), `AI_MODEL` (default `meta-llama/llama-3.1-8b-instruct:free` — an OpenRouter free-tier model), `AI_API_KEY`, `AI_BASE_URL` (overrides the provider's built-in default endpoint — required for `azure-openai`, which has no sane default since every Azure resource has its own URL), `AI_TEMPERATURE` (default 0.7), `AI_MAX_TOKENS` (default 1024). `env.ai.isConfigured` is `true` for `ollama` unconditionally (local daemon, no key) or when `AI_API_KEY` is set — checked before any provider call, and now ALSO checked at the very top of `AiChatService#respond` (added after live-testing surfaced that persisting the user's message BEFORE that check left an orphaned message with no reply once a key was later added).

**Schema** (tenant-plane, WITH RLS — `ai_conversations`/`ai_messages`/`ai_request_logs`, migration `20260803114656_ai_business_assistant_module` + RLS migration `20260803114700_enable_rls_ai_assistant`): `AiConversation` (one per chat, scoped to `userId` — "conversation history per user" business rule, `title` auto-derived from the first message), `AiMessage` (`role` USER/ASSISTANT/SYSTEM, `actionPayload`/`actionStatus` present only while a proposed action awaits confirm/cancel), `AiRequestLog` ("Log AI requests" business rule — provider/model/token counts/duration/status per actual provider call, independent of `AiMessage` since a request can error before any content streams back).

**Context-gathering** (`services/ai-context.service.ts`) — builds the system-prompt's "what does this gym look like right now" block from REAL queries (member/staff/branch counts, expiring memberships, today's check-ins, workout/diet assignment counts, this-month revenue + outstanding invoices, scheduled-report count, unread notifications, timezone/currency), with each section gated behind the SAME permission key the equivalent page already requires (`members:view`, `finance:view`, etc.) — a user without `finance:view` never sees revenue figures surface through the AI either, closing the same gap `requirePermission` closes for the REST API itself. One section's query failing doesn't take down the others.

**AI Actions** (`constants/action-types.ts` + `services/ai-actions.service.ts`) — 6 types (create_member, renew_membership, assign_workout, assign_diet, generate_report, send_notification). The model is instructed (system prompt) to end a message proposing one with a fenced ` ```ai-action ` JSON block; `extractActionProposal()` parses and strips it from the displayed reply, validating both the outer shape and the action-specific zod params schema — malformed/unrecognized JSON silently degrades to plain text rather than surfacing an error, since a model hallucinating a broken block shouldn't break the chat. **Nothing executes from the chat/streaming path** — a proposal is only ever persisted as `actionStatus: 'PENDING'`; the SEPARATE `POST .../actions/:messageId/confirm` endpoint re-checks the confirming user's permission for the action's real domain permission (`members:create`, `memberships:renew`, `workouts:assign`, `diets:assign`, `reports:export`, `notifications:manage` — reusing the exact same permission each action's underlying service call already requires everywhere else, not a new blanket "AI actions" permission) and only THEN calls the real service (`MemberService.create`/`.assignMembership`, `MemberService.renewMembership`, `WorkoutPlanService.assign`, `DietPlanService.assign`, `ReportExportService.exportCsv` — returns a row-count + filename summary since there's no new file-delivery infra, full export stays in the Reports Center — `tenantNotificationService.notifyTenant`), appending a SYSTEM message with the real result. `/cancel` marks it `CANCELLED` with zero side effects. Both confirm and cancel 422 if the action was already resolved (double-click safe).

**Backend module** (`modules/ai-assistant/`, mirrors `modules/support/`'s layer shape): `GET /ai/config` (provider/model/temperature/maxTokens/isConfigured — no key ever returned), `GET|POST /ai/conversations`, `GET|DELETE /ai/conversations/:id`, `POST /ai/conversations/:id/clear`, `POST /ai/conversations/:id/messages` (non-streaming Send Message — internally drains the same generator the streaming endpoint uses), `POST /ai/conversations/:id/messages/stream` (raw SSE — first one in this codebase; `res.writeHead(200,{'Content-Type':'text/event-stream',...})`/`res.write`/`res.end`, an `AbortController` tied to `req.on('close')` so **Stop Generation** (the client aborting the fetch) actually cancels the upstream provider call, not just the client-side display — also backs **Regenerate Response** via `{regenerate: true}` in the body, which deletes the trailing assistant message(s) and re-runs from the same point), `POST .../actions/:messageId/{confirm,cancel}`. New permission `ai:use` (colon-separated, granted to OWNER via `*`, and explicitly to MANAGER/TRAINER/RECEPTIONIST — deliberately NOT to MEMBER, since the context list (Members/Staff/Branches/.../Settings) makes this a staff tool, not a member-facing one). Gated behind the pre-existing `ai_coach` plan-feature-flag catalog key (previously an architecture-only "coming soon" entry, `included: false` on every tier since Prompt 8) — flipped to `true` for Professional+Enterprise tiers; existing tenants provisioned before this flip need a manual `TenantModule` row backfill (same precedent as other prompts' "re-seed doesn't retroactively touch already-provisioned tenants" gotcha).

**Frontend** (`apps/tenant-web`, new `features/ai-assistant/` + a new dependency, `react-markdown`+`remark-gfm` — confirmed absent from this app before, no markdown renderer existed anywhere): a floating launcher button (`ai-launcher-button.tsx`, mounted once in `app-providers.tsx` as a sibling of `<SessionExpiryModal/>`/`<Toaster/>`, gated on BOTH `tenant.featureFlags.includes('ai_coach')` AND `hasPermission('ai:use')` — renders nothing at all if either fails, same two-check pattern `/support`'s page uses) opens `ai-chat-panel.tsx`, a `Drawer` (this app's existing Radix-Dialog-based slide-out primitive, widened past its `max-w-sm` default) containing `conversation-list.tsx` (new/select/delete) and `chat-window.tsx` (message list + `SuggestedPrompts` on an empty conversation + streaming display + Send/Stop/Regenerate/Clear). `message-bubble.tsx` renders markdown via `react-markdown`+`remark-gfm` with a per-bubble Copy button; `action-confirmation-card.tsx` is the ONE place a proposed action's Confirm/Cancel buttons live. `hooks/use-ai-assistant.ts`'s `useAiChatStream` hook owns the SSE-consumption lifecycle via a raw `fetch()` (not axios — its browser adapter can't hand back a readable byte stream as chunks arrive), reusing the exact same `Authorization`/`X-Tenant-Slug` header logic `api-client.ts`'s interceptors apply. `ai-settings-dialog.tsx` is read-only (mirrors the backend's config-only, no-update-endpoint design — provider/model are environment-configured, not user-editable).

**A real gap found via live testing, fixed in the same pass**: sending a message while the AI provider isn't configured (or any other request-level failure) silently dropped the optimistic user-message bubble with ZERO visible feedback — the SSE `error` event was received but nothing consumed it. Fixed by adding `toast.error(event.message)` in `useAiChatStream`'s stream-consumption loop; confirmed live (Playwright, polling for `[data-sonner-toast]`) the toast now genuinely appears.

Verified: typecheck/eslint (0 warnings, both apps)/build/vitest (237/237, API). Live verification against the running dev stack: `GET /ai/config` correctly reports `isConfigured: false` with no key set; full conversation CRUD (create/list/get/clear/delete) via curl; a nonexistent conversation ID correctly 404s (tenant isolation); sending a message with no key configured fails BEFORE persisting anything (confirmed message count stays 0, not 1, across repeated attempts — the exact bug the isConfigured-guard fix closes); **the full AI Action confirm/cancel loop verified end-to-end independent of any real LLM first** (a proposal was inserted directly as `ai_messages.action_payload`, exactly the shape a real model response produces): confirming a `send_notification` action created a REAL row in `tenant_notifications` and flipped the message to `CONFIRMED` with a `SYSTEM` note recording the result; cancelling a second proposal left zero side effects, flipped it to `CANCELLED`, and re-confirming correctly 422'd. DB-level permission check confirmed `ai:use` was granted to exactly OWNER/MANAGER/TRAINER/RECEPTIONIST/SUPER_ADMIN, not MEMBER. Full Playwright pass on the frontend (login → floating button visible → panel opens → new chat → suggested prompts → send → conversation history → settings dialog) — zero console errors.

**Then verified against a REAL OpenRouter key + a real free model, end-to-end** (user supplied a working `AI_API_KEY` in the same session): found and fixed two more real bugs in the process — (1) `factory.ts` computed the provider's base URL as `env.ai.baseUrl ?? DEFAULT_BASE_URLS[provider]`, but `AI_BASE_URL=` (present-but-empty in `.env`) is a valid, non-`undefined` string as far as zod's `.optional()` is concerned, so `??` never fell through to the default — fixed by switching to `||`. (2) The hardcoded default free model (`meta-llama/llama-3.1-8b-instruct:free`) had been pulled from OpenRouter's free tier entirely (404 "no longer available for free" — free-tier model availability rotates over time, not a bug in this codebase) — swapped the default to `inclusionai/ling-3.0-flash:free` after confirming it live (a couple of other current free models were tried first: `google/gemma-4-31b-it:free` hit a transient shared-pool 429, `openai/gpt-oss-20b:free` is a "reasoning" model whose `delta.content` can stay empty for many tokens while `delta.reasoning` streams instead — the working default is a plain instruct model that streams real `content` deltas immediately). With those two fixes: a real chat message got a real, context-grounded reply ("We have **20** active members" — matching the tenant's actual seeded member count); the streaming endpoint produced real incremental token-by-token deltas assembling correctly into the final text (verified via curl AND visually in the browser — screenshotted, markdown bold rendering correctly, `…` placeholder during streaming, Regenerate button appearing after completion); and asking the assistant to "send a notification" produced a genuine model-generated `ai-action` proposal (correctly inferring `category: ANNOUNCEMENT`) which, on confirm, created a real `tenant_notifications` row — the entire pipeline from a real user prompt through a real LLM through the confirm gate to a real database write, with zero manual data insertion this time. All test conversations/notifications cleaned up afterward. Nothing is unverified in this module anymore.

35.5 — **AI Business Assistant: per-tenant "bring your own key" (BYOK) + actionable expiry messaging (same-session follow-up)**: user-requested extension — each tenant should be able to configure their OWN AI provider/key (separate from the platform-wide default), and see a clear "add credit or update your key" message in the chat itself when it stops working, rather than a dead-end error.

**Storage**: new `TenantAiSettings` (tenant-plane, WITH RLS, migrations `20260804043312_ai_assistant_tenant_byok` + `20260804043400_enable_rls_ai_tenant_settings`) — one row per tenant, every field optional (`provider`/`model`/`apiKeyEncrypted`/`baseUrl`/`temperature`/`maxTokens`), `null` meaning "fall through to the platform default" for that specific field (a tenant can override just the model while still using the platform's key, for instance). No prior encryption-at-rest precedent existed in this codebase to reuse (the one prior mention, on `Payment.metadata`, was aspirational/undone) — new `core/security/encryption.util.ts` (`encryptSecret`/`decryptSecret`/`maskSecret`, AES-256-GCM via Node's built-in `crypto`, no new dependency) is the first real implementation, keyed by a new required `ENCRYPTION_KEY` env var (32-byte, base64 — generate with `node -e "console.log(require('crypto').randomBytes(32).toString('base64'))"`). The raw key is never sent back to the frontend — `GET /ai/settings` returns only `hasApiKey`/`apiKeyMasked` (last 4 chars).

**Provider factory made tenant-aware**: `providers/factory.ts#getAiProvider()` now takes an optional `AiConfigOverride`; with no override it returns the same cached platform-default singleton as before, with one it always builds a fresh (uncached) instance — these adapters are stateless `fetch()` wrappers, so per-request construction has no real cost and avoids a per-tenant cache-invalidation story for zero benefit. `AiChatService#respond()` resolves the tenant's override (`AiTenantSettingsService#resolveProviderOverride`, decrypting the key only at this point) before persisting anything, same "fail before persisting" discipline as the original `isConfigured` guard.

**Actionable error classification**: every provider adapter's HTTP-failure path now classifies the response status via a new `providers/error-classification.ts` (`401`/`403` → `AUTH_INVALID`, `402`/`429` → `QUOTA_EXCEEDED`, missing-config → `NOT_CONFIGURED`, else `GENERIC`), attached via `AppError`'s `details.code`. `ChatStreamEvent`'s `error` variant now carries this `code` through both the streaming AND non-streaming send-message paths, with a distinct human message per code ("Your AI provider API key looks invalid or has been revoked" / "out of credit or rate-limited"). Frontend: `useAiChatStream` shows these as a `sonner` toast with an `action` button ("Open AI Settings") that routes straight to the fix — confirmed live: `AUTH_INVALID` really does reach the UI as a clickable action, not just a dead-end message.

**Frontend**: new "AI Assistant" tab in `GymSettingsNav` → `/gym-settings/ai` (same `UnsavedChangesBar`+`Card` form pattern as every other Gym Settings page) — provider dropdown/model/base URL/temperature/max-tokens, all optional, plus a password-masked API key field (placeholder shows the existing key's last 4 chars, typing replaces it, a "Clear stored key" link explicitly reverts just that field, a page-level "Reset everything to the platform default" link `DELETE`s the whole row). The in-chat `AiSettingsDialog` (previously read-only/env-var-only) now reflects `usingOwnKey` and links to the full settings page. Gated the same as the rest of the module (`settings:read`/`settings:manage`, not a new permission — this is exactly what those keys already mean elsewhere).

Verified: typecheck/eslint (0 warnings, both apps)/build/vitest (237/237, API). Live, end-to-end, with real HTTP calls to OpenRouter (not simulated): (1) confirmed a tenant starts on the platform default (`usingPlatformDefault: true`); (2) set the tenant's own real key + a different model via `PUT /ai/settings`, confirmed `apiKeyMasked` matched the real key's last 4 chars, confirmed `GET /ai/config` flipped to `usingOwnKey: true`, and sent a real chat message that genuinely used the override (the model followed an exact-echo instruction, proving the decrypted tenant key — not the platform one — made the call); (3) set a deliberately invalid key, sent a message, and got back a REAL 401 from OpenRouter correctly classified as `AUTH_INVALID` with the actionable message, on both the non-streaming AND SSE-streaming endpoints; (4) `DELETE /ai/settings` correctly reverted to the platform default. Full Playwright pass on the frontend: AI Settings page loads/saves with a toast, and — the actual point of this feature — triggering a chat send with the bad key produced the actionable toast with a real, clickable "Open AI Settings" button (screenshotted) — zero console errors throughout.

**Housekeeping note**: partway through this follow-up, all three dev servers (api/tenant-web/super-admin) were found down — the api process had exited (not hung; nothing was listening on :4000) for reasons unrelated to this session's own code changes. Restarted both `api` and `tenant-web` in the background to continue verification; if a THIRD dev-server outage is seen again in this environment, worth checking for an external cause (OS sleep, resource limits) rather than assuming a code regression.

35.6 — **AI Business Assistant: Super Admin side (same-session follow-up, phase 2)**: the second half of the user's request — the platform team gets its own AI assistant (own chat history, own optional API key), with context over tenants/payments/revenue/support instead of members/attendance/etc. Deliberately query-only for this pass — no AI Actions/confirm-execute flow on the admin side (not asked for, and admin-side write actions — suspend a tenant, refund a payment — are meaningfully higher-risk than the tenant side's create-a-member scope).

**Schema**: new admin-plane tables (no RLS, same convention as every other admin-plane table) — `AdminAiConversation`/`AdminAiMessage` (scoped to `adminUserId`, reuses the existing `AiMessageRole` enum verbatim rather than duplicating it), `AdminAiRequestLog`, and `PlatformAiSettings` — the admin-side equivalent of `TenantAiSettings`, but application-enforced SINGLE-ROW (there's only one "platform team" to override for, not one row per tenant) rather than per-user. Migration `20260804050048_ai_assistant_admin_side`.

**Reused, not duplicated**: the entire `modules/ai-assistant/providers/` abstraction (interface, OpenRouter/OpenAI/Anthropic/Gemini/Azure/Ollama adapters, `getAiProvider()`/`getEffectiveAiConfig()`, error classification) is provider-agnostic and tenant-agnostic by design — the admin module imports it directly rather than re-implementing anything. New `modules/admin-ai-assistant/` mirrors the tenant module's shape (services/repositories/controllers/routes/validators) but simpler: `AdminAiChatService#respond()` is the same streaming-generator pattern minus the AI-Actions branch, `AdminAiSettingsService` is `AiTenantSettingsService`'s shape against the singleton table, `admin-ai-context.service.ts` mirrors `ai-context.service.ts`'s permission-gated-sections pattern but for admin permissions (`tenants:read` → tenant counts by status, `payments:read` → revenue this-month/all-time, `revenue:read` → MRR from active subscriptions grouped by plan, `support:manage` → open ticket count).

**Endpoints** under `/admin/ai/*`, gated by `dashboard:read` at the router level (every admin role already has this — reused rather than inventing a new permission, matching "respect existing permissions") plus `settings:manage` specifically for the settings write endpoints (same permission that already gates System Settings). No new admin permission keys added.

**Frontend** (`apps/super-admin`, new `features/ai-assistant/` mirroring tenant-web's structure almost 1:1, adapted): same floating-launcher + slide-out-panel pattern, gated on `dashboard:read` via `useHasPermission`. New dependencies `react-markdown`+`remark-gfm` (confirmed absent before, same as tenant-web needed) and a new `components/ui/drawer.tsx` (copied verbatim from tenant-web's — this app had no slide-out-panel primitive before, only `dialog.tsx`). Settings UI lives at `/settings/ai` (a real page this time, not a Gym-Settings-style tab bar, since super-admin's Settings area is a single flat page) — linked both from the AI panel's settings dialog and a new card on the main `/settings` page for discoverability. Mounted once in `providers/app-providers.tsx` as a sibling of `<Toaster/>`.

Verified: typecheck/eslint (0 warnings, both apps)/build/vitest (237/237, API). Live, end-to-end, real HTTP calls: `GET /admin/ai/config` correctly falls back to the env-var default with no `PlatformAiSettings` row; a real chat message got a real, context-grounded reply ("We have **1** tenant (currently in trial)" — correct, matches the one real seeded tenant); streaming produced real incremental deltas assembling into a correct answer ("$0.00" revenue — correct, the one tenant is still on trial with no payments); setting the platform team's own key flipped `usingOwnKey: true` and was genuinely used; setting a deliberately invalid key produced a real 401 from OpenRouter correctly classified `AUTH_INVALID`; `DELETE /admin/ai/settings` correctly reverted. Full Playwright pass: floating button visible, chat panel opens, a real message gets a real reply (screenshotted), `/settings/ai` loads — zero console errors.

36 — **Security hardening (audit remediation, High-priority items)**: a full-codebase security/performance/UX audit (3 parallel research passes) surfaced a prioritized punch list; this prompt closes the three **High** items under Security.

**Global API rate limiter**: every endpoint outside the 6 pre-existing auth-specific limiters (login/register/OTP/password-reset/invitations/admin-auth) had zero throttling. Added `apiRateLimiter()` in `core/middleware/rate-limiter.ts` — 300 req/min per `tenantId:ip` (or `platform:ip` for the admin/webhook plane), same Redis-backed `createRateLimiter()` factory as every other limiter, mounted in `app.ts` right before the `v1Router` mount so it covers the entire API surface including `/admin/*`. Gateway webhooks (`/webhook/*`) are exempted via a new `skip` option — Stripe/Razorpay legitimately burst-retry and are already authenticated by signature, not this. `createRateLimiter()` also gained a blanket `env.isTest` skip so the real supertest-based integration spec (`auth.integration.spec.ts`) never risks tripping a limiter across repeated CI runs sharing one Redis instance.

**`SupportTicket` RLS gap closed**: this was the one tenant-scoped table (confirmed by cross-referencing every `tenantId`-bearing model against every `enable_rls_*` migration) relying solely on application-level `WHERE tenantId = ...` filtering, explicitly flagged as such in `ticket.repository.ts`'s own header comment ("in case RLS is ever added to this table"). Added the standard hand-written migration (`20260804060000_enable_rls_support_tickets`, same `ENABLE/FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` + `tenant_isolation` policy shape as every other one) — `tenant_id` is nullable (a ticket can predate any tenant, per Prompt 9), and Postgres's `NULL = uuid` semantics correctly keep those rows invisible to every tenant RLS session with no special-casing needed. Updated the repository's comment to match. The admin plane's cross-tenant ticket queue reads via the raw, non-tenant-scoped client and is unaffected.

**Dependency vulnerability scanning**: `pnpm audit` found 40 pre-existing advisories (1 critical — `vitest@2.1.9`, a direct dev-only dependency; 20 high, mostly `brace-expansion` several layers deep inside `exceljs`'s and ESLint tooling's transitive trees, not fixable with a one-line bump).
