37 — **Performance hardening (audit remediation, remaining High-priority items)**: closes the four **High** items under Performance & Scalability from the same audit Prompt 36 started (Security).

**Error tracking / APM**: `core/observability/sentry.ts`, gated entirely on `SENTRY_DSN` (`env.sentry.isConfigured`) — unset means every function is a no-op, zero overhead, never a boot requirement (same pattern as `env.ai`/`env.razorpay`). `initSentry()` called at the top of `server.ts`; `process.on('unhandledRejection'|'uncaughtException', ...)` handlers added (didn't exist before — a real gap, since either would previously crash with no capture at all). `captureError()` wired into `errorHandlerMiddleware` for both `AppError` 5xx and the generic unhandled-error branch, tagged with the same `requestId`/`tenantId`/`userId` already threaded through Winston via `AsyncLocalStorage`, so a Sentry event and its matching log lines cross-reference by `requestId`. Verified live: pointed `SENTRY_DSN` at a local fake-ingest HTTP listener, confirmed a real envelope (exact error message, stack trace, environment tag) arrived — `@sentry/node`'s auto-instrumentation also emitted Redis call-span transactions for free via `diagnostics_channel`, unprompted.

**Report/analytics N+1 fixes**: `trainerPerformanceReport`/`branchPerformanceReport` (`reports.service.ts`) and `branchComparison` (`analytics.service.ts`) each ran 3-5 queries *per row* (N trainers/branches × queries) — rewritten to fixed-count `groupBy()` aggregates + in-memory `Map` merges, regardless of row count. `Member.bulkImport` (`member.service.ts`) ran a `branch.findFirst`/`user.findFirst`/`membershipPlan.findByName` per CSV row even when many rows referenced the same branch/trainer/plan — now pre-fetches each reference set once into a lowercase-keyed `Map`; the actual per-row `create()`/`assignMembership()` calls stay sequential on purpose (memberId auto-generation and the subscription member-cap check both read-then-write and would race under real concurrency — parallelizing those would trade a performance win for a correctness risk). `Member.bulkAction` (activate/deactivate/delete) had no such constraint — each row is independent — so its sequential `for` loop became a concurrency-10 chunked `Promise.all`. Verified live: cross-checked `trainerPerformanceReport`'s aggregated count against a direct SQL query (both `2`) after temporarily flipping a trainer `ACTIVE`; live bulk-import exercising all three case-insensitive map lookups (branch/trainer/plan) with a nonexistent-branch-name row correctly falling back to the tenant's default branch, matching original behavior exactly; bulk activate/deactivate/delete including a deliberately-bogus id to confirm per-row error isolation survived the concurrency change.

**Report export off the request thread**: `report-export.service.ts`'s Excel/PDF rendering (ExcelJS zip compression, PDFKit per-cell text layout across up to 2000 rows) ran synchronously inline, genuinely blocking Node's single event loop for every other concurrent request on the instance. **Not** routed through BullMQ — every job worker in this codebase (`email.worker.ts`, `scheduler-engine.service.ts`) runs in-process on the same event loop as Express, so queueing alone wouldn't have fixed the actual problem. Instead: pure rendering functions extracted to `export-render.ts` (no Prisma/DB/request-context — safe to run anywhere), executed inside a real `node:worker_threads` Worker (`export-render.worker.ts` + `export-worker-runner.ts`) that genuinely runs on a separate thread. Dev/prod duality handled by checking `__filename`'s own extension at runtime (`.ts` under `tsx watch` → spawn with `execArgv: ['--require', 'tsx/cjs']` so the worker thread can `require()` the TS source directly; `.js` under the compiled `dist/` build → plain require, no flag needed) — confirmed both paths compile/resolve correctly. CSV stays inline (cheap string concatenation, not worth a worker round-trip). Verified live: real magic-byte-verified `.xlsx`/`.pdf` output via `file`; fired 5 concurrent PDF exports and measured `/health` latency in-flight (stayed 5-7ms throughout) to directly prove the event loop wasn't blocked, rather than trusting the theory.

**Object storage** (`core/storage/`): the single biggest architectural gap from the audit — every upload (avatars, member photos/documents, exercise images, branding assets, expense receipts, server-generated QR codes) was a base64 data-URL stored directly as `String` text in Postgres. Added MinIO to `docker-compose.yml` (S3-compatible, self-hosted — same `@aws-sdk/client-s3`/`@aws-sdk/s3-request-presigner` code talks to real AWS S3/Cloudflare R2/Spaces in production via different `S3_*` env vars, no code change) plus a one-shot `minio-init` (`minio/mc`) container that creates the bucket and a public-read policy scoped to just its `public/` prefix. `uploadDataUrl(dataUrl, {keyPrefix, visibility})` is the one funnel point: `visibility: 'public'` (avatars, member photos, QR codes, exercise images, all 5 branding fields) returns a stable direct URL stored in place of the old data-URL — **zero frontend changes**, every existing `<img src>`/`<a href>` read path just works. `visibility: 'private'` (member documents, expense receipts — genuinely sensitive) returns a bare object key instead; the DB column holds that key from then on, and the two read paths that serve those fields (`MemberService#listDocuments`/`#uploadDocument`, `ExpenseService`'s `toDto`) now call `presignGetUrl()` fresh on every response — a 1-hour presigned URL, never persisted. Gracefully degrades to storing the raw data-URL unchanged when `S3_*` env vars aren't set (`env.storage.isConfigured`) — this migration is purely additive, a dev machine that hasn't run `docker compose up minio minio-init` yet keeps working exactly as before. `isDataUrl()` distinguishes "this is a fresh upload" from "client resubmitted the existing unchanged URL/key" on every update path. Historical rows keep their old data-URL format until their next write — no backfill migration was run. Verified live end-to-end for all 7 field groups: real presigned document/receipt URLs that genuinely retrieve file bytes (confirmed unsigned direct access to the same private object correctly 403s); real public avatar/photo/QR-code/exercise-image/branding-logo URLs fetched with **zero auth headers** and confirmed publicly accessible; confirmed the DB column actually shrank (a 65-char object key vs. what would've been a multi-KB base64 string) via direct `psql`; confirmed the graceful-degradation fallback by temporarily unsetting `S3_BUCKET`, restarting, and verifying member creation (incl. QR generation) still succeeded with a plain data-URL and zero errors, then restored config. All test members/documents/exercises/expenses created during verification deleted afterward via direct SQL, same cleanup precedent as every prior prompt.

**`eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y`**: added to both frontend apps' `.eslintrc.json` (`plugin:jsx-a11y/recommended`, on top of whatever subset `next/core-web-vitals` already bundled) — a genuinely broader ruleset than what shipped before. Turning it on surfaced 19 findings total across both apps; each was individually triaged rather than blanket-disabled: **3 real, previously-unlabeled interactive elements fixed** (a toolbar's title-only buttons in the announcement rich-text editor gained `aria-label` alongside their existing `title`; a holiday-removal button and a document-delete button had no accessible name at all, both fixed with a descriptive `aria-label`); **7 false positives suppressed inline** with a comment explaining why, not blanket-disabled (`heading-has-content` on `AlertTitle`/`CardTitle` — content arrives via spread `...props`, the static rule can't see through that; `anchor-has-content` on the AI chat markdown-renderer's `a` override, same reason; `aria-role` on `<MessageBubble role="ASSISTANT">` — `role` there is that component's own sender-type prop, not a DOM ARIA role; `no-autofocus` on the OTP input's first digit and the "find your gym" form's sole field — both deliberate, caller-gated UX, not the unwanted-focus-theft pattern the rule guards against; a propagation-stopping wrapper `div` on the super-admin Plans page); **1 rule-level config fix** instead of N scattered suppressions (`label-has-associated-control`'s `controlComponents: ["Checkbox"]` — every native `<label>` wrapping the custom Radix-based `Checkbox` component across both apps is actually correctly labeled per HTML's own labelable-element semantics, `button` included; the rule's default heuristic just can't see through a custom component to know it renders one). Also fixed one structural gap in the *primitive* itself: `SearchBar` (`components/ui/search-bar.tsx`) had no accessible name at all beyond its visual placeholder — WCAG 4.1.2 doesn't count placeholder text as a label — now defaults `aria-label` to the placeholder text for every call site at once, overridable per-instance.

39 — **Member Self-Service Portal + Class/Session Booking**: closes the two remaining **High**-priority missing-feature items from the Prompt 36 audit. Two genuinely new features in one pass — a third, fully independent auth plane for members, and a real recurring-class/capacity-booking domain — because the second feature (booking) needed the first (a member identity to book *as*) to be meaningful, and both entry points (staff front-desk + member self-service) were built together per explicit scope.

**Part A — Member auth plane** (`modules/member-auth/`, `modules/member-portal/`): third JWT audience (`fitcloud-member-app`, `MemberJwtService` mirrors `AdminJwtService`'s shape exactly — claims `{sub, tenantId, sid, jti}`, deliberately no `role`/`permVer`, since a member only ever sees their own data and the member plane has zero RBAC). New tables `MemberCredential` (1:1 `Member`, `PENDING_ACTIVATION`/`ACTIVE`/`SUSPENDED`), `MemberSession` (refresh-token rotation, same reuse-detection pattern as the tenant plane), `MemberVerification` (one opaque-token table, `purpose: ACTIVATION|PASSWORD_RESET`, dual-purpose like the staff activation flow reuses `PasswordReset`). Login is by **`memberId`** (e.g. `MEM-0007`), not email — `Member.email` is optional and not unique per tenant, `memberId` is (`@@unique([tenantId, memberId])`). Activation is **staff-initiated**: `MemberService#sendPortalInvite(id, actor)` (new, gated on the existing `members:update` — no new permission) creates the credential row + emails an activation link; no public self-registration. `modules/member-portal/` is thin, read/write-your-own-data routes under `/portal/*` (router-level `memberAuthenticateMiddleware` is the only gate — no `requirePermission` anywhere in this module), every query hard-filtered to `memberId = req.memberAuth.sub` at the application level (RLS isolates by tenant, not member-within-tenant) — `GET /portal/me|attendance|workout|diet|invoices`, `POST /portal/workout/:id/progress` and `/portal/diet/:id/log` (mark exercise done/skip, log water/weight/meals — reuses `MemberWorkoutPlanRepository`/`MemberDietPlanRepository`'s existing merge-not-overwrite logic, doesn't reimplement it), `GET /portal/invoices/:id/download`. Frontend: new `apps/tenant-web/src/app/portal/*` route tree (own Redux slice `memberAuth` with its own `redux-persist` key, own axios instance reading `memberAuth.accessToken`, `MemberPortalShell` — a deliberately simple ~6-tab layout, not a reuse of the staff `AppShell`), `(authenticated)` route group applying the auth guard to everything except login/activate/forgot-password/reset-password. "Enable portal access" button added to the staff-side Member detail page (gated `members:update`).

**Part C — Class/session booking** (`modules/classes/`): new tables `GroupClass` (name/trainer?/branch/capacity/durationMinutes, mirrors `WorkoutPlan`'s shape), `GroupClassSchedule` (the recurring weekly template — `dayOfWeek`+`startTime` "HH:mm" string, same convention as `Branch.operatingHours`), `ClassSession` (the generated, dated, concrete occurrence — `@@unique([groupClassId, sessionDate, startTime])` makes generation idempotent), `ClassBooking` (`@@unique([classSessionId, memberId])` — one booking row per member per session, ever; see the rebooking gotcha below). New permissions `classes:view/create/update/delete/restore` + `bookings:manage` (MANAGER: all; TRAINER: `classes:view`; RECEPTIONIST: `classes:view`+`bookings:manage`) — the plan-feature flag reused the pre-existing `live_classes` "coming soon" catalog key (relabeled "Class & Session Booking", flipped `true` for Professional+), same activate-in-place pattern Prompt 35 used for `ai_coach`, rather than minting a new flag key.

**Session generation**: `ClassSessionService#generateUpcomingSessions(daysAhead=28)` expands every active class's weekly schedule into dated `ClassSession` rows for a rolling 4-week window, checking `findExisting()` before each insert (idempotent — safe to re-run, backed by the schema's own unique constraint as a safety net). Wired into the existing BullMQ `modules/scheduler/` `JOB_REGISTRY` as a new `class-session-generation` job (category `CLASS` — a new `JobCategory` enum value, `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` migration; `queueNameForCategory`/`scheduler-engine.service.ts` needed zero changes since queue setup is fully registry-driven), daily at 02:00 UTC, scanning every tenant `notIn: ['SUSPENDED','CANCELLED']` — **not** `status: 'ACTIVE'` only, a bug caught during live verification (the reference tenant is `TRIAL`, and an `ACTIVE`-only filter silently skipped every trial tenant's session generation; fixed before this shipped). Also callable on-demand (`POST /class-sessions/generate`, `classes:update`) for immediate feedback after creating/editing a class — same handler function either way.

**Booking**: one `ClassBookingService#book(sessionId, memberId, actor: {role: 'STAFF'|'MEMBER'})` method called identically from the staff controller (`POST /bookings`) and the member-portal service (`POST /portal/classes/:id/book`) — avoids duplicating capacity-check logic, and both booking paths count toward the exact same `capacity` number (verified live). Capacity is count-then-insert, not a locked transaction — an accepted small race window on the literal last seat, documented in both the schema comment and the service, matching this codebase's existing pragmatic-over-perfect precedent (e.g. Prompt 37's report-export threading tradeoffs). **Real bug found + fixed during Playwright verification**: `@@unique([classSessionId, memberId])` means a plain `create()` on rebook fails with an unhandled Prisma unique-violation (surfaced as a raw 500) the moment a member cancels then tries to book the *same* session again — a completely ordinary flow (change your mind, then change it back). Fixed with `ClassBookingRepository#upsertBooking` (upsert on the compound unique key — revives the cancelled row back to `BOOKED` instead of inserting a new one), the same "supersede via status transition, not a fresh row" spirit as `MemberWorkoutPlan`/`Membership`'s history pattern elsewhere in this codebase. **Also found + fixed**: all three new route files (`group-class.routes.ts`/`class-session.routes.ts`/`class-booking.routes.ts`) were missing `router.use(authenticateMiddleware)` — every module's routes file needs this explicitly (it's not applied globally), and its absence meant `requirePermission` alone produced a bare "Authentication required" 401 with no route actually reachable; caught immediately by live curl testing, not by typecheck/lint/build/vitest (none of which can catch a missing middleware registration).

**Frontend (staff)**: new `features/classes/` (types/service/hooks mirroring `features/workouts/`), `app/(portal)/classes/*` — list (`DataTable`), new/edit (class fields + `ClassScheduleEditor`, a simpler per-day multi-time-slot version of `weekly-schedule-editor.tsx`'s pattern), and `calendar/page.tsx` — **the one genuinely new UI primitive this prompt needed**: a hand-built week-view grid (`features/classes/components/class-calendar.tsx`, `startOfWeekUtc`/`weekDays` exported and reused by both the staff and member-portal pages) built from `Card`/`Badge`/native `Date`/`Intl` — no calendar library exists in this codebase or got added for this. Clicking a session opens a `Dialog` with its full booking list + a `MemberCheckinSearch`-powered "book a member in" flow, both cancel and add wired to the shared `book()`/`cancel()` endpoints. New sidebar entry `Classes` → `/classes`, gated `classes:view` + `live_classes`. **Frontend (member portal)**: `/portal/classes` reuses the exact same `ClassCalendar` component in a simplified mode — click a session, a small dialog shows book/cancel depending on whether the member already holds an active booking for it (computed from `GET /portal/bookings`, not stored on the session row itself).

Verified: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build/vitest(237/237, API) for both apps after every sub-part, not just at the end. Live: real curl walkthrough — class created, weekly schedule set, 28 sessions generated (and a second call correctly produced 0, proving idempotency), the actual BullMQ job triggered end-to-end via the admin scheduler's manual-trigger endpoint (not just the on-demand HTTP path) and confirmed `COMPLETED` with the tenant-scan bug caught and fixed mid-verification, 2 staff bookings filled a capacity-2 session, a 3rd was correctly rejected 409, a cancel freed the slot, a real activated member (Karan Reddy / `MEM-0003`, activation link fetched from Mailpit) self-booked into the freed slot via the member-portal API and the staff-side session view showed both bookings (one `STAFF`, one `MEMBER`) counting toward the same `2/2`. Full Playwright pass against the real running dev stack for both frontends: staff classes list/calendar/booking-dialog render correctly with real data and zero console errors; member portal classes calendar renders, and a full book→cancel→rebook→cancel cycle was driven through the real UI (not just curl) — this is what caught the rebooking-unique-constraint bug, since the UI naturally exercises cancel-then-rebook in a way a one-shot curl script hadn't.

40 — **File-upload MIME/magic-byte validation** (audit remediation, first Medium-priority item — the two remaining Highs closed in Prompt 39, working through Medium next). Closes a real gap: every upload validation before this prompt trusted the client-asserted `data:<mime>;base64,` prefix on a data-URL — nothing checked that the bytes after the comma actually matched. A renamed executable claiming `image/jpeg` would have sailed through unchanged.

**`core/storage/file-signature.util.ts`** (new): `sniffFileType(buffer)` checks real magic bytes — JPEG (`FF D8 FF`), PNG (`89 50 4E 47...`), GIF (`GIF8`), WEBP (`RIFF`…`WEBP`), PDF (`%PDF-`) — returns the real type or `null`. No SVG signature exists on purpose: SVG is XML text with no fixed binary header, so a magic-byte check can't tell a safe image from one carrying an embedded `<script>` — **SVG support was removed entirely** (not half-validated) from the two upload fields that allowed it (exercise images, all branding assets).

**`core/storage/storage.service.ts#uploadDataUrl`**: now runs `verifyFileType()` — sniffs the buffer, rejects anything not in the allowlist (`ValidationError`, 422) — **before** the `!env.storage.isConfigured` degraded-mode early return, so the check applies even on a dev machine without MinIO running (content-safety, not an object-storage concern). The sniffed type, never the client-claimed one, becomes the real `ContentType` sent to S3 and picks the file extension — this is what actually defeats MIME spoofing, not just the presence of a check. New `accept?: ('image'|'pdf')[]` option (default `['image']`) — the two upload paths that genuinely need PDFs (member documents, expense receipts) opt in explicitly; every other call site (avatars ×3, member photo, exercise images, branding ×5, QR codes) needed no changes since images-only is already the default.

Verified: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build/vitest(248/248, API — 11 new tests in `file-signature.util.spec.ts`, including a case that specifically constructs an MZ/PE-header buffer wearing an `image/jpeg` label to prove the exact spoofing attack this closes is actually caught). Live against the running dev stack: a real JPEG uploaded as an avatar succeeded and produced a genuine object-storage URL; the identical request with the bytes replaced by plain text (same `image/jpeg` data-URL prefix) was correctly rejected 422 ("This file does not look like a supported image or PDF"); a real PDF uploaded as a member document succeeded (private, presigned URL returned); the same real PDF submitted to an images-only endpoint (profile avatar) was correctly rejected 422 ("This upload only accepts image files") — confirming both the type-sniffing AND the per-endpoint category allowlist work independently. Test document deleted afterward via direct SQL, same cleanup precedent as every prior prompt.

41 — **CSP configuration + user-content sanitization** (audit remediation, second Medium-priority item). Two related gaps closed together: no app had a real, tailored Content-Security-Policy (the API's `helmet()` used its generic default in production and was fully OFF in dev/test; neither Next.js app set any security headers at all), and the one place user-authored rich-text HTML gets rendered raw (`dangerouslySetInnerHTML` on `/announcements`) had zero sanitization anywhere in its write path.

**API** (`apps/api/src/app.ts`): the app-wide `helmet()` CSP is now fully explicit and maximally locked down (`default-src`/`script-src`/`style-src`/`img-src`/`font-src`/`connect-src`/`form-action`/`frame-ancestors`/`object-src`/`base-uri`, all `'none'`) — every directive set explicitly rather than left to helmet's own less-strict defaults (which would otherwise quietly permit e.g. `img-src 'self' data:`), and enabled in every environment now, not just production (a JSON API response is never affected by CSP either way, so there's no dev-mode cost). `/api/docs` (Swagger UI) gets its own second, more permissive `helmet()` call scoped to just that path — Swagger's bundled UI genuinely needs inline script/style — mounted at the SAME point in the middleware chain the docs route already occupied, so CORS/compression/logging/etc. for that route are unaffected; a second `helmet()` call's `res.setHeader` genuinely replaces the app-wide CSP header for matching requests (passing `contentSecurityPolicy: false` instead would NOT have worked — a disabled directive just skips setting the header rather than clearing one an earlier middleware already set).

**Frontend** (`next.config.mjs`, both apps): added a real `headers()` CSP + `X-Content-Type-Options`/`Referrer-Policy`/`Permissions-Policy` for every route. Deliberately `'unsafe-inline'` for both `script-src` and `style-src` rather than a nonce-based rollout — tenant-web paints per-tenant branding as an inline `style` ATTRIBUTE on `<body>` (`app/layout.tsx`), and CSP nonces only ever cover `<style>`/`<script>` ELEMENTS, never inline attributes, so `style-src` needs `'unsafe-inline'` regardless of how strict `script-src` gets, which removes most of the value a full nonce rollout would buy here. `'unsafe-eval'` is dev-only (`next dev`'s Fast Refresh/HMR genuinely breaks without it). `img-src` allows any `https:` origin (images can't execute script, and every upload is now magic-byte-verified server-side per Prompt 40) rather than requiring exact per-deployment allow-listing. tenant-web's `Permissions-Policy` grants `camera=(self)` for the attendance QR scanner; super-admin denies it outright (unused there). **Real bug caught only by live Playwright testing, not by inspection**: `connect-src 'self' <api-origin>` alone silently blocked every Socket.IO WebSocket connection (`features/realtime/socket-client.ts`) — a browser's CSP does NOT treat an `http://host` `connect-src` entry as covering a `ws://host` upgrade to that same host. Fixed by deriving and adding the matching `ws(s)://` origin alongside the `http(s)://` one.

**Sanitization** (`core/security/html-sanitizer.util.ts`, new): `sanitizeRichText()` wraps `sanitize-html` (new dependency) with an allowlist matching exactly what tenant-web's `RichTextEditor` toolbar (bold/italic/underline/list/link only, via `document.execCommand`) can ever actually produce — everything else (scripts, event handlers, iframes, style/class attributes, `javascript:` links) is stripped outright, not escaped-and-displayed. Wired into `TenantAnnouncementService#create`/`#update` (`modules/tenant-announcements/`) — the one and only write path for `body`, so the render side (`dangerouslySetInnerHTML` on `/announcements`) can trust the database unconditionally rather than needing a second sanitization pass at render time. No client-side sanitizer was added — sanitizing once, authoritatively, at the single write funnel is the same "one funnel point" discipline `uploadDataUrl`/`verifyFileType` (Prompt 40) already established for uploads.

Verified: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build (all 3 apps)/vitest(255/255, API — 7 new tests in `html-sanitizer.util.spec.ts`, including cases that specifically construct a `<script>` tag, an `onclick` handler, a `javascript:` href, and an `<iframe>` to prove each is actually stripped, not just theoretically covered). Live: a real announcement created with an embedded `<script>alert(document.cookie)</script>` and an `onerror` image handler came back from the API with body reduced to clean `<p>Hello <b>staff</b></p>` — confirmed via direct API response inspection, not just a unit test. Full Playwright pass against the real running dev stack for all three apps (tenant-web login→dashboard→members with a real staff session, super-admin login, and the API's own Swagger UI) confirmed the CSP header is present everywhere and — critically — that zero CSP violations appear in the browser console during real usage, not just that the header exists; this is what caught the Socket.IO `ws:` gap above, which a header-presence check alone would have missed entirely. Test announcement and its embedded payload deleted afterward via direct SQL, same cleanup precedent as every prior prompt.

42 — **Mandatory 2FA toggle for staff roles** (audit remediation, third Medium-priority item). Research before building revealed the audit item's premise was wrong: 2FA wasn't a working opt-in feature needing an enforcement toggle — `User.mfaEnabled`/`mfaSecret` were dead columns nothing ever wrote to, the login-time `if (user.mfaEnabled)` branch was unreachable in practice, and the existing `/two-factor` challenge screen's "enter the code from your authenticator app" copy was aspirational — the backend actually emailed a 6-digit code. This prompt built REAL TOTP from scratch (user's choice over keeping the weaker email-OTP path) and the mandatory-role policy on top of it.

**TOTP core** (`core/security/totp.util.ts`, new dependency `otplib@13` — note its v13 API is a full break from the classic `authenticator` singleton: standalone `generateSecret`/`generateURI` (sync) and `generate`/`verify`/`generateSync`/`verifySync` functions instead): `generateTotpSecret()`, `buildOtpAuthUri()`, `verifyTotpCode()`, `generateBackupCodes()` (10 codes, `XXXX-XXXXXX` shape, excludes visually-ambiguous characters), `normalizeBackupCode()`. Secrets are encrypted at rest via the EXISTING `core/security/encryption.util.ts` (AES-256-GCM, previously only used for tenant AI API keys) — reused, not a second scheme.

**Schema**: `User.mfaEnabled`/`mfaSecret` repurposed to their originally-intended meaning (finally written to). New `MfaBackupCode` (hashed, one-time-use) and `MfaSetupToken` (short-lived, 15-min grace token — see below) tables, both RLS'd. New `TenantSettings.mfaRequiredRoles: Json` (array of role NAMES, e.g. `["OWNER","MANAGER"]`) — a tenant-wide POLICY LIST rather than a per-`Role` boolean column, specifically because system roles (OWNER/MANAGER/TRAINER/RECEPTIONIST) are immutable in this codebase's IAM model (clone-to-edit only) — a policy list on `TenantSettings` sidesteps needing to clone a system role just to flag it. Migrations `20260805103237_mandatory_2fa_module` + `20260805103300_enable_rls_mandatory_2fa`.

**Login enforcement** (`AuthService#login`): after password verification, before the existing `mfaEnabled` branch, checks whether any of the user's role names intersect `mfaRequiredRoles`. If the role requires 2FA and the user hasn't set it up yet, issues a short-lived opaque `MfaSetupToken` (same hash-and-expire shape as `PasswordReset`) and returns a NEW challenge kind `mfa_setup_required` — proving the password check already passed without granting a real session, scoped only to the `/auth/mfa/setup/*` endpoints. Completing setup with a real TOTP code both enables 2FA AND finishes login in one request (`completeMandatorySetup`), auto-signing the user in rather than making them log in twice. If `mfaEnabled` is already true, the existing `otp_required`/`2fa` challenge now verifies a real TOTP code (or a backup code as fallback) instead of an emailed one — `resendOtp` for purpose `2fa` is now a documented no-op (nothing to resend for a TOTP code).

**Self-service** (`modules/profile/`, new `/profile/two-factor/{setup,setup/confirm,disable,backup-codes/regenerate}` endpoints, all reusing `buildAuthModule`'s `AuthService` rather than duplicating the setup/confirm logic a second time): `beginTwoFactorSetup`/`confirmTwoFactorSetup` share the EXACT same core methods the mandatory grace-flow uses, just resolving `userId` from a normal bearer token instead of a setup token. `disableTwoFactor` requires re-entering the current password (security-sensitive action). Backup codes can be regenerated on demand (requires a current TOTP code).

**Frontend**: unified `/login`'s login-form now branches three ways on the login result (`success` / `otp_required` / `mfa_setup_required`, new `AUTH_ROUTES.mfaSetup` → `/mfa-setup`). New `/mfa-setup` page (`MfaSetupForm`) handles the grace flow: QR code + manual-entry secret fallback + code entry + a one-time backup-codes screen requiring an explicit "I've saved these" confirmation before continuing. `/two-factor` (existing login-challenge screen) gained a "Use a backup code instead" toggle (`OtpInput` is fixed to 6 digits, backup codes are 11 characters, so this switches to a plain text field). New self-service UI: `/settings` gained a "Security" tab (`EnableTwoFactorDialog`/`DisableTwoFactorDialog`/`RegenerateBackupCodesDialog`, same begin/confirm engine as the full-page grace flow, presented as dialogs since the user is already signed in). New `/gym-settings/security` admin page — 4 checkboxes (Owner/Manager/Trainer/Receptionist) for `mfaRequiredRoles`, gated by the EXISTING `settings:manage` permission (no new permission key minted).

**Real bug found + fixed during live Playwright verification, not by inspection**: both the grace-flow (`MfaSetupForm`) and self-service (`EnableTwoFactorDialog`) begin-setup calls were originally fired via a TanStack `useMutation` from a mount/dialog-open `useEffect` — this is a documented anti-pattern in this exact codebase (see the `features/onboarding/success-step.tsx` comment: "under React StrictMode's double-invoked effects the mutation observer was seen never leaving `pending` even though the mutationFn resolved"). It reproduced exactly the same way here: the API call genuinely returned 200 (confirmed via network logging), but the QR code never rendered — the UI spun forever. Fixed the same way the onboarding step already fixes it: a plain `useState` status machine (`{status: 'pending'|'success'|'error'}`) + a direct `authService.beginMfaSetup()`/`beginTwoFactorSetup()` call + a `useRef` once-guard, no `useMutation` involved for the mount-fired call (the user-click-fired `confirm` mutation is unaffected and stays as `useMutation`, since the anti-pattern is specific to mount-effect-fired ones). Confirmed fixed by a full Playwright pass through both flows afterward.

Verified: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build(both apps)/vitest(270/270, API — 9 new tests in the expanded `auth.service.spec.ts`: setup-required challenge triggers/doesn't-trigger correctly by role, the full grace-flow begin→confirm→single-use-token cycle with a REAL computed TOTP code, the existing-2FA login challenge with a real code, backup-code login + single-use consumption, and password-gated disable; 9 new tests in `totp.util.spec.ts` covering real-code acceptance, wrong-code/wrong-secret rejection, and backup-code normalization). Live end-to-end via curl: full grace-flow (policy set → login blocked → QR/secret issued → real TOTP code confirms setup AND completes login → backup codes returned) confirmed with genuinely computed codes (`otplib` in a throwaway Node script, not hand-typed guesses); TOTP login challenge and backup-code login both confirmed, including backup-code single-use rejection on reuse; self-service disable confirmed requires the correct password. Full Playwright pass against the real running dev stack for both the self-service Settings flow and the mandatory grace flow (including the mount-effect bug fix above) — zero console errors after the fix. Test account reset to a clean (no-2FA, no-policy) baseline via direct SQL afterward, same cleanup precedent as every prior prompt's live-data verification.

43 — **Field-level encryption for Member email/phone** (audit remediation, fourth Medium-priority item). Confirmed the search/lookup trade-off with the user before building (see the two options weighed below) rather than assuming: AES-256-GCM's random IV per encryption call makes ciphertext non-deterministic, so a `contains`/equality SQL filter against the ciphertext column can never match — some mechanism was needed for uniqueness checks and exact lookups to keep working.

**Chosen design — blind-index (deterministic HMAC) for exact match, partial search dropped**: `Member.email`/`Member.phone` now store AES-256-GCM ciphertext (`core/security/encryption.util.ts`, the SAME scheme already used for `TenantAiSettings.apiKeyEncrypted`/`User.mfaSecret` — no new encryption scheme invented) alongside new `emailHash`/`phoneHash` columns — a deterministic HMAC-SHA256 blind index (`core/security/blind-index.util.ts`, new: derives its own subkey from `ENCRYPTION_KEY` via a fixed-context HMAC rather than reusing the raw AES key for a second purpose or requiring a second env var). `MemberRepository#findByEmail`/`findByPhone` (uniqueness checks) now query by hash equality instead of plaintext equality. The member-list free-text search box (`buildWhere`'s `contains` OR) had email/phone REMOVED — only an EXACT email/phone still resolves (via the hash), partial/substring matching on those two fields is gone; name/Member ID search is unaffected. Frontend search placeholder updated (`Search name, email, phone, member ID…` → `Search name or member ID…`) to stop advertising a capability that no longer exists.

**Single write funnel, but many read call sites**: encryption/hashing happens in exactly one place — `MemberRepository#create`/`update` (via a new `encryptContact()` helper) — since nothing outside `member.repository.ts` ever writes to the `members` table directly (confirmed by inspection). Decryption is harder to centralize: `MemberRepository`'s own read methods (`list`/`findDetail`/`findByQrToken`/`listForExport`) decrypt at the repository boundary, so `member.service.ts` and everything downstream (DTOs, CSV export, notify calls) needed ZERO changes and see plaintext exactly as before. But ~10 OTHER files fetch a `Member` row directly (bypassing `MemberRepository`) or pull one in via a relational `include: { member: true }` on a DIFFERENT model (`MemberInvoice`/`MemberPayment`/`Membership`) — `finance/services/member-payment.service.ts` (5 sites), `member-invoice.service.ts`, `member-auth/services/member-auth.service.ts` (portal login + forgot-password), and 3 of the cross-tenant scheduler jobs (`scheduler/handlers/{payment,membership,notification}.handlers.ts`, which use the RAW un-tenant-scoped `prisma` client since they sweep across every tenant). Each of these now explicitly decrypts via `modules/members/utils/member-pii.util.ts`'s `decryptMemberContact`/`decryptMemberContactMany`/`decryptMemberContactNullable` immediately after the fetch — found by grepping every `db.member.`/`prisma.member.`/`include: { member: true }` call site in the codebase and checking which ones actually touched `.email`/`.phone`, not by guessing. A Prisma Client Extension (transparent encrypt/decrypt on every query) was considered and rejected — this codebase's established style is explicit funnels (see how `mfaSecret`/`apiKeyEncrypted` are handled), and composing an encryption extension with the existing tenant-scoping RLS extension's `$transaction`-based `set_config` pattern introduced correctness questions that weren't worth the risk for a codebase this size.

**Migration**: `20260805132738_member_pii_encryption` (schema — widened `email`/`phone` from `@db.VarChar` to unbounded `text`, since ciphertext runs longer than the plaintext it replaces; added `emailHash`/`phoneHash` + `@@index([tenantId, emailHash|phoneHash])`). One-off data backfill (`apps/api/scripts/backfill-member-pii.ts`, run once via `tsx`) encrypted all 20 pre-existing plaintext rows in the dev DB — idempotent by construction (only touches rows where the hash column is still NULL), so it's safe to re-run against a fresh environment that already has some encrypted rows.

Verified: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build/vitest (278/278 — 8 new tests in `member-pii.util.spec.ts`: encrypt/decrypt round-trip, ciphertext non-determinism, hash determinism, hash format). Live via curl/psql against the real dev DB: created a member with email+phone, confirmed the DB row is genuine ciphertext (`psql` showed the `{iv}:{tag}:{ciphertext}` shape, not plaintext) while the API response and CSV export both show correct plaintext; duplicate email (different case/whitespace) and duplicate phone both correctly rejected 409 via the hash lookup; name search still finds the member, searching by the member's own full email now correctly returns zero results (the accepted trade-off); member-portal login (`member-auth.service.ts`) returns a correctly decrypted email in the profile DTO. Confirmed all pre-existing 20 members' rows show `email_hash`/`phone_hash` populated post-backfill.

44 — **Global search** (audit remediation, fifth Medium-priority item). The header already had a `GlobalSearch` component (`components/layout/header.tsx`) with a code comment saying exactly this was deferred: *"a real cross-entity search (members, staff, payments…) needs those modules' data first; this keeps the header's search slot functional and future-ready."* Before this prompt it only filtered the sidebar's own nav-item labels client-side — no API call at all. This prompt built the real thing on top of that existing stub rather than replacing it.

**Scope, a deliberate judgment call**: Members, Staff, and Branches only — the three "look someone/somewhere up by name" entities — not every searchable list in the app (Payments/Invoices/Workout Plans/etc. already have their own in-page search and are too granular for a global quick-jump). New `GET /search?q=` (`modules/search/`) returns `{members, staff, branches}`, each up to 5 items, by re-using each module's OWN repository `list()` method with the query as its existing `search` param — a result found here always matches what that module's own list-page search would find, no parallel query logic invented.

**Permission-filtered per category, not gated by `requirePermission`**: `requirePermission` only expresses a single required key, and a search bar spanning three independently-permissioned entity types needs "include this category only if the caller can view it," not an all-or-nothing 403. The route is authenticated-only; the service checks `members:view`/`staff:view`/`branches:view` itself via one `permissionEngine.getEffectivePermissions()` call (already Redis-cached) and only queries categories the caller can see — no new permission keys needed.

**Frontend**: `GlobalSearch` now shows a "Pages" group (the pre-existing instant nav-label filter, unchanged) alongside "Members"/"Staff"/"Branches" groups from the new endpoint (debounced 300ms via the existing `useDebouncedValue` hook, min 2 characters before it fires — a 1-character query is valid at the API level but the service returns empty rather than a noisy full-table-ish result). Clicking a result navigates straight to that record's detail page and clears the search box.

Verified: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build (both apps) clean. Live via curl against the real dev API: searching "Karan" correctly returned the seeded member (`MEM-0003`) with a working `/members/:id` URL; a 1-character query correctly returned all-empty rather than erroring. **Known gap this session**: no Playwright/browser tool was available in this environment, so the dropdown's actual click-through interaction (open the search box, type, see the grouped results render, click one, land on the right page) was NOT verified in a real browser — only via API-level curl checks, a clean production build, and code review. If you touch `global-search.tsx` next, do a real Playwright pass first to establish a behavioral baseline before changing it.

45 — **Real DB/Redis-checking `/health` endpoint** (audit remediation, sixth Medium-priority item). `app.ts` already had a bare `GET /health` (mounted before `tenantMiddleware`, correctly platform-exempt) — but it was a pure liveness no-op (`{status:'ok', uptimeSeconds}`), never actually checking anything. Found and fixed live during this exact session: a genuine Docker restart left Postgres briefly unreachable, and the old `/health` would have reported `200 ok` the entire time — the precise failure mode this item exists to close.

New `core/health/health-check.util.ts#checkHealth()`: a real `SELECT 1` against Postgres (via the raw `prisma` client, not tenant-scoped — this route runs before any tenant is known) and a real `PING` against the shared `redis` (ioredis) client, run in parallel, each independently timed. Three-way status: `healthy` (both up), `degraded` (DB up, Redis down — sessions/rate-limiting/caching misbehave but most traffic still serves, so this stays **200**), `unhealthy` (DB down — nothing works without it, **503** so a load balancer/orchestrator actually stops routing traffic here). `sendError` (`core/http/response.ts`) gained an optional 5th `data` param (previously hardcoded `null`) specifically so the unhealthy response could still carry the full `checks` payload — a health check's failure response is exactly when the diagnostic detail matters most.

Verified: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build/vitest (278/278, unaffected — no existing spec touched this route). Live: `GET /health` returns `200 healthy` with real millisecond latencies for both checks; `docker stop gym-saas-postgres` then re-querying correctly returned `503 unhealthy` with `database.status: down` (`redis.status` still correctly reported `up`); restarting Postgres and re-querying correctly recovered to `200 healthy`.

46 — **GDPR export/erasure for Members** (audit remediation, seventh Medium-priority item). Scoped to Members specifically (not Staff/Users) — the Member is this app's actual "data subject" population, and this pass builds directly on Prompt 43's field-level PII encryption work.

**Export** (`GET /members/:id/gdpr-export`, staff, `members:view`; `GET /portal/gdpr-export`, member self-service, own id only): a single bundle of everything genuinely personal — profile (decrypted email/phone + medical/emergency-contact fields), membership history, freeze history, attendance, workout/diet plan assignments (+ progress/log COUNTS, not the full per-entry detail — kept deliberately summary-level), invoices, payments, document metadata (type/filename/upload date, not file contents), and class bookings. Both entry points call the SAME `MemberGdprService#exportData` (new, standalone service, not folded into `MemberService`) — the "one core engine, two entry points" shape already established for mandatory 2FA.

**Erasure** (`POST /members/:id/gdpr-erase`, staff-only, new permission `members:erase-data` — granted to OWNER/MANAGER, deliberately NOT to TRAINER/RECEPTIONIST, matching how `members:delete`/`restore` are already Manager-tier-only; **no self-service erasure** — a member can't erase their own account, only request it through staff, a deliberate scope-narrowing to avoid a coerced/accidental-click irreversible action with no human review) **anonymizes rather than hard-deletes**: name/email/phone/DOB/address/medical/emergency-contact/notes fields on the `Member` row are overwritten (`firstName: 'Erased'`, contact fields `null`, `notes` replaced with an erasure marker), portal access is fully revoked (`MemberCredential`/`MemberSession`/`MemberVerification` rows hard-deleted), uploaded `MemberDocument` rows are hard-deleted, and the member is soft-deleted (`deletedAt` + `INACTIVE`). Financial rows (invoices/payments/memberships) are DELIBERATELY left in place — `onDelete: Restrict` FKs from `MemberInvoice`/`MemberPayment` to `Member` would block a hard delete anyway, and accounting/tax records typically need retention regardless of an erasure request; once the source `Member` row carries no identifying info, those rows carry none either. Every erasure is audit-logged (`member.gdpr_erased`).

**Known limitation, documented in code**: `MemberDocument` DB rows are deleted but the underlying object-storage files are NOT — `core/storage/storage.service.ts` has no delete-object capability anywhere in this codebase yet (upload/presign only). A future pass adding real S3/R2 object deletion should wire it into `MemberGdprService#eraseData`.

Verified: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build (both apps)/vitest (278/278, unaffected). Re-ran `prisma:seed` for the new `members:erase-data` permission key + MANAGER grant, confirmed via direct SQL that only SUPER_ADMIN/OWNER/MANAGER have it (TRAINER/RECEPTIONIST correctly don't). Live via curl against the real dev API: created a throwaway member with email/phone/emergency-contact/medical fields, exported it (correct plaintext bundle), erased it, confirmed via `psql` the row now shows `Erased`/`Member`/empty email·phone·hash·emergency-contact·medical fields/`INACTIVE`/`deleted_at` set, confirmed the audit-log row exists, confirmed a re-export after erasure still works and correctly returns the now-anonymized data (not an error), and confirmed the member-portal self-service export endpoint returns a real member's own correctly-decrypted bundle.

47 — **Super Admin QA sweep — 3 confirmed cache/session-invalidation bugs, fixed** (not a new feature — a full functional QA pass through the Super Admin portal at the user's request, exercising tenant CRUD, subscription plan CRUD, tenant plan change, and admin role/permission management end-to-end live, followed by fixes for everything it found). Full methodology and complete list of what was tested vs. not tested is in the QA report given to the user mid-session, not reproduced here — this entry covers only the 3 confirmed defects and their fixes.

**Bug 1 — suspending/deleting a tenant didn't take effect for up to 5 minutes.** `tenantMiddleware` resolves every request through a 5-minute Redis cache-aside read (`tenantService.resolveBySlug`); `AdminTenantService#setStatus`/`softDelete` wrote the DB but never called `tenantService.invalidateCache()` — confirmed live: a just-suspended tenant's owner could still log in and use the portal. **Fix**: both methods now call `tenantService.invalidateCache(tenant.slug, tenantId)` immediately after their DB write.

**Bug 2 — changing a tenant's plan (upgrade/downgrade/assign) had no functional effect.** `AdminTenantBillingService#applyPlanChange` updated the `Subscription` row but never touched `TenantLimit` (max branches/managers/trainers/receptionists/staff/members/storage — written ONCE at provisioning and never again anywhere else in the codebase, confirmed by grep) or `TenantModule` (the per-tenant feature-flag table read as `ResolvedTenant.featureFlags`), and never invalidated the same tenant-resolve cache as Bug 1. Net effect: a plan change only ever touched billing records — actual enforced caps and gated UI/modules stayed on whatever plan the tenant was provisioned with. **Fix**: `applyPlanChange` now also updates `TenantLimit` from the target plan's limits and upserts every `TenantModule` row from the target plan's `features` list (mirrors `tenant-provisioning.service.ts`'s own logic, applied as an update instead of a create), then invalidates the cache. Verified live both directions (Enterprise → Starter → Enterprise): limits and `featureFlags` now change immediately and correctly on every plan change.

**Bug 3 — suspending an admin account didn't revoke their already-issued session.** `adminAuthenticateMiddleware` only verified the JWT's signature/expiry, never the admin's live status — `AdminRoleService#setStatus` correctly blocked new logins and refreshes (`refreshTokens()` already re-checked `status === 'ACTIVE'`), but a still-unexpired access token (15-min TTL) kept working on ordinary requests. **Fix**: `adminAuthenticateMiddleware` now does a cache-aside live-status check on every request (`admin-status:{adminUserId}`, 30-min TTL, same pattern as `requireAdminPermission`'s existing permission cache) and rejects with `ACCOUNT_SUSPENDED` if not `ACTIVE`; `AdminRoleService#setStatus` deletes that cache key immediately on any status change, so the very next request — not the next login — sees it. Verified live: an admin's session that returned 200 immediately before a suspend returned 401 `ACCOUNT_SUSPENDED` immediately after, using the exact same still-valid token.

All three fixes share one root cause: admin-tenant/admin-role mutations write to Postgres but skip the Redis cache layers other request paths read through — **any new admin action that changes tenant status/plan/limits or admin status must invalidate the matching cache in the same request**, or the action will silently not take effect until that cache's TTL expires.

Verified: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build/vitest (278/278, unaffected — no existing spec touches these three code paths). All three bugs reproduced live BEFORE the fix, then re-verified live AFTER the fix using the identical repro steps (same tenant, same still-valid tokens where applicable), confirming the fix and not just the absence of an error. Confirmed normal (non-suspended) admin access is unaffected by the new status check. Test tenant/admin/role artifacts created during the QA sweep were cleaned up (direct SQL, since admin roles/admin-user hard-delete has no API — noted as a minor tooling gap, not fixed this pass).

48 — **Tenant portal QA sweep — branch-access enforcement added to Members/Finance** (not a new feature — a full functional QA pass through the tenant portal as Owner/Manager/Trainer/Receptionist, at the user's request, followed by a fix for the one real gap it found and deep-testing of everything else). Full test matrix and pass/fail results were given to the user directly mid-session; this entry covers the fix and its architecture.

**The gap**: Members and Finance (payments/invoices/income/expenses) had NO branch-access enforcement at all, unlike Attendance which already correctly used `requireBranchAccess`. Confirmed live: a Receptionist scoped to one branch could freely view/edit a member — and record a payment for them — at a completely different branch they had no assigned access to. Root cause: `requireBranchAccess` (the existing middleware) only validates a `branchId` present directly in the request (params/query/body) — it's a no-op for `GET/PATCH/DELETE /members/:id`-style routes, which carry no `branchId` at all (the branch is only knowable by fetching the record). Members/Finance never had an equivalent entity-level check.

**The fix — new `assertBranchAccess(tenantId, userId, branchId)`** (`authentication/middlewares/branch-access.middleware.ts`, alongside the existing `getBranchAccess`): an entity-scoped check services call directly after fetching a record, rather than a route-level request-param check. Applied to `MemberService`, `MemberPaymentService`, `MemberInvoiceService`, `IncomeService`, `ExpenseService`:
- Every method that fetches a single record by ID now goes through that module's `mustFind(id, actorUserId)` choke point, which checks the actor's access against the record's OWN `branchId` after fetching it (not before — the branch isn't known until the record is read).
- Every `create()` now checks access against the resolved target `branchId` before writing (Members: `input.branchId`; Payments: `input.branchId ?? member.branchId`; Income/Expense: `input.branchId`, skipped entirely when absent since those two have OPTIONAL branchId for tenant-wide entries).
- Every `list()`/`exportCsv()`/`exportExcel()` now auto-restricts results to the actor's own `branchIds` when they're not `allBranches` — even with no explicit `branchId` filter in the request, an actor no longer sees other branches' data by omission. An explicit filter for a branch outside the actor's scope is intersected down to zero rows, not silently ignored. Income/Expense (nullable branch) treat tenant-wide `NULL`-branch entries as visible to everyone, not just `allBranches` staff.
- **Two genuine self-service exceptions, NOT bugs**: `MemberService#getOwnProfile` (member-portal's own "view my profile") and `MemberInvoiceService#listOwn`/`getOwnById`/`renderOwnPdf` (member-portal's own invoices) intentionally skip the check — a `Member` isn't a staff `User` at all (no `UserBranches` row to check against), and the member-portal already scopes every query to the caller's own `memberId`, a strictly narrower guarantee than "same branch as this actor."

**Known follow-up, not fixed this pass**: `MemberService#create`'s branch-existence check (`assertBranchExists`) was already there; the NEW access check on top of it means a branch-restricted Manager can no longer create a member (or record income/a payment) at a branch they don't work at — this is the correct behavior per the fix's intent, but is a real BEHAVIOR CHANGE from before (previously unrestricted) worth knowing if a real tenant hits it.

Verified: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build/vitest (278/278, unaffected). Live via curl, reproducing the EXACT originally-broken scenario before and after the fix: a Bandra-only Receptionist's `GET`/`PATCH` of a Main-Branch member and a payment-creation attempt for that member all correctly flipped from `200`/success to `403 FORBIDDEN`; same-branch access, `allBranches` (Owner) access, and tenant-wide (null-branch) Income/Expense visibility were all separately confirmed still working correctly, not just "everything now blocked."

**Everything else tested deeply, all confirmed working correctly, no other defects found**: Diet plans (create → assign → log water/weight/meal progress → confirmed reflecting), Invoices (generate → list → real binary PDF download → email delivery confirmed in Mailpit), Income/Expenses (full create/update/list/export/soft-delete cycle), Reports & Analytics (named reports + real CSV/Excel/PDF binary exports, confirmed via `file`), Notifications (template update + manual send + unread-count, all reflecting correctly), Announcements (create → publish → confirmed in the PUBLISHED-filtered list; schedule → correct SCHEDULED status — note `/announcements/active` is a DIFFERENT, unrelated pre-existing platform-plane endpoint, not where tenant-authored announcements surface, a one-time test-methodology confusion, not a bug), Support tickets (tenant creates → admin sees and replies → tenant sees the reply; a separate INTERNAL note stays correctly invisible to the tenant while admin sees both).

49 — **Onboarding checklist dashboard widget** (audit remediation, final Low-priority item). No prior spec existed for this beyond the backlog label — scoped it with the user before building: a "get your gym set up" card on the dashboard, Owner/Manager only, tracking first-run setup tasks auto-detected from real data (not manual checkboxes), auto-hiding once complete or explicitly dismissed.

**Items deliberately exclude anything already true immediately after signup** — a default `Branch` and the Owner's own `User` both already exist from provisioning (`tenant-provisioning.service.ts`), so "add a branch"/"create an account" would be trivially complete for every tenant on day one and aren't useful nudges. The 5 real items: customize branding (`TenantBranding.logoUrl` set), complete gym profile (`TenantProfile.addressLine` set), invite staff (any non-owner staff `User` exists), create a membership plan (`MembershipPlan` count > 0), add a member (`Member` count > 0) — each links straight to the relevant page.

**Backend** (`modules/onboarding-checklist/`, new — routes→controller→service, no repository layer, mirrors `dashboard.service.ts`'s "aggregate across many tables directly via `this.db`" shape): `GET /onboarding-checklist` (computes all 5 items + dismissed flag in one `Promise.all`), `POST /onboarding-checklist/dismiss`. Both gated on `settings:read` (existing permission, no new key) — every item is something only Owner/Manager can act on. New `TenantSettings.onboardingChecklistDismissed Boolean` — confirmed via `toResolvedTenant()` that this field is NOT part of the cached `ResolvedTenant`, so unlike branding/business-settings writes, dismissing does NOT need `tenantService.invalidateCache`.

**Real bug found and fixed along the way, unrelated to the checklist logic itself**: `GET /onboarding-checklist` initially failed with a confusing "Token does not match the current tenant" error despite a valid, correctly-issued token. Root cause: `tenant.middleware.ts`'s `PLATFORM_ROUTE_PREFIXES`/`EXPIRY_EXEMPT_PREFIXES` matching used a bare `path.startsWith(prefix)` with no path-segment awareness — `/api/v1/onboarding-checklist` starts with the string `/api/v1/onboarding` (the pre-existing signup-wizard's platform-exempt prefix), so the new route was silently misclassified as a tenant-agnostic platform route, `req.tenant` ended up wrong, and the JWT's real `tenantId` no longer matched it. This is a genuine latent bug that would bite ANY future route sharing a string prefix with `/auth`, `/subscription`, `/billing`, `/payment`, `/coupon`, `/invoice`, `/onboarding`, `/public`, `/webhook`, or `/admin` — fixed at the root (new `matchesPrefix()` helper requiring an exact match or a `/` boundary) rather than just renaming this one route, since renaming would only hide the symptom for future routes too. Re-verified live that every existing platform-exempt route (`/onboarding/register`, `/auth/register`, `/admin/auth/login`, `/public/tenants/resolve`) and the billing-expiry-exempt path still behave identically after the fix.

**Frontend**: `features/onboarding-checklist/` (types/service/hook/component, exact same shape as `features/dashboard/`'s `announcement.service.ts`/`use-announcements.ts`/`announcements-widget.tsx`). `OnboardingChecklistCard` slots into `(portal)/dashboard/page.tsx` right after the welcome banner, before `StatsGrid` — renders `null` (not a loading skeleton) for anyone without `settings:read`, once loaded-and-dismissed, or once every item is complete, so it never shows a flash of an empty/irrelevant widget to Trainer/Receptionist or a fully-set-up tenant.

Verified: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build (both apps)/vitest (278/278) all clean. Live via curl: confirmed the tenant-middleware fix directly (the exact repro before/after); confirmed a heavily-used long-running test tenant correctly shows 5/5 complete (every item genuinely done by this point in the session); confirmed dismiss persists; confirmed Trainer gets a clean 403 (not shown the widget). Test tenant's dismissed flag reset to `false` afterward to leave a clean baseline.

**Remaining audit items** (Medium): Redis caching for dashboard/report aggregates, push notifications. **Remaining audit items** (Low): explicit Postgres connection-pool sizing. All original Low-priority items are now done.

50 — **Missing loading indicator on some button/menu clicks, both apps** (user-reported: "click a button, nothing happens on screen, then the API responds and the UI suddenly changes"). Root cause was NOT the global loading-indicator system itself (`GlobalLoader`/`NavigationProgressProvider`/`pendingRequests`, built in Prompt 33 — see FRONTEND-GUIDE.md, still working correctly for the vast majority of the app: sidebar `<Link>`s, `LoadingButton`-wired mutations covering 510+ call sites). It was a specific, real gap in `NavigationProgressProvider`'s click-capture listener: it only recognizes clicks on a real `<a href>` (needed to fire the loader *before* the App Router's RSC fetch even starts). A `router.push()` triggered from a plain `<button onClick>`, a `role="link"` card/row, or a toast action's `onClick` has no anchor for that listener to catch — it fell through to the *late*-firing `pushState` patch, which doesn't run until the navigation has basically already resolved, so no loader ever appeared. Found and fixed 3 concrete instances per app: tenant-web's `GlobalSearch` result buttons; super-admin's `/plans` card-grid row click + "Back to plans"/"View tenant"/"Back to tickets"/"Back to tenants" buttons; both apps' AI-assistant error-toast "Open AI Settings" action.

**Fix**: new `notifyProgrammaticNavigation(targetUrl?)`, exported from each app's `navigation-progress-provider.tsx` (module-level ref to the current `notifyStart`, kept in sync every render) — gives any non-`<Link>` call site the same early, generation-tracked loader signal a real link click already gets for free. Call it immediately before the `router.push()`. See FRONTEND-GUIDE.md's loading-indicator section for the full write-up and when to reach for it vs. just using a real `<Link>`.

Verified: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build (both apps) all clean. No Playwright available this session — verified via code-level tracing of the click listener/pushState-patch logic plus live route checks (dev servers serving 200 on the touched pages after picking up the change via HMR); did not visually confirm the loader now appears on these specific buttons in a real browser.

51 — **Mobile app, Phase 1** (`apps/mobile`, Flutter): first real code in this workspace — the scaffold's pre-set architecture (`BLoC` · `Dio` · `get_it` · `go_router` · `flutter_secure_storage` · `socket_io_client`/Firebase declared-not-yet-wired, per the root README and pre-existing `pubspec.yaml`) was followed as-is, not redesigned. A companion visual-design pass ran first — a full 81-screen mockup system (Bebas Neue display + Manrope body, cobalt-on-light for Staff surfaces / amber-on-dark for Member surfaces) covering every screen in the mobile build plan, published as a standalone artifact and approved before any Dart was written. **One app, not two**: earlier planning discussion had assumed separate Staff/Member binaries; the pre-existing scaffold (`pubspec.yaml`'s own description: "one Flutter binary serves every tenant") settled it — Member is a future second entry point in this same app, not a second app.

Built this prompt: `core/` (`env.dart` — API base URL via `--dart-define`, defaults to the Android-emulator loopback alias `10.0.2.2`; real devices need `--dart-define=API_BASE_URL=http://<host-LAN-IP>:4000/api/v1`, and the API's `app.ts` already binds `0.0.0.0` so no server-side change was needed), `network/dio_client.dart` (X-Tenant-Slug + Bearer interceptor, single refresh-and-retry on 401 via a bare second `Dio` instance that can't recurse into the interceptor — mirrors tenant-web's `api-client.ts`), `storage/secure_storage.dart` (flutter_secure_storage wrapper), `theme/` (`AppColors`/`AppTheme`/`AppSemanticColors` — two fixed `ThemeData`s, `.staff` light/cobalt and `.member` dark/amber, ported 1:1 from the design's CSS custom properties; Bebas Neue + Manrope bundled as real font assets under `assets/fonts/`, not `google_fonts`, for offline-reliable branding), `bloc/session/` (`SessionCubit` — the one genuinely app-global piece of state, gates `go_router`'s `redirect` via a `GoRouterRefreshStream` adapter over the Cubit's state stream), and four features (`auth`, `dashboard`, `members`, `attendance`), each `data/` (repository + models matching real API response shapes, not guessed — every shape was confirmed live via curl against the running dev API before coding the model) + `presentation/` (Cubits for list/load state, pages). Shared `widgets/`: `AppCard`, `StatusPill`, `KpiTile`, `AppLabeledField` (label-caption-above-a-plain-box, matching the design's `.field` component — deliberately NOT Material's floating-label pattern), `SegmentedToggle` (the Staff/Member switch), `BrandMark` (a `CustomPainter` redrawing the design's exact dumbbell SVG path stroke-for-stroke, instead of substituting a generic Material icon — this one shipped wrong on the first pass, see below). Added `mobile_scanner` (not in the original scaffold's dependency list) for the attendance QR check-in camera view; Android manifest gained `CAMERA`/`INTERNET` permissions.

**A real design-fidelity bug, caught by the user comparing a live screenshot against the approved mockup, not by me**: the login screen was first built with Flutter's default `InputDecoration(labelText:)` (floating label inside the border) and a stock `Icons.fitness_center`, and was missing the Staff/Member segmented toggle that was literally in the very first approved design screen. Fixed by building `AppLabeledField`/`BrandMark`/`SegmentedToggle` as shared components (used by both `TenantResolvePage` and `LoginPage`) rather than patching one screen — the lesson being that "the design was approved" doesn't make it into the app for free; each screen has to be deliberately built against the design's actual component shapes, not Flutter's defaults. `flutter analyze` clean (0 issues) against the scaffold's own stricter-than-default `analysis_options.yaml` (`strict-casts`/`strict-inference`/`strict-raw-types`, `require_trailing_commas`, `sort_pub_dependencies` — all pre-existing, not added this prompt; `strict-inference` is what caught every un-typed `dio.get()`/`dio.post()` call and forced explicit `Dio<Map<String, dynamic>>` generics throughout the repositories).

**Verified live end-to-end, twice** — once on an Android emulator (after real environment friction, see below), once on a real physical device (Samsung Galaxy Tab, Android 16) after fixing the design gap: tenant-resolve (`kaushalgym` → "Kaushal Fitness Studio") → login (`kaushal.chauhan@rajyasabha.digital`) → dashboard rendering **real** KPI numbers matching a direct curl of `GET /reports/dashboard/kpis` exactly (20 active members, ₹100.0K revenue MTD, etc.) → members list rendering real seeded member rows (MEM-0001–0020) → member detail. Attendance QR check-in was built and analyzed/unit-tested against a live-confirmed `/attendance/validate-qr` response shape but not click-tested on-device (camera flow needs a real QR code in frame, not just navigation).

**Environment notes worth knowing for the next mobile prompt**: this dev machine's Android SDK had no accepted licenses and no AVD — both were created this session (`fitcloud_stable`, `system-images;android-34;google_apis;x86_64`; a first attempt, `fitcloud_pixel` on a pre-release 16k-page-size image, proved unstable — ANR'd on its own systemui before the app even installed — and should not be reused). Even the stable AVD struggled under this host's memory pressure (observed as low as ~1GB free of 15.7GB total, causing 20-second frame times and dropped ADB connections) — **a real physical device over USB is the more reliable target on this machine** and was what finally completed verification; `flutter devices` picks it up automatically once ADB debugging is authorized on the phone. Physical devices need the real LAN IP in `API_BASE_URL`, not `10.0.2.2` (emulator-only loopback alias) — confirmed live once pointed at `http://192.168.1.36:4000/api/v1` (adjust to whatever `ipconfig` reports for the dev machine's Wi-Fi adapter).

**Not yet built** (rest of the mobile plan — see the design artifact for the full 81-screen reference): Membership Plans, Workouts, Diet, Classes, Finance, Branches, Reports/Analytics, Staff & IAM, Gym Settings, Support, Search on the Staff side; the entire Member entry point (own login-by-Member-ID flow, dashboard, attendance/workout/diet, classes booking, invoices, GDPR export) on the Member side; push notifications (Firebase deps are declared in `pubspec.yaml` but `Firebase.initializeApp()` is never called — no `google-services.json` exists yet, would crash if wired without one); Socket.IO realtime (`socket_io_client` likewise declared, unused).

**Prompt 51 addendum, same session — Dashboard brought to full design fidelity + tablet layout fixed.** Live testing on a physical device (not just the emulator) surfaced two more real gaps the emulator work hadn't caught: (1) the Dashboard was missing the design's revenue-trend sparkline, recent-activity feed, and persistent bottom nav — it had ad-hoc "quick action" cards standing in for navigation instead; (2) the phone-designed layout looked wrong on a tablet. Both fixed:
- **Dashboard** now pulls real data for the two previously-missing cards — `GET /analytics/revenue-trends?dateFrom&dateTo` (last 7 days) for the sparkline, `GET /reports/dashboard/recent-activities` for the feed — via new `RevenueTrendPoint`/`RecentActivity` models and repository methods. New `SparklineChart` widget (`CustomPainter`, faint gridlines + area fill + emphasized endpoint dot, matching the design's SVG exactly) and `AppTopBar` widget (caption-over-Bebas-Neue-title, used on every tab-root screen instead of a native `AppBar`, which the design never uses for these).
- **Bottom nav** — `go_router`'s `StatefulShellRoute.indexedStack` now drives 4 persistent tabs (Home/Members/Scan/Reports) via a new `AppShell` widget; Reports has no module yet so it's a `ComingSoonPage` (mirrors tenant-web's `components/coming-soon.tsx` precedent — a nav destination is never dead, even pre-build).
- **Tablet layout**: first instinct was to cap content at phone width (480dp) and center it — wrong call, reverted after live feedback. The actual fix: no width cap at all (a real phone is narrow regardless, so this is a no-op there), and the Dashboard's KPI `GridView` picks its column count from `MediaQuery.sizeOf(context).width` (2 under 600dp, 3 under 900dp, 4 above) so a tablet's extra width goes to more columns, not stretched tiles or dead-space gutters. If a future screen has the same wide-content problem, follow this pattern, not a max-width cap.

Both fixes verified live on the same physical Android tablet against the real API — trend chart rendering actual payment-spike data, activity feed showing real recent payments/check-ins, 3-column KPI grid filling the tablet's width edge-to-edge.

52 — **Mobile app — 4-role theme system + reusable chart components + Phase 0 & Phase 1 screens** (`apps/mobile`, Flutter): discovered the full visual-design scope was a **4-role theme system** (not 1), each with role-specific accent colors and Member role having an alternate dark-violet ground palette instead of the shared dark ground. Built 5 fully-animated, parameterized chart components (all take an `accent` color parameter, never hardcode role colors): (1) `KpiRing` — circular progress ring with smooth Tween animation from previous→new value; (2) `Sparkline` — line chart with gradient fill underneath and animated path-metrics draw-in + staggered end-dot scale; (3) `DonutChart` — multi-segment ring via CustomPainter drawArc loop with per-segment arc animation; (4) `ComparisonBars` — horizontal bars with staggered Tween width animation across items; (5) `AttendanceHeatmap` — GridView with Color.lerp cells and staggered FadeTransition per cell. Built Phase 0 auth flow: added `SplashPage` (fade-in + scale animation on the logo) and updated `app_router` to show splash during `SessionBooting` state. Built Phase 1 Owner/Manager screens: (1) `StaffListPage` — team-member list with avatar+name+role; (2) `NotificationsPage` — activity feed (5-item mock) with icon/timestamp/unread indicator; (3) `SettingsPage` — account settings with sign-out flow and preference sections; (4) `AttendancePage` — heatmap widget showing 4-week attendance pattern, plus summary stats (visits, average daily, peak hour); (5) `FinancePage` — revenue summary card + donut breakdown chart (4 segment colors) + recent transaction list. Updated `app_router.dart` to wire all new routes as nested branches under `/dashboard`, so navigation from dashboard screens is `context.push('/dashboard/staff')` etc. Verified: `flutter analyze` (0 issues, including on the newly-strict chart widget classes), `flutter build apk --debug` succeeds (17.4s) with a working APK output. All 4 theme variants compile and typecheck correctly; chart animations respect `MediaQuery.disableAnimations`. Not yet built: Phase 2 Coach screens (7), Phase 3 Desk/Receptionist screens (6), Phase 4 Member screens (9), Add Member workflow, Finance/Reports/Settings full implementations, real data integration, Socket.IO realtime — chart components and routing are ready for all downstream features to wire in.

Design's "Cancel session" button dropped — only per-booking cancel exists (`POST /bookings/:id/cancel`), no cancel-the-whole-session endpoint.

  Verified the same way as Chunk 3: `flutter analyze` clean, every endpoint curl-tested (including creating a live `RECEPTIONIST` test user through the real invite→Mailpit→activation flow, generating a real `GroupClass`+schedule+sessions via the Owner token to have real Classes data to test against, and booking/checking-in against it), on-device via `uiautomator dump` (this session's touch-input lag was worse than Chunk 3's — several taps landed 2+ screens later than intended, including one that exited to the OS launcher — dumps taken after generous `sleep` delays remained the reliable signal throughout). **Not yet built at the time**: Trainer module (Chunk 4) and Member entry point (Chunk 6) — both landed later, in Prompts 55/56. The Chunk-2e design-fidelity gap noted in Prompt 53 was closed in Prompt 57.

55 — **Mobile app — Trainer module complete (Chunk 4, sub-chunked 4a–4f; built after Chunk 5 at the user's direction — "first complete chunk 4")** (`apps/mobile`, Flutter). `TrainerShell` — the **only 4-tab shell** in the app (Home/Workouts/Diet/Menu, vs every other role's 5), selected by `HomeRouterScreen` off `session.user.isTrainer`. New models (`Exercise`, `WorkoutPlan`+`PlanExercise`+`WeekDay`, `Food`, `DietPlan`+`PlanMeal`+`MealType`, `MemberWorkoutProgress`, `MemberDietProgress`) and repositories (`ExerciseRepository`, `FoodRepository`, plus full CRUD added to the previously list-only `WorkoutPlanRepository`/`DietPlanRepository` — their old `list()` methods returning `WorkoutPlanSummary`/`DietPlanSummary` are left untouched, since the Owner/Manager read-only catalog screens from Chunk 2c still use them). Support/Notifications/Reports-hub screens reused unmodified from Chunks 2/5.

Plans are reached only through the coaching flow: My clients → Assign → Workout/Diet plan → plan picker → builder. The picker list itself has no design frame (the design jumps straight from "4a. Assign plan" to an already-open plan in "6. Workout builder"); it's the minimal glue screen needed when more than one plan exists.
