Full typecheck/eslint(0 warnings) clean on tenant-web.

73 — **Member Portal: nav moved from a horizontal top bar into a sidebar** (`apps/tenant-web`). Immediate user follow-up to Prompt 72 — explicitly asked for the same left-sidebar layout the staff side uses instead of the scrollable top tab row `MemberPortalShell` had. Rebuilt as a desktop rail (`w-64`, tenant logo+name header, active-item left-accent-bar nav rows — same visual language as the staff `Sidebar`/`SidebarNav`) + a mobile slide-over `Drawer`, with a slim mobile-only top header (hamburger + name + `DownloadAppButton`) replacing the old always-visible top bar. Deliberately NOT wired to the staff `Sidebar`'s `navigation` Redux slice (no collapse state, no permission/feature-flag filtering) — the member plane has no RBAC and only ever has these fixed 8 tabs, so local `useState` for the mobile drawer open/closed state is all that's needed; this stays a small self-contained component, per the same "deliberately simple" reasoning Prompt 39 originally gave for this shell, just relocated visually. Removed the old shell's `max-w-4xl` centered-column constraint (pages now use the full sidebar-layout width, matching every staff-side page). Verified live via Playwright at both desktop (1440px, sidebar visible, active-item highlight correct across routes) and mobile (390px, hamburger opens/closes the drawer correctly, all 8 tabs present). Typecheck/eslint(0 warnings) clean.

74 — **Fixed ₹ showing as `$`/garbled in downloaded invoice PDFs — two separate bugs, both platform-wide** (`apps/api`). User-reported via an actual downloaded PDF: `member-invoice.service.ts`'s `renderPdfFor` (backs both the staff "Download PDF" button and the member portal's own invoice download) had a literal hardcoded `const money = (amount) => \`$${...}\`` — completely ignoring the tenant's real currency, unlike every other money-formatting call site in the codebase. Fixed to use the tenant's actual `currencySymbol` (same `settings.currencySymbol ?? '₹'` + `formatMoney()` pattern `email()` already used in this same file). `invoice.service.ts`'s two PDF/HTML renderers already used the tenant's real `currency` correctly, just with `en-US` locale grouping — switched to `en-IN` for consistency.

  - **Second, deeper bug found while verifying**: even after that fix, the actual PDF still didn't show `₹` — PDFKit's built-in "standard 14" fonts (Helvetica etc.) use WinAnsiEncoding and have **no `₹` glyph at all**; the correct string was being passed but rendered as a garbled fallback character. Fixed by bundling DejaVu Sans (full Unicode coverage including `₹`, permissive Bitstream Vera license — `assets/fonts/DejaVuSans{,-Bold}.ttf` + `DejaVuSans-LICENSE.txt`) and registering it (`core/pdf/pdf-fonts.ts` → `PDF_FONT_REGULAR`/`PDF_FONT_BOLD`) in both PDFKit generators via `doc.registerFont('body', PDF_FONT_REGULAR).font('body')`. Any future PDFKit-based document that prints a currency amount must do the same — relying on the default font will silently reintroduce this exact bug.
  - **Third bug, a data-integrity gap, found while investigating why testgym's PDF still showed `$` after the code fix**: `TenantSettings.currencySymbol` is a genuinely independent stored column (by design — a tenant can customize it away from the derived default), but every tenant provisioned before Prompt 70's onboarding-symbol-derivation fix (or via the older direct `/auth/register` path, which never sets currency/currencySymbol at all) was stuck at whatever the schema default was at creation time, regardless of its actual `currency` code. Extracted the onboarding fix's inline symbol-deriver into a shared `core/utils/currency.util.ts#deriveCurrencySymbol` (Intl-based, not a hand-maintained map) and ran a one-off local backfill: of this dev DB's 4 tenants, ALL FOUR had a stale `currencySymbol` (2 had `currency: INR` with symbol still `$`; the other 2 had `currency: USD` itself, clearly a stale never-customized default given every tenant in this app is India-based test data) — corrected all 4 to `INR`/`₹` directly via SQL, matching the user's explicit ask ("show INR default or select current"). **Not yet run against the deployed server's database** — needs the same investigation (check for any tenant that looks like a deliberate non-INR choice before blanket-correcting) before applying there.
  - Verified: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/vitest(288/288) clean; re-downloaded the exact invoice PDF that surfaced this bug and confirmed via `pdftoppm` visual render (not just `pdftotext`, which itself was silently mis-extracting the pre-fix glyph) that `₹7,499.00` now renders correctly throughout.

75 — **Deployed server converted to a real git repo — a second, git-based redeploy path** (`infrastructure/testing-deploy`). The server's `~/gym-management` had never been a git repo (every `06-redeploy-code.sh` run rsyncs a local checkout, and rsync always excludes `.git`) — user asked for a manual, SSH-only deploy path that doesn't depend on having a local clone + the `.pem` key on the same machine. `git init` + `remote add origin` + `fetch origin main` + `reset --hard origin/main` directly on the server (the initial `checkout main` step failed — "untracked working tree files would be overwritten", since every already-rsynced file was untracked in the brand-new repo — but `reset --hard` isn't blocked by that the way `checkout` is, and completed the sync anyway; cleaned up the stray `git init`-default `master` branch afterward, keeping just `main` tracking `origin/main`). Verified `.env`/pm2 processes were completely undisturbed (`reset --hard` only touches tracked files, never gitignored ones). New sibling script `07-redeploy-via-git.sh` mirrors `06-redeploy-code.sh`'s install/migrate/build/restart tail but starts with `git fetch && git reset --hard origin/main` on the server instead of rsync-ing a local checkout — use this one when deploying from a machine with SSH access but no local repo clone.

76 — **Future-dated membership assignment ("join today, start in N days") — web + mobile** (`apps/api`, `apps/tenant-web`, `apps/mobile`). India-common gym flow: a member pays/joins today but tells the owner to start on a specific future date. `assignMembership` already accepted an optional `startDate`, but always stored the new `Membership` as `ACTIVE` regardless — a future-dated assignment granted check-in access immediately, which was the actual gap. Added a `PENDING` value to the `MembershipStatus` enum (migration `20260905115928_add_pending_membership_status`, plain `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE`, no RLS involved — enum, not a new table): `assignMembership` now stores `PENDING` when `startDate` is in the future, `ACTIVE` when it's today/past (unchanged same-day behavior). Every "current membership" lookup already keyed off `status === 'ACTIVE'` (`canCheckIn`, the assign/renew/upgrade/downgrade/cancel/extend "must already have one" guards), so a `PENDING` membership automatically grants no check-in and blocks nothing else — the one deliberate addition is `assignMembership`'s own "already has one" guard, extended to also block a second assignment while a `PENDING` one is outstanding (couldn't double-book two future start dates). New scheduler job `pending-membership-activation` (`membership.handlers.ts`, cron `0 0 * * *`, mirrors the existing `auto-membership-status-update` expiry-flip job in the opposite direction) flips `PENDING` → `ACTIVE` once `startDate <= now`. `toListItem`'s `currentMembership` falls back to a `PENDING` row when there's no `ACTIVE` one, so staff still see what's coming up instead of a blank "no plan." Frontend: tenant-web's member-detail "Assign a plan" card gained a start-date `<input type=date>` (mirrors the existing diet/workout-plan assign-card pattern) plus a `MembershipStatusBadge` PENDING case (same sky-blue "info" style already used for `FROZEN` member status); mobile's member-creation form (`member_form_screen.dart`, which already chains a first-plan assignment after `POST /members`) gained the same date-picker row, copied from the catalog module's `AssignToMemberCard` pattern, plus a `PENDING` case in the member-detail screen's history-status color switch and "Starts X" vs "Ends X" wording on the membership summary card. Verified live via curl on the running dev stack: future `startDate` → `PENDING`/`canCheckIn: false`; no `startDate` → `ACTIVE`/`canCheckIn: true` (same-day case unaffected); a second assignment attempt while `PENDING` correctly rejected; the new scheduler job invoked directly against a backdated test row correctly flipped it to `ACTIVE`. Typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/vitest(288/288)/build all clean on API + tenant-web; `flutter analyze` clean on mobile (0 new issues).

77 — **Membership Plan field enforcement audit + implementation — web + mobile** (`apps/api`, `apps/tenant-web`, `apps/mobile`). User audit request: of `MembershipPlan`'s ~13 configurable fields, only `validityStart`/`validityEnd`/`minAge`/`maxAge`/`renewalWindowDays`/`autoRenewalAllowed` were actually enforced anywhere — the other fields (shown in a real "Create Membership Plan" form: PT sessions, group classes, guest passes, gym access branches, diet consultation, locker access, freeze allowed/days limit, grace period) were pure write-only display values with zero business logic behind them. Full audit + fix, tiered by user priority:
    - **Existing-logic gaps (real bugs)**: `freezeAllowed`/`freezeDaysLimit` — `MemberService#freeze` now checks `plan.freezeAllowed` and rejects if the membership's cumulative CLOSED-freeze days (`MembershipRepository#sumFreezeDays`, new — sums `unfrozenAt - frozenAt` per membership, min 1 day each) already meets `freezeDaysLimit` (`null` = uncapped, unchanged default). `gracePeriodDays` — new `isWithinGracePeriod()` helper (`members/utils/duration.util.ts`) shared by three call sites that previously used a hard `endDate < now` cutoff: `AttendanceService#eligibility` (check-in), `MemberService#toDetail`'s `canCheckIn` (now genuinely enforced, not just informational — `AttendanceService#eligibility` independently derives and enforces the same result), and the `auto-membership-status-update` scheduler job (now fetches candidates + filters in JS instead of a plain `updateMany`, since Prisma can't express "endDate + a related row's field" in one WHERE). `gymAccessAllBranches`/`accessBranchIds` — previously only filtered the branch-scoped plan-picker dropdown (`MembershipPlanRepository#buildWhere`), never gated an actual check-in; `AttendanceService#performCheckIn` now allows check-in away from the member's home branch only when the active plan's access rule permits it (home branch itself is always allowed as a safety net even if misconfigured).
    - **New quota-tracking tables (new business concepts)**: `guestPasses` and `ptSessionsIncluded` had no tracking concept anywhere — added `GuestVisit` and `PtSessionLog` (both new, RLS'd — migration `20260905133329_guest_visits_and_pt_session_logs` for the tables, `20260905133400_enable_rls_guest_visits_pt_sessions` for RLS), one row per guest visit / PT session logged against a specific `membershipId` (quota resets naturally on renewal — a new `Membership` row means a fresh id to count against). New `MemberService` methods `logGuestVisit`/`listGuestVisits`/`logPtSession`/`listPtSessions`, new routes `POST/GET /members/:id/guest-visits` and `/pt-sessions` (reuse `members:view`/`members:update` — no new permission keys). `groupClassesIncluded` had a real `ClassBooking` table already but nothing counted a member's bookings against it — `ClassBookingService#book` now counts non-cancelled bookings whose session falls inside the active membership's `[startDate, endDate]` window (`ClassBookingRepository#countActiveInPeriod`, new) and rejects once the quota's hit. **Deliberate convention across all three quota fields**: `0` means "not configured on this plan" (the field's actual default), never "zero allowed" — a literal zero-quota read would have silently locked every pre-existing plan that never touched these fields out of guest visits/PT sessions/class booking entirely; only a plan with an explicit positive quota gets enforced. New `MemberDetailDto.planUsage` (`MemberService#buildPlanUsage`) surfaces used/included counts for all four quotas in one field so the frontend doesn't need 4 extra round-trips.
    - **`dietConsultationIncluded`** (user explicitly chose "enforce it" over "leave descriptive," aware of the tradeoff): `DietPlanService#assign` now rejects when the member has an active membership whose plan has this `false` — **note this field defaults `false` on every plan**, so any pre-existing plan that never explicitly enabled it will now block diet-plan assignment until edited; `lockerAccess` was deliberately left descriptive-only per the same conversation (no digital enforcement point exists for a physical amenity).
    - Frontend: tenant-web's member-detail membership card gained a "plan usage" row (guest passes/PT sessions/group classes/freeze days, each only shown when the plan actually configures it) plus "Log guest visit"/"Log PT session" quick actions (only shown when `status === 'ACTIVE'` and the respective quota is configured); mobile's member-detail screen got the matching usage row + two dialog-prompted log actions, plus a `PENDING`-adjacent `AppColors.warning` case in the history-status switch. Verified live via curl against a purpose-built test plan (all quotas=1, `freezeAllowed=false`→`true`+`freezeDaysLimit=1`, `accessBranchIds=[mainBranch]`, `gracePeriodDays=2`, `dietConsultationIncluded=false`): every one of the 9 enforcement paths (guest pass/PT session/group class quota, freeze gate, cumulative freeze-days cap, diet gate, branch-access check-in, grace-period check-in both directions, grace-period-aware scheduler flip both directions) hit the exact expected accept/reject outcome. Typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/vitest(288/288)/build clean on API + tenant-web; `flutter analyze` clean on mobile.

78 — **Fixed hardcoded `$` showing instead of the tenant's real currency symbol across the entire tenant-web frontend** (`apps/tenant-web`). User-reported: gym branding configures currency symbol `₹` (Regional & Display settings), but the Membership Plans list showed `$`. Turned out to be systemic, not a one-off — an exhaustive repo-wide grep found the same literal `` `$${...}` `` / bare `$` pattern hardcoded in **12 files**: membership plans list + plan-select dropdown + membership-history table, invoices list/detail/new, payments list/detail/new, expenses list, income list, the analytics branch-comparison chart tooltip, the finance revenue-trend chart tooltip, and finance summary cards — none of them read the tenant's actual `BusinessSettings.currencySymbol` (already correctly plumbed and used correctly on the *backend* PDF renderers since Prompt 74, but that fix was never mirrored on this frontend list/chart side). Fix: new shared `useCurrencySymbol()` hook (`lib/currency.ts`, wraps the existing `useBusinessSettings()` query, defaults `₹` while loading — same convention as Prompt 74's backend fix) reused at all 12 call sites in place of the literal `$`. **Deliberately did NOT touch** the two hardcoded `₹` symbols in the *member portal* dashboard (`app/portal/(authenticated)/dashboard/page.tsx`) — that plane uses a separate `member-api-client.ts` / auth token from the staff-side `useBusinessSettings()` call, so reusing the same hook there risks a 403 against a staff-only endpoint (and, worse, an unwanted session-expired redirect via the staff API client's interceptor); fixing that plane safely would need its own member-portal-safe currency endpoint, out of scope for this bug report. Verified the fix's data source live via curl (`GET /settings/business` → `currencySymbol: "₹"` for the test tenant) and typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build all clean — no full Playwright browser pass (subdomain tenant routing isn't configured in this dev environment's `/etc/hosts`), so this is verified via the underlying data + zero-error build, not a rendered screenshot.

79 — **Mirrored Prompt 78's currency-symbol fix on mobile** (`apps/mobile`). User follow-up: "is the above change done in mobile as well" — it wasn't; mobile turned out to have a bigger version of the same bug. `Formatters.currency`/`currencyCompact` hardcoded not just `₹` but **Indian lakh-grouping** (`₹1,20,000`) — a symbol-only swap would've shown `$1,20,000` for a non-INR tenant, arguably worse than the honest INR-only assumption it replaced. User explicitly chose the full fix over a quick symbol swap or leaving it INR-only. New `AppCurrency` (`core/utils/app_currency.dart`) is a static, synchronously-readable `{symbol, code}` cache (defaults `₹`/`INR`, unchanged from before) — refreshed via `GymSettingsRepository.getBusinessSettings()` once per staff session (`SessionCubit.staffSignedIn`/`restore()`'s staff branch, fire-and-forget `unawaited()`) and reset back to the INR default on sign-out/change-gym so a stale currency never bleeds into the next tenant's session on the same device. `Formatters.currency`/`currencyCompact` now use `AppCurrency.symbol` and switch grouping style on `AppCurrency.code`: Indian lakh-grouping only when it's actually `'INR'`, otherwise standard thousands-grouping (`$120,000`) with `k`/`M` compact suffixes instead of `k`/`L`. Also fixed 13 more hardcoded `₹` literals that bypassed the shared formatter entirely (same "many independent copies" pattern as the web bug), across 4 **staff**-plane screens (`member_detail_screen.dart`, `member_upgrade/downgrade/renew_screen.dart`) — all now read `AppCurrency.symbol`. **Deliberately left untouched**: the 2 **member**-plane screens (`member_invoices_screen.dart`, `member_invoice_detail_screen.dart`, `features/member/presentation/`) — `GET /settings/business` is staff-only, same auth-plane boundary Prompt 78 drew on web (the member portal has no safe way to fetch it without its own new member-portal-safe endpoint, out of scope here). `flutter analyze` clean (0 new issues, same 2 pre-existing unrelated infos as always).

80 — **Super Admin's "Feature Flags" actually wired up — a real, enforced platform-wide kill switch** (`apps/api`). User asked what Feature Flags does; the honest answer was "nothing" — the seeded 13 keys (`attendance`/`workout`/`chat`/`inventory`/`pos`/`white_label`/`custom_domain`/`video_training`/`ai_coach`/`marketplace`/etc.) had zero code reading the `FeatureFlag` table anywhere; toggling one just wrote a DB row + audit entry. Separately, the REAL per-tenant module gating (`TenantModule`, read as `ResolvedTenant.featureFlags`, driving both tenant-web's `use-filtered-nav.ts` sidebar and mobile's menu tiles) already existed and worked, using its own different key vocabulary (`workout_plans`/`diet_plans`/`live_classes`/`payments`/`income`/`expenses`/`support_tickets`/etc. — see `nav-config.ts`). User asked for it to be updated to match real/current features AND to actually disable a module completely on both web and mobile when toggled. Fix, in the one place that already feeds both frontends:
    - **Re-seeded `FEATURE_FLAGS`** (`prisma/seed.ts`) with 11 keys chosen to exactly match the live `TenantModule` vocabulary above — `attendance`, `workout_plans`, `diet_plans`, `live_classes`, `payments`, `income`, `expenses`, `reports` (covers both Reports and Analytics nav items, same as the plan-feature side), `notifications`, `support_tickets`, `ai_coach`. Dropped the 6 keys with literally no backend module behind them (`chat`/`inventory`/`pos`/`white_label`/`custom_domain`/`video_training`/`marketplace` — `chat` and `white_label`/`custom_domain` DO exist as real `TenantModule`/plan-feature keys, just with no dedicated router this prompt could hard-block, so they're deliberately left without a platform kill switch rather than building one that can't verifiably do anything). Deliberately excludes `members`/`staff`/`branches`/`membership_plans` too — foundational, no real "disable Members platform-wide" scenario exists. Seed script now also `deleteMany`s any stale key not in the new list.
    - **`TenantService#toResolvedTenant`** (`modules/tenants/service/tenant.service.ts`) now ANDs a tenant's plan-granted modules (`record.modules`) against a new platform-flags map — `featureFlags: record.modules.filter(m => m.enabled && (platformFlags.get(m.key) ?? true))...`. A key with no matching `FeatureFlag` row (i.e. not one of the 11) reads as platform-enabled by default via `?? true`, so this never affects a module Prompt 80 didn't turn into a switch. Backed by its own tiny cache entry (`platform:feature-flags:enabled`, 30s TTL) **separate** from the existing 5-minute per-tenant `tenant:slug:*`/`tenant:id:*` cache — `AdminFeatureFlagService#setEnabled` calls the new `invalidatePlatformFeatureFlagsCache()` right after every toggle, so a kill switch is live within seconds platform-wide, not up to 5 minutes per already-cached tenant.
    - **New `requireModuleEnabled(key)` middleware** (`modules/tenants/middleware/require-module-enabled.middleware.ts`) — reads the SAME `req.tenant.featureFlags` array `tenantMiddleware` already attaches, 403s with "This module is currently unavailable." if the key's missing. Mounted at the top of every real gated router (right after `authenticateMiddleware`): attendance, workout-plans + exercises, diet-plans + foods, classes + class-sessions + bookings, member-payment + member-invoice + finance-dashboard, income, expenses, reports + dashboard + scheduled-report + analytics, tenant-notification + notification-template + announcements + tenant-announcements, support tickets, and ai-assistant — 23 router files total.
    - **Zero frontend code changes needed** on either web or mobile — both already read `tenant.featureFlags` (`GET /public/tenants/resolve`) for nav-hiding, so a platform-disabled module now disappears from the sidebar/menu automatically, on top of the new hard 403 at the API if someone still hits the route directly.
    - Verified live: disabling `attendance` — `/attendance/today` flips 200→403 immediately (no cache wait), and a fresh `/public/tenants/resolve` call's `featureFlags` array immediately drops `attendance` — then confirmed re-enabling restores both. Spot-checked the two non-standard router-mounting shapes too (`ai_coach` via a combined `.use(authenticateMiddleware, requireModuleEnabled(...), requirePermission(...))`, and `notifications` via `announcements`' single inline-middleware route) — both behave identically. Typecheck/eslint(0 warnings, one auto-fixed import-order batch)/vitest(288/288)/build all clean.

81 — **CMS: wired the two spots that already existed and were dead** (`apps/api`, `apps/tenant-web`). Same "what does this actually do" pattern as Prompt 80's Feature Flags investigation, this time for Super Admin's CMS screen (`CmsPage` — LANDING/BLOG/FAQ/TESTIMONIAL/TERMS/PRIVACY/COOKIE): the admin CRUD was fully real, but nothing public ever read it — no `/public/cms` route existed, and neither `tenant-web` nor `landing-web` fetched it. `landing-web` (where LANDING/BLOG/TESTIMONIAL would eventually live) is still a completely empty scaffold, out of scope here — but two spots in the ALREADY-LIVE `tenant-web` app were found dead and got wired instead: the registration form's "I agree to Terms of Service / Privacy Policy" checkbox (`register-form.tsx`) had both links hardcoded to `href="#"`, and the tenant Help Center's FAQ list (`support/page.tsx`) was a hardcoded 3-item array. New `GET /public/cms/pages?type=X` (`modules/admin-cms/routes/public-cms.routes.ts`, no auth — same "platform-wide content, not tenant-scoped" reasoning as `/public/app-releases`), backed by a new `AdminCmsService#listPublished` (published rows only, drafts never leak through — verified live: a draft FAQ never appeared in the public list). New tenant-web `features/cms/` (types/service/`usePublishedCmsPages` hook) plus two new public pages, `/terms` and `/privacy` (Server Components, plain `fetch` at request time with a 5-minute revalidate — same pattern `resolveTenant()` already used for pre-login rendering), rendering a CMS `TERMS`/`PRIVACY` page's `content.body` split into paragraphs, with an honest "hasn't been published yet" fallback rather than fabricated placeholder legal text. `support/page.tsx`'s FAQ card now renders `usePublishedCmsPages('FAQ')` (each `CmsPage` row = one Q&A, `title`/`content.answer`) instead of the hardcoded array, with a loading state and an honest empty state. Checked mobile for an equivalent hardcoded FAQ/Terms spot — none exists (no registration flow or Help Center on that plane), nothing to mirror there. Verified live end-to-end: created + published real FAQ/TERMS pages via `/admin/cms/pages`, confirmed both appear via `/public/cms/pages`, confirmed a draft does NOT, confirmed `/terms`'s rendered HTML contains the live content, confirmed the FAQ endpoint is reachable identically from within an authenticated tenant session. Typecheck/eslint(0 warnings, one auto-fixed import-order)/vitest(288/288)/build clean on API + tenant-web.

82 — **Add Member redesigned into a 3-step wizard — extended personal info, Health Background Screening, and a Program+Payment step folded into onboarding (web only)** (`apps/api`, `apps/tenant-web`). User request, driven by screenshots of a reference gym-CRM's onboarding flow; user confirmed the reference app's "Program" = this codebase's existing `MembershipPlan`/`Membership` concept, not a new entity — no new billing/plan model was introduced.

  - **Schema** (migration `20260906231504_add_member_wizard_fields`, additive columns only — no new table, so no new RLS migration needed; `members`/`memberships` were already RLS'd): `Member` gained `fatherNameOrAadhaar`, `maritalStatus` (new enum), `anniversary`, `goal` (new `FitnessGoal` enum — deliberately separate from the pre-existing free-text `fitnessGoals`, which is untouched), `registrationFee` (a one-time gym fee, distinct from `MembershipPlan.joiningFee`), `bodyType`/`foodPreference` (new enums), 7 nullable health-screening booleans (heart condition/pain-during-activity/dizziness-or-balance/diabetes-or-BP/asthma/bone-or-joint-problem/other-condition — nullable rather than defaulted `false`, so "never asked" stays distinguishable from "asked, answered No" for every pre-existing member), `awarenessSource` (new enum), `healthScreeningOtherDetails` (kept distinct from the pre-existing generic `notes` column), and a self-referential `referredByMemberId` (`SetNull`, +index) for real referral tracking. `Membership` gained `targetWeight` (per-assignment, not per-plan — member-specific).
  - **Backend — zero new endpoints**: the wizard's final Save just calls the three endpoints that already existed, in sequence — `POST /members` (now accepting all the new fields), `PUT /members/:id/membership` (now accepting `targetWeight`), and the pre-existing `POST /payments` (unchanged — its existing auto-invoice-on-SUCCESS + auto-`Income`-row business rule fires exactly as it always has, verified live). New `MemberService#assertReferredMemberValid` (mirrors `assertTrainerValid`) rejects a bogus or self-referential `referredByMemberId` with a clean 404/422. `member.validators.spec.ts` grew from ~28 to 38 tests covering the new fields; full API suite 296/296 green.
  - **Frontend**: `/members/new` is now `AddMemberWizard` (`features/members/components/add-member-wizard/`) — a local 3-step container (Personal & Program → Health Screening → Proforma Invoice/Payment) using plain `useState` throughout (matching this module's existing `plan-form-fields.tsx`/member-detail-page convention, not react-hook-form) with **no generic Stepper primitive promoted to `components/ui/`** (kept feature-local, like `member-checkin-search.tsx`). **Submits only at the final step** — steps 1-2 are pure local state, nothing is created until Save fires the create→assign→pay sequence; if a later step fails after the member is created, a specific toast names which step failed and the user lands on `/members/:id` to finish it manually via the pre-existing inline sections there (nothing is silently lost). A $0 payment skips the `/payments` call entirely rather than recording a `$0 SUCCESS` row. Two new field-set components — `member-extended-info-fields.tsx` and `member-health-screening-fields.tsx` — are shared between the wizard and a set of two new "Additional details"/"Health Screening" cards added to the existing member detail/edit page's main form (folded into its pre-existing single dirty-tracked `FormState`/`UnsavedChangesBar` cycle, not a new immediate-save mini-section), finally closing this module's long-standing create/edit field-parity gap (create previously exposed only 8 of 23 backend fields). The referral field reuses the existing `MemberCheckinSearch` component verbatim. Program selection reuses the existing `MembershipPlanSelect` plus a new small read-only "Details" popover-dialog showing the selected plan's feature list. Program (`planId`) is deliberately **not** hard-required in the wizard despite the reference screenshot marking it so — preserves the pre-existing "walk-in member, no plan yet" flow.
  - Verified live end-to-end via curl against the running dev stack (no Playwright/browser tool available this session, same documented gap as Prompt 44 — pages confirmed rendering via a 200 + no error-boundary markers, not a real click-through): full happy path (create with all new fields → assign with `targetWeight` → record a discounted CASH payment → confirmed the invoice auto-generated as `PAID` and a `MEMBERSHIP_FEE` Income row auto-created), the walk-in path (no plan/payment → confirmed empty `membershipHistory`/no payment row), the referral FK (`referredByMember` summary renders correctly; a self-referral attempt correctly 422s). Typecheck/eslint(0 warnings, both apps)/build(both apps)/vitest(296/296) all clean.
  - **Not done this pass**: mobile (`member_form_screen.dart` + the split edit screens) — explicitly scoped as a follow-up phase once web is confirmed working, per the user's own "first do in web when complete then start in mobile" instruction.

83 — **Member detail page: staff can now send an existing portal member a "reset password" link** (`apps/api`, `apps/tenant-web`). User request. The member self-service portal plane (Prompt 39) already had a staff-triggered "Enable portal access" button (`sendPortalInvite`), but that call hard-rejects with 409 once the member's `MemberCredential` is `ACTIVE` — there was no staff-facing way at all to help a member who forgot their password; the only path was the member's own self-service, enumeration-safe `POST /member/auth/forgot-password` (keyed by their public `memberId` code, silently no-ops on anything not ACTIVE).

  - **Backend — new `MemberAuthService#adminResetPassword(memberId, name, email)`**: unlike `forgotPassword` (deliberately silent/enumeration-safe), this is called from an authenticated staff action against an internal id already fetched via `mustFind`, so it fails loudly — 404 "Portal access is not set up for this member" if no `MemberCredential` row exists yet, 422 "hasn't activated portal access yet — use Enable portal access instead" if it's `PENDING_ACTIVATION`/`SUSPENDED`. On an `ACTIVE` credential it creates a `PASSWORD_RESET` `MemberVerification` row and fires the same `MemberAuthEvents.PasswordResetRequested` event the self-service flow already uses — zero new email-template plumbing needed. New `MemberService#sendPortalPasswordReset` wraps it (mirrors `sendPortalInvite`'s shape exactly) behind new route `POST /members/:id/portal-reset-password` (permission `members:update`, same as `portal-invite`), audited as `member.portal_password_reset_requested`.
  - **New `MemberDetailDto.portalStatus`** (`PENDING_ACTIVATION | ACTIVE | SUSPENDED | null`) — closes a real, separate gap found while building this: the member detail page previously had **zero visibility** into whether a member already had a portal account at all, so "Enable portal access" was always shown identically regardless of state (only erroring after the click on an already-ACTIVE member). `MEMBER_INCLUDE` now joins `credential: { select: { status: true } }`; `toDetail` maps `member.credential?.status ?? null`.
  - **Frontend**: the member detail page's "Member portal" card now branches on `data.portalStatus` — `null` shows "Enable portal access" (unchanged), `PENDING_ACTIVATION` shows "Resend activation email" (same invite endpoint, relabeled — it's already idempotent for this state), `ACTIVE` shows the new "Send reset password link" button (`useSendPortalPasswordReset`), `SUSPENDED` shows status text with no action (reactivating a suspended portal account is out of scope here). `useSendPortalInvite` now also invalidates the member-detail query on success, so the button's label updates immediately once a member moves from no-account → `PENDING_ACTIVATION` without a manual refresh.
  - Verified live end-to-end against the running dev stack, including real Mailpit email delivery at every step: created a test member with no portal account (reset-password correctly 404s, distinct message from the "no email on file" 422), enabled portal access, attempted reset-password while `PENDING_ACTIVATION` (correctly 422s with the "use Enable portal access instead" message), completed real activation via the actual Mailpit email's token, confirmed `portalStatus` flips to `ACTIVE`, then called reset-password again — succeeded, and a real "Reset your password" email arrived in Mailpit whose token verified as genuine via `GET /member/auth/reset-password/:token` (returned the correct member name + a real 30-minute expiry). Typecheck/eslint(0 warnings, both apps)/vitest(296/296)/build(both apps) all clean. No Playwright/browser tool available this session (same documented gap as Prompts 44/82) — button visibility logic verified by direct code review against the confirmed API contract, not a live click-through.

84 — **Mobile parity for Prompt 83's member portal password-reset work** (`apps/mobile`). User asked to check, then port, Prompt 83's two web features to mobile. Found: the member-side "request a reset link" already existed on mobile (`forgot_password_screen.dart` calling `MemberAuthRepository.forgotPassword`), but the "consume the emailed token and set a new password" half did not exist anywhere in the app; the staff-side "Enable portal access" button existed but always showed unconditionally, with no equivalent to web's new `portalStatus`-aware "Send reset password link".

  - **Member reset-password**: new `MemberAuthRepository.resetPassword({token, password})` (mirrors `forgotPassword`'s try/`DioException`/`_mapError` shape exactly) and a new screen `features/auth/presentation/member_reset_password_screen.dart` (route `/member/reset-password`), reachable from the forgot-password confirmation view via a new "Have a reset code? Enter it here" link (member role only). **This app has zero deep-link handling** (no `app_links`/`uni_links` dependency, no platform manifest wiring, no listener in `main.dart`) — confirmed by grep, this was true before this prompt too, for every feature. A member tapping the real emailed link opens a browser, not the app, so the new screen has the user paste the code or the whole link by hand; `_extractToken()` pulls the `token` query param out if a full URL is pasted, otherwise treats the input as the raw token.
  - **Staff-side parity**: `GymMember` gained `portalStatus` (nullable `String`, matching this exact model's existing convention for `status`/`gender`/`bloodGroup` — deliberately not a new enum class, even though other unrelated models in this codebase do enum-ify similar fields). New `MemberRepository.sendPortalPasswordReset()` (`POST /members/:id/portal-reset-password`). `member_detail_screen.dart`'s `_buildPortalCard` now branches on `portalStatus` exactly like web's Prompt 83 card (null → Enable portal access, `PENDING_ACTIVATION` → Resend activation email, `ACTIVE` → Send reset password link, `SUSPENDED` → status text/no button) plus an `AppPill` status badge; `_runAction` gained an optional `successMessage` param (shown via the existing inline `ScaffoldMessenger` pattern — no shared toast helper exists in this codebase) so both portal actions confirm success, and `_sendPortalInvite` now reloads the member after success (previously `reload: false`, since there was nothing new to reflect before `portalStatus` existed).
  - **Not verified by tooling — flag this clearly**: the Flutter SDK is not installed on this dev machine (`flutter`/`dart` absent from PATH in both available shells, no `.dart_tool/` directory, `pubspec.lock` present but dependencies never resolved) — `flutter analyze`, `flutter build`, and a real device/emulator run were all unavailable this session. Verification was careful manual review only: every widget API used (`AppPill`, `AppLabeledField`, `AppButton`, `AppCard`, `FormAlert`) was checked against its real source file or an existing call site already working elsewhere in the same file, and every new field/method uses named parameters (no positional-arg ordering risk). **Run `flutter analyze` and a real device pass (per this doc's own adb workflow) before trusting this further** — this is the first mobile change in this codebase's history made without either check available.

85 — **Play Store readiness pass 1** (`apps/mobile`). User asked whether the app could be published to Google Play; audited the actual repo (not a generic checklist) and found 4 hard blockers + 4 secondary gaps — see the audit's findings folded into this entry. Fixed everything fixable without a deployed production URL, a JDK, or the user's own company/legal details:

  - **App identity**: `AndroidManifest.xml`'s `android:label` changed from the raw `gym_saas_mobile` to "FitCloud".
  - **SDK versions**: `minSdk` pinned to `21` explicitly in `build.gradle.kts` (confirmed against `mobile_scanner`'s own documented floor — checked its `android/build.gradle` on GitHub rather than guessing). `compileSdk`/`targetSdk` deliberately left tracking Flutter's bundled defaults, NOT hardcoded — Flutter's tooling keeps those current with Play's yearly-rising minimum target API requirement, and picking a specific number here risked being wrong given the knowledge-cutoff gap (this assistant's cutoff is Jan 2026; work happened Sept 2026).
  - **ProGuard/minification**: new `android/app/proguard-rules.pro` (standard Flutter-embedding keep rules + a `-dontwarn` for Play Core's deferred-components classes, which a default Flutter release build references even when unused) + `isMinifyEnabled`/`isShrinkResources` enabled on the release build type.
  - **Release signing plumbing**: `build.gradle.kts` gained a `signingConfigs.release` block reading `keyAlias`/`keyPassword`/`storeFile`/`storePassword` from `android/key.properties` (gitignored — file doesn't exist yet), with `buildTypes.release` pointed at it instead of the debug key. New `android/key.properties.example` documents the exact `keytool` command to generate a real keystore (needs a JDK — none installed on this dev machine) and the fields to fill in. Until that file exists, a real `--release` build now fails loudly with a clear signing error instead of silently shipping debug-signed.
  - **App icon — first attempt (hand-built dumbbell mark) reverted, second attempt (user-supplied artwork) finalized**: first pass hand-built a full icon set from 3 original SVGs (a dumbbell mark on a violet→teal gradient); the user asked to remove all of it immediately after seeing it, so it was fully reverted (`git restore` for the modified `ic_launcher.png`s, delete for everything newly created) and confirmed clean via `git status`. User then generated their own "FitCloud" artwork externally (a cloud+person-with-dumbbell mark, magenta→cyan gradient, plus a full text lockup on a dark violet→navy rounded-square) via an AI image tool and asked for the exact size/format/prompt spec, then pasted 3 images into the conversation. Those pasted images weren't reachable on disk (no upload path in this harness), so the user saved them to `Downloads/` and gave real file paths — from there the work was fully self-contained:

Rendered and visually reviewed actual preview PNGs at the critical sizes (512, 192, 48px legacy; 432px adaptive foreground; 512px Play Store icon) before wiring anything in, and got explicit user confirmation on those previews before finalizing this time.

Modern `keytool` defaults to PKCS12 format, which — a real gotcha hit live — doesn't support separate store/key passwords despite `-keypass` being accepted on the command line (silently ignored with a warning); `key.properties` correctly uses the same password for both fields to match.

A temporary plaintext-password scratch file used to hand values between two shell commands (`.keystore-passwords.tmp`, NOT gitignored — an oversight in the pattern, not a real leak since it was deleted within the same command chain before ever being left sitting) was deleted immediately after `key.properties` was written from it.

89 — **Push notifications (FCM) — backend + mobile** (`apps/api`, `apps/mobile`). User request: every business event that already emails a member should now also push to their phone, and announcements should push too. `TenantNotificationChannel.PUSH` had existed as a schema-only placeholder since Prompt 21 — this prompt is what actually wires it.

  - **New tables**: `StaffDeviceToken`/`MemberDeviceToken` (migration `20260929090150_add_device_tokens` + RLS migration `20260929090200_enable_rls_device_tokens`) — deliberately two separate tables, not one with two nullable FKs, mirroring the pre-existing `UserSession`/`MemberSession` split (this codebase never shares a table across the staff/member identity planes). `token` is globally unique (not composite with `tenantId`) since an FCM token belongs to one app install, which can legitimately move to a different tenant/owner over time — the register path (`modules/device-tokens/repositories/*.repository.ts#upsert`) uses raw `prisma`, not the tenant-scoped client, specifically so a cross-tenant reassignment isn't invisible to RLS (same documented raw-`prisma` exception as onboarding's duplicate-checks).
  - **New `infrastructure/push/fcm.client.ts`** — Firebase Admin SDK wrapper, same optional-config `isConfigured`-getter pattern as `env.pusher`/`env.s3`; new `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON_BASE64` env var (whole service-account JSON, base64-encoded). **Left `infrastructure/push/pusher.client.ts` untouched** — confirmed via grep it's still 100% dead (nothing calls `.trigger()`), and it's a different mechanism (Pusher Channels websocket pub/sub) from native FCM device-token push; not repurposed.
  - **New `infrastructure/queue/push.queue.ts` + `push.worker.ts`** — mirrors `email.queue.ts`/`email.worker.ts` exactly (BullMQ queue `notifications-push`, `enqueuePush()` helper, `startPushWorker()`/`stopPushWorker()` registered in `server.ts` bootstrap alongside the email worker). Dead-token pruning (`messaging/registration-token-not-registered`/`messaging/invalid-registration-token`) happens inline in the same worker job, not a separate scheduler sweep — it's ordinary response handling for work already running, not periodic work.
  - **New `modules/device-tokens/`** — DB access only (`StaffDeviceTokenRepository`/`MemberDeviceTokenRepository`/`DeviceTokenService`), no standalone routes/permission key. Register/unregister is exposed as self-service on the two existing "manage your own account" routers instead: `POST/DELETE /profile/device-token` (staff, `modules/profile`) and `POST/DELETE /portal/device-token` (member, `modules/member-portal`) — same "no permission key needed for your own data" precedent as everything else on those two routers.
  - **`notification-trigger.service.ts`**: `fireTemplated`'s `opts` gained `recipientMemberId?` — when a template's `channels` includes `PUSH` and a member id is given, pushes that specific member's own device(s), independent of the `IN_APP` channel's staff-wide audience. Every member-facing `notify*` function (`notifyMembershipRenewed`/`Expiring`/`Expired`, `notifyPaymentReceived`/`Failed`, `notifyBirthdayWishes`, `notifyMembershipAssigned`) now takes a `memberId` param threaded from its call site (`MemberService`, `MemberPaymentService`, `scheduler/handlers/membership.handlers.ts`+`payment.handlers.ts`+`notification.handlers.ts`). **New capability, beyond the original 10-template spec**: `notifyWorkoutAssigned`/`notifyDietAssigned`/`notifyAttendanceCheckIn`/`notifyAttendanceCheckOut` now also push the specific member directly (previously staff-feed-only, no member-facing channel existed at all for these four events) — `DEFAULT_TEMPLATES`' `channels` arrays updated to include `PUSH` for every member-facing template.
  - **`TenantNotificationService#notifyTenant`/`#create`** (the shared choke point every staff-facing `TenantNotification` write already goes through) now also fans out to every `StaffDeviceToken` in the tenant — one change point gives blanket staff push for everything already in the Notification Center feed (ad-hoc creates, membership-assigned, staff-invitation, subscription/billing lifecycle events, and the `IN_APP` half of every templated event), no per-event special-casing needed.
  - **Announcements** (`TenantAnnouncementService#publish`, the scheduler's `sendScheduledAnnouncements`): `notifyAnnouncementPublished` now takes `audience`/`branchId` (both already existed on `TenantAnnouncement`, previously dropped on the floor — `audience: MEMBERS` never reached an actual member through any channel before this). The staff feed write is unchanged/unconditional (staff always sees what was published); `audience ∈ {ALL, MEMBERS}` additionally pushes every member device, filtered to `Member.branchId` when the announcement targets one branch.
  - **Fixed live while building this**: `DEFAULT_TEMPLATES.BIRTHDAY_WISHES`'s `description` field (returned verbatim by `GET /notifications/templates`, shown in the tenant's template-settings screen) still said "no automatic birthday sweep exists yet" — stale since the Scheduler's `birthday-wishes` job (which actually fires this template daily) was added; description corrected.
  - **Mobile** (`apps/mobile`): `firebase_core`/`firebase_messaging` packages added; `android/app/google-services.json` + the `com.google.gms.google-services` Gradle plugin wired (gitignored, per-environment like `key.properties`/`*.jks`); `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` permission added to the manifest. `main.dart` now async (`Firebase.initializeApp()` + a top-level background message handler registered before `runApp`). New `PushNotificationService` (`core/push/`, `get_it` singleton) requests notification permission + registers the token **lazily on first login/session-restore** (not at cold start), listens for token refresh, and handles foreground (in-app snackbar via a shared `scaffoldMessengerKey`, since this app has no other centralized toast system) and tap navigation (background `onMessageOpenedApp` + cold-start `getInitialMessage()`, both routed through the same `GoRouter` instance attached from `main.dart`). New `DeviceTokenRepository` (same `Dio`-wrapping shape as `profile_repository.dart`). `SessionCubit.staffSignedIn`/`memberSignedIn`/`restore()` register the token; `signOut()`/`_handleForcedLogout()` unregister it — `signOut()` unregisters BEFORE calling the auth repository's `logout()`, since the DELETE call still needs a valid Authorization header.
  - **Deliberate scope boundary at the time, per "mobile only ever renders real backend data"**: no member-facing in-app notification-history screen shipped in this prompt — the existing `notifications_screen.dart` is staff-only. A member's push arrived as a system tray notification only; tapping it opened the app's home shell, not a list, since no member-scoped notification-history endpoint existed to back one yet. **Closed by Prompt 90** (below) — don't design around this gap anymore.
  - **Verified live**: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build/vitest(296/296) all clean on the API. Live smoke test against the running dev stack — registered a real staff device token via `POST /profile/device-token`, confirmed idempotent re-registration and unregistration; created an ad-hoc notification and confirmed a `notifications-push` BullMQ job was enqueued and completed with the exact staff token + payload (inspected directly in Redis); published a real `audience: MEMBERS` announcement and confirmed TWO separate push jobs fired correctly — one staff-wide (from `notifyTenant`), one member-scoped (from the new announcement fan-out), each carrying the right token. `flutter analyze` clean (0 new issues) and both `flutter build apk --debug` (Gradle/Firebase wiring) and the API's own build succeeded. Actual FCM delivery to a real device was unverified at the time this prompt shipped (no service-account key yet) — **since resolved**: the key was supplied, deployed to both local dev and the real production server (`api.appkraft.info`), and confirmed live — see the credential-wiring note below Prompt 89 in this file's history for the exact verification (real Firebase API call, correct auth, clean per-token response).

90 — **Member notification history — backend + tenant-web + mobile** (`apps/api`, `apps/tenant-web`, `apps/mobile`). User follow-up to Prompt 89: "add a list also so user can see notification later in all logins" — confirmed scope as members specifically (staff already has this, on both tenant-web and mobile) covering every event type a member receives. Closes the exact gap Prompt 89 flagged as deliberately deferred.

  - **New `MemberNotification` table** (RLS'd, migrations `20260930124402_add_member_notifications` + `20260930124410_enable_rls_member_notifications`) — one row per member per event (unlike `TenantNotification`'s tenant-wide sharing), reusing the existing `TenantNotificationCategory` enum rather than adding a new one.
  - **`TenantNotificationService` gained the member-plane counterpart to `notifyTenant`** (same class, same file — mirrors the `DeviceTokenService` precedent of one service class covering both planes): `notifyMember(tenantId, memberId, category, title, body)` writes the history row then pushes, and `notifyMembersInTenant(...)` (the announcement bulk case) writes a row for **every** member in the tenant/branch — not just those with a registered device token — so it's still in their history once they do log in, then pushes to whichever ones have a token.
  - **`notification-trigger.service.ts`**: every existing `deviceTokenService.pushToMember(...)` call became `tenantNotificationService.notifyMember(...)` instead — same call sites, same params (this session's Prompt 89 work already threaded `memberId` everywhere needed), just routed through the richer method. History-writing is gated by the exact same `template.channels.includes('PUSH')` check the push itself already used — turning PUSH off for a template also turns off that event's history entry, same channel-gating precedent `IN_APP` already has for staff.
  - **New routes on `member-portal`** (self-service, no permission key): `GET /portal/notifications`, `GET /portal/notifications/unread-count`, `POST /portal/notifications/:id/read`, `POST /portal/notifications/read-all` — identical envelope shape to staff's `/notifications` equivalents. Thin delegating methods on `MemberPortalController`/`MemberPortalService`, same pattern as Prompt 89's `registerDeviceToken`/`unregisterDeviceToken`.
  - **tenant-web**: new `app/portal/(authenticated)/notifications/page.tsx`, a 9th entry in `MemberPortalShell`'s `TABS` (the member shell has no bell-icon/header affordance — a flat tab matches its own documented "flat, unweighted list" convention), new hooks in `use-member-portal.ts` (`useMemberNotifications`/`useMarkMemberNotificationRead`/`useMarkAllMemberNotificationsRead`) and service methods on `memberPortalService`, all through the member plane's own `memberApiClient` (never the staff `apiClient`).
  - **Mobile**: reused the existing `TenantNotification` Dart model as-is (the `/portal/notifications` response shape is identical to `/notifications` — no need for a duplicate model). New `MemberNotificationRepository`, new `MemberNotificationsScreen` (`features/member/presentation/`, **always `AppRole.member` themed** — lime→teal, never staff coral/violet, per this doc's own hard rule), a new entry in `member_menu_screen.dart` (the member shell has no free tab slot and no app-bar bell — same insertion point as Attendance/Invoices/Profile). **Fixes Prompt 89's own documented gap**: `PushNotificationService._navigateFor` now routes a member's push tap to `AppRoutes.memberNotifications` instead of falling through to `AppRoutes.home`.
  - **Verified live**: typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/build/vitest(296/296) clean on the API; typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/`next build` clean on tenant-web (`/portal/notifications` confirmed in the route manifest); `flutter analyze` (0 new issues) and `flutter build apk --debug` clean on mobile. Live smoke test: published a real `audience: MEMBERS` announcement, confirmed via direct DB query that **all 4 members** in the tenant got their own `MemberNotification` row (not just ones with a device token); logged in as a real member (`MEM-0004`) and confirmed `GET /portal/notifications` returned the row, `POST .../read` marked it read, and `unread-count` correctly dropped to 0 afterward.

91 — **Membership Plan "Membership Rules" removed — backend + tenant-web + mobile** (`apps/api`, `apps/tenant-web`, `apps/mobile`). User request: in membership plan create/update, drop the "Membership rules" section and rename "Joining fee" to "Registration fee"; follow-up asked to drop the backing DB columns too, and to change both web and mobile together.

  - **`MembershipPlan` lost 7 columns**: `validityStart`/`validityEnd`/`gracePeriodDays`/`renewalWindowDays`/`autoRenewalAllowed`/`minAge`/`maxAge` — migration `20261001120000_remove_membership_plan_rules`, hand-written (this environment's shell is non-interactive, which `prisma migrate dev` requires) and applied via `prisma migrate deploy` — the same command a production deploy runs, so this ships identically on the server.
  - **Enforcement logic deleted, not just the columns** — flagged to the user mid-task since this was real business-rule enforcement, not display: `MemberService#assertPlanAssignable`/`#assertRenewalWindow` (plan validity window, age eligibility, renewal window) and the `autoRenewalAllowed` gate on `assignMembership` are gone. The grace-period concept (`duration.util.ts#isWithinGracePeriod`, previously shared by `MemberService#toDetail`'s `canCheckIn`, `AttendanceService#eligibility`, and the `autoMembershipStatusUpdate` scheduler job) is gone too — all three now do a plain `endDate >= now` check; a membership is checkable through its exact end date and nothing past it, no grace window anymore.
  - **Frontend (both platforms)**: tenant-web's `plan-form-fields.tsx` and mobile's `MembershipPlanFormScreen` both lost the "Membership rules" section (7 fields + the date pickers it needed). "Joining fee" relabeled **"Registration fee"** in both plan forms and in the member-creation plan-details popup (web's `member-program-fields.tsx`) — label text only; the field is still `joiningFee` on the wire, distinct from `Member.registrationFee` (Prompt 82's separate one-time fee — don't conflate them).
  - **Verified live**: API typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/vitest(294/294) clean; tenant-web typecheck/eslint(0 warnings) clean; `flutter analyze` clean on every touched file (no build was run for any app, per explicit instruction this prompt — build verification deferred to later). Live smoke test against the running dev stack: created + updated a membership plan via curl, confirmed none of the 7 removed fields appear in the response; `POST /attendance/validate-qr` against a member with an active, not-yet-expired membership returned `valid: true` under the new no-grace-period check; `/memberships/new` returns 200 on tenant-web.

92 — **Membership plan pricing fixed — backend + tenant-web** (`apps/api`, `apps/tenant-web`). User-reported bug: created a plan with price 2000 + tax, the Membership Plans list still showed 2000. Investigation found the bug was much bigger than the list column — `taxPercentage`/`discountPercentage`/`joiningFee` were captured on a plan but **never applied anywhere in the whole app** (confirmed via full-codebase grep: every reference was pass-through, zero arithmetic) — not the list, not the Add Member wizard's totals, not invoice generation. User explicitly asked to fix both the display AND the actual billing.

  - **New shared formula** (`features/members/utils/plan-pricing.ts#computePlanPrice()`, discount-then-tax, user-confirmed): `finalPrice = (price − price×discount%) × (1 + tax%)`, `totalWithJoiningFee = finalPrice + joiningFee`. Used by the `/memberships` list's Price column (now shows the final price with a "Base ₹X" subtext when tax/discount apply), the Add Member wizard's plan-details popup (new Discount/Tax/Total rows), and the wizard's Proforma Invoice step.
  - **Proforma Invoice step** (`step-payment-summary.tsx`) now itemizes Plan price → Plan discount → Tax → Plan registration fee (kept a distinct label from the pre-existing member-level "Registration fee" row to avoid confusing the two) → Program amount → the existing rows → a new **Total due** row → Payment received → Pending amount. "Payment received" now auto-prefills to the computed total once a plan is picked.
  - **Actual billing fix** (`add-member-wizard.tsx#handleFinalSubmit`): `MemberPayment.tax` was never sent (always 0) and `.discount` only ever carried the manual override, never the plan's own discount%. When the typed "Payment received" covers the full total due (the common case), the wizard now sends `amount = price+joiningFee+registrationFee` (gross), `discount = planDiscount+manualDiscount`, `tax = planTax` — reconstructing the exact total-due figure via the server's existing `finalAmount = amount − discount + tax` formula. A partial payment (typed less than total due) falls back to the exact pre-existing literal behavior, deliberately not decomposed — can't cleanly attribute a partial shortfall to "pre-tax" vs "pre-discount" without guessing.
  - **Invoice itemization** (`MemberInvoiceService#generateForPayment`, called from `MemberPaymentService#onPaymentSucceeded`): previously hardcoded `taxAmount: 0, discountAmount: 0` with one flat line regardless of the Payment's real values. Now threads through the Payment's own `tax`/`discount` so `subtotal − discountAmount + taxAmount` always equals `Payment.finalAmount` exactly, by construction. The invoice detail page, its PDF, and both mobile invoice-detail screens already rendered these fields — no code changes needed there, they just started showing real numbers.
  - **Deliberately out of scope**: the core `amount − discount + tax = finalAmount` formula, partial-payment/installment tracking (still one `MemberPayment` per invoice, pre-existing), and every payment-creation entry point other than the Add Member wizard (renewals etc. unchanged).
  - **Verified live**: API typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/vitest(79/79 relevant suites) clean; tenant-web typecheck/eslint(0 warnings) clean. Live smoke test: created a plan (price 2000, discount 5%, tax 10%, joining fee 100 → expected 2190), assigned it to a test member, recorded a payment with the wizard's exact formula (`amount:2100, discount:100, tax:190`), confirmed `finalAmount:"2190"` on the payment and `subtotal:"2100", taxAmount:"190", discountAmount:"100", totalAmount:"2190"` on the auto-generated invoice — reconciles exactly. Test member/plan cleaned up afterward.

93 — **Renew re-gated to expired-only — backend + tenant-web + mobile** (`apps/api`, `apps/tenant-web`, `apps/mobile`). User-reported: clicking Renew on a member detail page still worked even though their membership had days left — asked for this to be blocked. This is a partial, deliberate reversal of Prompt 91's "remove all Membership Rules enforcement," since this specific restriction wasn't actually about `renewalWindowDays` (that field is gone for good) — it's a new, simpler, hardcoded rule.

  - **Flagged the structural wrinkle before implementing**: the scheduler's `autoMembershipStatusUpdate` job flips a membership from `ACTIVE` to `EXPIRED` once its `endDate` passes, and `renewMembership` only ever looks up an `ACTIVE` membership — so "only allow Renew once actually expired" makes Renew a near no-op in practice (by the time it's really expired, there's no longer an `ACTIVE` row to find; the member's `currentMembership` reads `null` and the UI falls back to the Assign flow instead). User chose this anyway ("block renew entirely while still ACTIVE") over the alternative (a configurable N-day renewal window).
  - **`MemberService#renewMembership`**: throws a 422 (`This membership is still active until {endDate} — it can only be renewed once it has expired.`) when `current.endDate > now`. `startDate` for the new period simplified to always `now` (the pre-existing `current.endDate > now ? current.endDate : now` ternary's true branch became unreachable under the new guard).
  - **tenant-web**: member detail page's Renew button disabled (with an explanatory message, Extend left untouched) via a new `membershipHasExpired` derived flag.
  - **Mobile**: `MemberRenewScreen` computes the same check, swaps its body copy, and disables `_RenewButton` via a new required `enabled` param.
  - **Verified live**: API typecheck/vitest(44/44 members suite) clean; tenant-web typecheck/eslint(0 warnings) clean; `flutter analyze` clean on the touched file. 95 — **Member-list phone search — backend only, both frontends benefit** (`apps/api`). User asked for member-list search to also match mobile number, on web and mobile. Phone is AES-GCM ciphertext at rest (Prompt 43) with the Prompt 43 doc comment explicitly saying "don't try to restore partial matching without discussing the alternative" — presented the exact trade-off (exact-match via existing hash index, free, vs. partial/substring match via decrypt-and-filter, doesn't scale) before touching anything. User chose partial match.

  - **`MemberRepository#list`**: new `findPhoneMatchingIds()` runs only when `search` contains a digit — fetches `{id, phone}` for every member matching every OTHER active filter (not `search` itself), decrypts each, and keeps ids whose digits-only phone contains the digits-only search term (formatting-agnostic both directions — `"987 654 3210"` matches `"6543"`). Those ids feed into the existing `firstName`/`lastName`/`memberId` OR clause; pagination/sorting/count stay fully DB-driven on the resulting query. Deliberately uncapped — a silently-incomplete partial match would be worse than the accepted per-search decrypt cost. A pure-name search never triggers this path.
  - **Frontend**: zero logic changes on either platform — both already sent the same `search` param to the same endpoint. tenant-web's members-list placeholder updated to "Search name, member ID, or phone…"; mobile's hint text was already generic and needed no change.
  - **Verified live**: API typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/vitest(44/44 members suite) clean; tenant-web typecheck clean. Live smoke test: searching `6543` (a middle substring of a test member's phone `9876543210`) returned exactly that member; a non-matching digit sequence returned zero; name search unaffected.

100 — **Push notifications actually display, with sound (`apps/api`, `apps/mobile`)**. User's real connected Android device (not emulator) received every FCM push at the native layer (`FLTFireMsgReceiver` fired 100% of the time, confirmed via live `adb logcat`) but nothing ever appeared — no system-tray entry in any app state, no sound. Root cause: (a) Android never auto-displays a notification while the app is foregrounded, for any app — expected, not a bug; (b) the app shipped with zero notification-channel configuration and no `flutter_local_notifications` package, so there was no mechanism capable of producing a real system notification in **any** state, foreground or background.
  - **Mobile**: added `flutter_local_notifications: ^19.4.2`. New `core/push/local_notifications.dart` — one shared `AndroidNotificationChannel` (`fitcloud_default`, `Importance.high`, `playSound: true`), `initLocalNotifications()`, `showPushNotification(RemoteMessage)` (reads `title`/`body` from `message.data`). Rewrote `push_notification_service.dart`: the background isolate handler and the foreground `FirebaseMessaging.onMessage` listener both now call `showPushNotification` directly — removed the old `scaffoldMessengerKey`/SnackBar mechanism entirely (no screen-local `BuildContext` ever reliably existed for it anyway). Required **core library desugaring** in `android/app/build.gradle.kts` (the plugin fails `checkDebugAarMetadata` without `compileOptions.isCoreLibraryDesugaringEnabled = true` + `coreLibraryDesugaring("com.android.tools:desugar_jdk_libs:2.1.4")`).
  - **Backend**: `fcm.client.ts#sendToTokens` now sends **data-only** FCM payloads (no `notification` block) — with a `notification` block present, Android auto-displays only while backgrounded and shows nothing while foreground, which was half of the original bug. Data-only forces every push through the app's own explicit display code in both states. Confirmed no `title`/`body` key collision with the `data: { category }` every real caller (`tenant-notification.service.ts`) actually sends.
  - **OEM gotcha found during live verification (Vivo/FuntouchOS/OriginOS)**: even with everything above correct — permission granted, high-importance channel, `adb shell dumpsys notification` confirming `numPostedByApp` incremented with `numBlocked=0` — the notification still didn't reach the shade, because Vivo's own notification-management layer silently gates new apps' banners/sound separately from standard Android permissions. Fixed by the user enabling **Settings → Notifications & status bar → App notifications management → FitCloud** (banner/pop-up style) and **Settings → Battery → Background power consumption management → FitCloud** (allow). Not a code issue — document this if it recurs on a future test device.
  - **Verified live**: `flutter analyze` clean on all changed files; API typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/vitest (309/309) clean. 10 real notification-template contents (every one of the 10 `DEFAULT_TEMPLATES` in `default-templates.ts`, rendered with realistic sample data) sent via the real `POST /notifications`/trigger pipeline against the user's own `kaushal-fitness` tenant and **received on the physical device in the system tray with sound**, both foregrounded and backgrounded — user confirmed.

Tenant-web's header already had a bell+unread-badge (`NotificationPanel`, from the Prompt 27 redesign); mobile had the backend plumbing (`TenantNotificationRepository`/`MemberNotificationRepository#unreadCount()`) but no UI ever called it. New `shared/widgets/notification_bell_icon.dart` — a self-contained `StatefulWidget` (no cubit; a single polled int didn't earn one), `isStaff: bool`, polls the right repo's `unreadCount()` on a 60s timer (matches web's `refetchInterval`), shows a capped "99+" red badge, pushes to `AppRoutes.notifications`/`memberNotifications` on tap and re-polls on return. Placed next to the avatar on each role's one primary header where that role's avatar already appears (not duplicated per-tab, since 4 of 5 role shells have no shared `Scaffold.appBar`): Owner/Manager dashboard screens, Trainer/Receptionist menu screens (their only avatar location), Member dashboard screen. `flutter analyze` clean.

102 — **Real production outage + two deploy-pipeline fixes, found and fixed live on the actual EC2 box (`.github/workflows/deploy-production.yml`, `infrastructure/testing-deploy/06-redeploy-code.sh`, `07-redeploy-via-git.sh`)**. Right after Prompt 100's push-notification work was pushed to `main`, the Prompt 99-era CI speed-up (building api/tenant-web/super-admin in **parallel** via `pnpm turbo run build`, instead of sequentially) caused a real outage:
  - **Chain of failure**: the production box only has **~900MB RAM**. 3 concurrent TS/Next builds exhausted it badly enough that the SSH deploy step hung for the full 30m `command_timeout` and never reached its `pm2 restart` line — but the memory pressure was severe enough to also take down the already-running pm2 processes, a full site outage (`api.appkraft.info` unreachable, EC2 **Instance status check: failed** — System status/hardware was fine, the OS itself was wedged). A plain **Reboot** didn't clear it on the first try; a second Reboot did. But PM2's daemon/process list is **not** persisted across a reboot unless `pm2 startup` (systemd integration) was configured — it never had been — so after the reboot, `pm2 status` came back completely empty: nothing auto-restarted.
  - **Recovery, live on the box**: `pm2 resurrect` restored all 3 apps from the last `pm2 save` dump (`/home/ubuntu/.pm2/dump.pm2`) — confirmed `curl https://api.appkraft.info/health` → `200`. Then `pm2 startup systemd -u ubuntu --hp /home/ubuntu` (ran the exact `sudo env PATH=...` line it prints) + `pm2 save` so a future reboot auto-restarts everything via the new `pm2-ubuntu.service` systemd unit — this gap is now closed.
  - **CI fix #1**: reverted the parallel `turbo run build` back to 3 sequential `pnpm --filter X run build` calls — slower, but this box can't safely build 3 apps at once. Pushed as `27c5ff9`.
  - **CI fix #2 — a SECOND, previously-unknown bug found while verifying fix #1**: even sequential, the very next deploy run revealed `fitcloud-tenant-web`/`fitcloud-super-admin` crash-looping **600-700+ times** in ~20 minutes (`pm2 status`'s `↺` column), driving the box to 99% CPU and heavy swap use, and producing a 502 — nearly a second outage. Root cause: the deploy rebuilds **in the same directory** the live `pm2`-managed `next start` processes are actively serving from — `pnpm install` rewriting the shared `node_modules` (and later each app's own `next build` rewriting `.next`) out from under a process that does live per-request disk reads crashes it; pm2 auto-restarts it straight back into the same still-changing files, repeat. `fitcloud-api` (a single pre-loaded compiled `dist/server.js`, no live per-request disk reads) was never affected by either incident — a useful tell for diagnosing which process type is at risk. **Fix**: `pm2 stop fitcloud-tenant-web fitcloud-super-admin` up front (before `pnpm install`, not just before each app's own build, since install alone rewrites node_modules for every workspace package), build api → `pm2 restart` it immediately, then build+`pm2 start` tenant-web and super-admin individually as each one's own build finishes (not waiting on all three) — api stays up the entire deploy, tenant-web/super-admin get a short predictable downtime window instead of an open-ended crash loop. Applied identically to all 3 places that share this script. Pushed as `f714d15`.
  - **Verified live**: both fixes confirmed via the actual triggered CI → Deploy Production run on the real box (not just read — watched `pm2 status`/`free -m`/the Actions run through to a genuine `success` conclusion and GitHub's own smoke-test step passing against `/health`).
  - **Takeaway for future deploy-script changes**: this box is resource-constrained enough (~900MB RAM) that both "more parallelism" and "rebuild in place under a live process" are real production-outage risks here, not just slower/uglier — budget for sequential-and-stop-before-rebuild as the baseline, not an afterthought.

103 — **Member self-service "Pay now" on outstanding invoices + early membership activation (`apps/api`, `apps/tenant-web`, `apps/mobile`)**. User: member should be able to pay an outstanding invoice from the dashboard's "Outstanding payments" tile, not just view it — and paying should start the membership immediately rather than waiting for its scheduled (future) `startDate`.
  - **Backend**: new member-auth-scoped `POST /portal/invoices/:id/pay/checkout` + `.../checkout/:paymentId/verify`, mirroring the existing membership-renewal flow's Razorpay Orders + Checkout-modal + local-signature-verify pattern exactly (`member-portal.service.ts`) — amount is always the invoice's own `totalAmount`, never client-supplied. Settlement reuses `MemberPaymentService#onPaymentSucceeded`'s existing `markStatus(invoiceId, 'PAID')` path, not a new one. New `activatePendingMembershipIfAny(memberId)`: any successful payment (staff CASH/UPI, a Payment Link, or this new member self-checkout — one shared hook, not three separate ones) now also flips a still-`PENDING` membership (`MembershipStatus.PENDING` = assigned with a future `startDate`, normally only flipped to `ACTIVE` by the scheduler's daily `startDate <= now` sweep) to `ACTIVE` immediately, shifting `startDate` to today and recomputing `endDate` from the plan's own duration (`addDuration`) — member gets their full paid period from when they actually paid, not a shortened one.
  - **tenant-web**: new shared `PayInvoiceButton` (member-portal feature) used in both the dashboard's `OutstandingDetail` modal and the standalone `/portal/invoices` list — same Checkout-modal component pattern as `/portal/renew`.
  - **mobile**: new `MemberPayInvoiceSheet`, mirroring `MemberRenewSheet`'s Razorpay round trip exactly; wired into both `member_dashboard_detail_sheet.dart`'s outstanding-payments list and `member_invoices_screen.dart`'s invoice cards, each refreshing their parent screen's data on a successful pay.
  - **Verified live**: API typecheck/eslint(0 warnings)/vitest (314/314) clean; tenant-web typecheck/eslint(0 warnings) clean; `flutter analyze` clean on every touched file (fixed one unrelated pre-existing compile error found along the way — a missing `WeekDayX` import in `member_dashboard_detail_sheet.dart`, not caused by this change). Razorpay isn't configured in this local `.env` (no test-mode keys), so the actual Checkout-modal click-through couldn't be driven end-to-end here — same accepted limitation as every other self-service Razorpay flow in this codebase (see Prompt 25's note). Instead verified the critical shared logic for real: recorded a live staff CASH payment against a member's `PENDING`-membership invoice via the real API and confirmed the invoice flipped to `PAID` and the membership flipped `PENDING → ACTIVE` with `startDate`/`endDate` correctly recomputed to today/+1 plan period — this is the exact same code path the new Razorpay verify endpoint calls into, so it's confirmed working end-to-end short of the Razorpay UI hop itself.

Membership, Workout, Diet, Measurements), right = new panels `MemberAttendancePanel` (month filter, calendar heatmap, today badge, check-in/out buttons moved here), `MemberPaymentPanel` (receipt timeline + pending balance), `MemberGuestPanel` (pass ring + visits + log form, moved from the Membership card's data), `MemberUsagePanel`. 105 — **Balance-due invoices + instant payment updates (`apps/api`, `apps/tenant-web`)**. Closes the two gaps left by Prompt 104. (1) **Dashboard "Outstanding" tile / invoice list / finance totals / member-portal Pay now ignored a part-paid plan's unpaid remainder**, because a payment only ever invoices what was received (a PAID receipt). New `MemberInvoice.membershipId` (migration `20261003100000_member_invoice_membership_link`, column only — RLS already on) and new `finance/services/member-balance.service.ts#MemberBalanceService`: keeps ONE auto-managed UNPAID "Balance due — <plan>" invoice per ACTIVE/PENDING/EXPIRED membership equal to `plan final price − SUCCESS payments`, where final price = `priceAtAssignment` with the plan's discount% then tax% applied (same formula as web/mobile `computePlanPrice`; joining/registration fees are not tracked in the balance). `reconcile`/`reconcileForMember` run after assign, renew, upgrade/downgrade, cancel (`MemberService`) and at the end of `MemberPaymentService#onPaymentSucceeded`; CANCELLED/SUPERSEDED memberships void their open balance invoice, EXPIRED ones keep it (the member still owes it). A payment that covers the whole open balance invoice becomes that invoice's itemized receipt (`settleBalanceWithReceipt`) instead of leaving a second PAID invoice; a smaller payment still gets its own PAID receipt and the balance invoice is resized. The payment-receipt email's "Due Amount" now reads the open balance invoice. The earlier list-only shortfall hack in `MemberService#buildOutstandingAmounts` was removed — invoices are the single source. **Backfill for existing data: `pnpm exec tsx scripts/reconcile-balance-invoices.ts` (run once after deploying + migrating; idempotent).** (2) **Instant updates**: `MemberPaymentService` emits Socket.IO `payment:updated` (`{memberId, memberName, status, amount}`) to the tenant room on payment success/failure (covers webhook, verify-status polling, staff entries); `realtime-provider.tsx` invalidates `payments/invoices/finance/members/reports/income` and shows a "Payment received" toast. The member page's 8s verify polling stays as the local-dev fallback (no webhook there). Verified live: assign → balance invoice for the full price; ₹100 part payment → receipt + balance resized; paying the remainder → single PAID itemized invoice; dashboard KPI and `/invoices?status=UNPAID` include the balance; a socket client received `payment:updated`; API typecheck/eslint clean, vitest 314/314; tenant-web typecheck/eslint clean. **Mobile**: balance invoices flow through the existing invoice/outstanding endpoints so mobile shows them already; mobile has no Socket.IO client, so no instant push there yet.
